```
1→"""Auto-reply engine — shared logic for CLI and gateway.
     2→
     3→Handles config parsing, state management, prompt building, and LLM calls.
     4→Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8→
     9→_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 20
    10→
    11→
    12→def parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    13→    """Parse /autoreply command arguments.
    14→
    15→    Returns (status_message, config_or_none).
    16→    - When enabling: returns (message, new_config_dict)
    17→    - When disabling/status/error: returns (message, None)
    18→
    19→    The caller is responsible for storing/clearing the config and
    20→    formatting the message for their platform (markdown vs plain text).
    21→    """
    22→    # /autoreply off
    23→    if args.lower() in ("off", "disable", "stop"):
    24→        return "off", None
    25→
    26→    # /autoreply max <N>
    27→    if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    28→        parts = args.split(None, 1)
    29→        if len(parts) == 2:
    30→            try:
    31→                n = int(parts[1])
    32→                if n < 1:
    33→                    return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
    34→                return f"max:{n}", None
    35→            except ValueError:
    36→                return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    37→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    38→
    39→    # /autoreply (no args)
    40→    if not args:
    41→        return "status", None
    42→
    43→    # Extract flags from args: --literal, --max N, --forever
    44→    literal = False
    45→    max_turns = _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS
    46→    remaining_parts = []
    47→
    48→    parts = args.split()
    49→    i = 0
    50→    while i < len(parts):
    51→        if parts[i] == "--literal":
    52→            literal = True
    53→        elif parts[i] == "--forever":
    54→            max_turns = 0  # 0 = unlimited
    55→        elif parts[i] == "--max" and i + 1 < len(parts):
    56→            i += 1
    57→            try:
    58→                n = int(parts[i])
    59→                if n < 1:
    60→                    return "error:--max must be at least 1.", None
    61→                max_turns = n
    62→            except ValueError:
    63→                return "error:--max requires a number.", None
    64→        else:
    65→            remaining_parts.append(parts[i])
    66→        i += 1
    67→
    68→    prompt = " ".join(remaining_parts).strip()
    69→
    70→    if literal:
    71→        if not prompt:
    72→            return "error:Usage: /autoreply --literal <message>", None
    73→        return "enabled", {
    74→            "prompt": prompt,
    75→            "model": None,
    76→            "max_turns": max_turns,
    77→            "turn_count": 0,
    78→            "literal": True,
    79→        }
    80→
    81→    if not prompt:
    82→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply <instructions>", None
    83→
    84→    return "enabled", {
    85→        "prompt": prompt,
    86→        "model": None,
    87→        "max_turns": max_turns,
    88→        "turn_count": 0,
    89→    }
    90→
    91→
    92→def build_autoreply_messages(config: Dict[str, Any],
    93→                             history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    94→    """Build the message list for the auto-reply LLM call.
    95→
    96→    Filters history to recent user/assistant messages, prepends a system
    97→    prompt with the user's instructions, and appends the generation request.
    98→    """
    99→    recent = [
   100→        m for m in (history or [])
   101→        if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   102→    ][-20:]
   103→
   104→    system_prompt = (
   105→        "You are generating a reply on behalf of the user in a conversation "
   106→        "with an AI assistant.\n\n"
   107→        "THE USER'S INSTRUCTIONS — follow these as your top priority:\n"
   108→        f"{config['prompt']}\n\n"
   109→        "Output ONLY the user's reply message. No labels, no meta-commentary, "
   110→        "no 'User:' prefix."
   111→    )
   112→
   113→    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
   114→    for m in recent:
   115→        content = m["content"]
   116→        # Flatten list-type content to plain text
   117→        if isinstance(content, list):
   118→            content = "\n".join(
   119→                p.get("text", "") for p in content
   120→                if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("type") == "text"
   121→            )
   122→        messages.append({"role": m["role"], "content": content})
   123→    messages.append({
   124→        "role": "user",
   125→        "content": "Based on the conversation above and your instructions, "
   126→                   "generate the next user reply.",
   127→    })
   128→    return messages
   129→
   130→
   131→def check_and_advance(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Tuple[Optional[str], bool]:
   132→    """Check turn cap and handle literal mode.
   133→
   134→    Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).
   135→    - (None, False): config is None / not applicable (caller didn't pass config)
   136→    - (None, True): turn cap reached, config should be removed
   137→    - (text, False): literal mode reply, turn_count incremented
   138→    - None for LLM mode: caller should proceed to call the LLM
   139→
   140→    For LLM mode, returns a sentinel to indicate the caller should proceed:
   141→    the return is (None, False) when turn_count < max_turns and not literal.
   142→    But we can't distinguish "no config" from "LLM needed" with just (None, False).
   143→
   144→    So the contract is: caller checks config existence first, then calls this.
   145→    If this returns (None, True) → cap reached. If it returns a string → use it.
   146→    If it returns (None, False) → proceed to LLM call.
   147→    """
   148→    if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
   149→        return None, True
   150→
   151→    if config.get("literal"):
   152→        config["turn_count"] += 1
   153→        return config["prompt"], False
   154→
   155→    return None, False
   156→
   157→
   158→def format_status(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   159→    """Format the auto-reply status for display."""
   160→    mode = "literal" if config.get("literal") else "LLM-generated"
   161→    label = "Message" if config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
   162→    prompt_preview = config["prompt"][:100]
   163→    if len(config["prompt"]) > 100:
   164→        prompt_preview += "..."
   165→    return (
   166→        f"Auto-reply active ({mode})\n"
   167→        f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}\n"
   168→        f"  Turns: {config['turn_count']}/{'∞' if config['max_turns'] == 0 else config['max_turns']}"
   169→    )
   170→
```

```
   501→
   502→
   503→class TestAutoreplyInHandleMessage:
   504→    """Tests for auto-reply wiring in _handle_message.
   505→
   506→    These test the auto-reply logic directly rather than going through the
   507→    full _handle_message pipeline, which requires too much setup. The
   508→    individual methods (_generate_autoreply, _process_autoreply) are
   509→    thoroughly tested above; here we verify the wiring code itself.
   510→    """
   511→
   512→    def test_autoreply_message_detected_by_message_id(self):
   513→        """Messages with autoreply- prefix in message_id are detected."""
   514→        event = _make_event(text="test", message_id="autoreply-123.456")
   515→        assert (event.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   516→
   517→        event2 = _make_event(text="test", message_id="regular-123")
   518→        assert not (event2.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   519→
   520→    def test_turn_counter_reset_logic(self):
   521→        """Real messages reset counter; autoreply messages don't."""
   522→        runner = _make_runner()
   523→        session_key = "agent:main:telegram:dm:12345"
   524→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key] = {
   525→            "prompt": "test", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 7,
   526→        }
   527→
   528→        # Real message — should reset
   529→        is_autoreply = ("regular-msg" or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   530→        if session_key in runner._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
   531→            runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
   532→        assert runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] == 0
   533→
   534→        # Restore and test autoreply message — should NOT reset
   535→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 7
   536→        is_autoreply = ("autoreply-123.456" or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   537→        if session_key in runner._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
   538→            runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
   539→        assert runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] == 7
   540→
   541→    def test_response_labeling_logic(self):
   542→        """Auto-reply responses get labeled with turn count."""
```

```
1→"""Slash command definitions and autocomplete for the Hermes CLI.
     2→
     3→Contains the shared built-in ``COMMANDS`` dict and ``SlashCommandCompleter``.
     4→The completer can optionally include dynamic skill slash commands supplied by the
     5→interactive CLI.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from collections.abc import Callable, Mapping
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→from prompt_toolkit.completion import Completer, Completion
    14→
    15→
    16→# Commands organized by category for better help display
    17→COMMANDS_BY_CATEGORY = {
    18→    "Session": {
    19→        "/new": "Start a new conversation (reset history)",
    20→        "/reset": "Reset conversation only (keep screen)",
    21→        "/clear": "Clear screen and reset conversation (fresh start)",
    22→        "/history": "Show conversation history",
    23→        "/save": "Save the current conversation",
    24→        "/retry": "Retry the last message (resend to agent)",
    25→        "/undo": "Remove the last user/assistant exchange",
    26→        "/title": "Set a title for the current session (usage: /title My Session Name)",
    27→        "/compress": "Manually compress conversation context (flush memories + summarize)",
    28→        "/rollback": "List or restore filesystem checkpoints (usage: /rollback [number])",
    29→        "/background": "Run a prompt in the background (usage: /background <prompt>)",
    30→        "/autoreply": "Enable auto-reply loop (usage: /autoreply <instructions> or /autoreply --literal <message>)",
    31→    },
    32→    "Configuration": {
    33→        "/config": "Show current configuration",
    34→        "/model": "Show or change the current model",
    35→        "/provider": "Show available providers and current provider",
    36→        "/prompt": "View/set custom system prompt",
    37→        "/personality": "Set a predefined personality",
    38→        "/verbose": "Cycle tool progress display: off → new → all → verbose",
    39→        "/reasoning": "Manage reasoning effort and display (usage: /reasoning [level|show|hide])",
    40→        "/skin": "Show or change the display skin/theme",
    41→    },
    42→    "Tools & Skills": {
    43→        "/tools": "List available tools",
    44→        "/toolsets": "List available toolsets",
    45→        "/skills": "Search, install, inspect, or manage skills from online registries",
    46→        "/cron": "Manage scheduled tasks (list, add, remove)",
    47→        "/reload-mcp": "Reload MCP servers from config.yaml",
    48→    },
    49→    "Info": {
    50→        "/help": "Show this help message",
    51→        "/usage": "Show token usage for the current session",
    52→        "/insights": "Show usage insights and analytics (last 30 days)",
    53→        "/platforms": "Show gateway/messaging platform status",
    54→        "/paste": "Check clipboard for an image and attach it",
    55→    },
    56→    "Exit": {
    57→        "/quit": "Exit the CLI (also: /exit, /q)",
    58→    },
    59→}
    60→
    61→# Flat dict for backwards compatibility and autocomplete
    62→COMMANDS = {}
    63→for category_commands in COMMANDS_BY_CATEGORY.values():
    64→    COMMANDS.update(category_commands)
    65→
    66→
    67→class SlashCommandCompleter(Completer):
    68→    """Autocomplete for built-in slash commands and optional skill commands."""
    69→
    70→    def __init__(
    71→        self,
    72→        skill_commands_provider: Callable[[], Mapping[str, dict[str, Any]]] | None = None,
    73→    ) -> None:
    74→        self._skill_commands_provider = skill_commands_provider
    75→
    76→    def _iter_skill_commands(self) -> Mapping[str, dict[str, Any]]:
    77→        if self._skill_commands_provider is None:
    78→            return {}
    79→        try:
    80→            return self._skill_commands_provider() or {}
    81→        except Exception:
    82→            return {}
    83→
    84→    @staticmethod
    85→    def _completion_text(cmd_name: str, word: str) -> str:
    86→        """Return replacement text for a completion.
    87→
    88→        When the user has already typed the full command exactly (``/help``),
    89→        returning ``help`` would be a no-op and prompt_toolkit suppresses the
    90→        menu. Appending a trailing space keeps the dropdown visible and makes
    91→        backspacing retrigger it naturally.
    92→        """
    93→        return f"{cmd_name} " if cmd_name == word else cmd_name
    94→
    95→    def get_completions(self, document, complete_event):
    96→        text = document.text_before_cursor
    97→        if not text.startswith("/"):
    98→            return
    99→
   100→        word = text[1:]
   101→
   102→        for cmd, desc in COMMANDS.items():
   103→            cmd_name = cmd[1:]
   104→            if cmd_name.startswith(word):
   105→                yield Completion(
   106→                    self._completion_text(cmd_name, word),
   107→                    start_position=-len(word),
   108→                    display=cmd,
   109→                    display_meta=desc,
   110→                )
   111→
   112→        for cmd, info in self._iter_skill_commands().items():
   113→            cmd_name = cmd[1:]
   114→            if cmd_name.startswith(word):
   115→                description = str(info.get("description", "Skill command"))
   116→                short_desc = description[:50] + ("..." if len(description) > 50 else "")
   117→                yield Completion(
   118→                    self._completion_text(cmd_name, word),
   119→                    start_position=-len(word),
   120→                    display=cmd,
   121→                    display_meta=f"⚡ {short_desc}",
   122→                )
   123→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Local-only HTTP control API for the Hermes gateway.
     3→
     4→Runs alongside the gateway as a background aiohttp server on 127.0.0.1.
     5→Provides programmatic control over live agent sessions — session listing,
     6→message injection, etc.
     7→
     8→External tools (e.g., desloppify) can call these endpoints to influence
     9→a running Hermes agent without needing direct process access.
    10→
    11→All mutations go through POST /sessions/{key}/message — inject any text
    12→(including /commands) as if the user typed it.
    13→
    14→Default port: 47823 (override with HERMES_CONTROL_PORT env var).
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import logging
    19→import os
    20→
    21→from aiohttp import web
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    26→
    27→# Commands available via /message injection.  This list is informational —
    28→# the message endpoint accepts any text, but GET /commands exposes these
    29→# so external tools know what's available.
    30→AVAILABLE_COMMANDS = [
    31→    {"command": "/reset", "description": "Reset conversation history"},
    32→    {"command": "/new", "description": "Start a new conversation"},
    33→    {"command": "/stop", "description": "Stop the running agent"},
    34→    {"command": "/compact", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    35→    {"command": "/compress", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    36→    {"command": "/status", "description": "Show session info"},
    37→    {"command": "/model <provider:model>", "description": "Switch model"},
    38→    {"command": "/personality <name>", "description": "Switch personality"},
    39→    {"command": "/autoreply <instructions>", "description": "Enable auto-reply loop"},
    40→    {"command": "/autoreply off", "description": "Disable auto-reply loop"},
    41→    {"command": "/reasoning <level>", "description": "Set reasoning effort"},
    42→    {"command": "/rollback [number]", "description": "List or restore checkpoints"},
    43→    {"command": "/background <prompt>", "description": "Run prompt in background session"},
    44→    {"command": "/undo", "description": "Undo last assistant response"},
    45→    {"command": "/retry", "description": "Retry last message"},
    46→    {"command": "/usage", "description": "Show token usage"},
    47→    {"command": "/help", "description": "List commands"},
    48→]
    49→
    50→
    51→def _get_port() -> int:
    52→    return int(os.getenv("HERMES_CONTROL_PORT", str(DEFAULT_PORT)))
    53→
    54→
    55→def _json_response(data: dict, status: int = 200) -> web.Response:
    56→    return web.Response(
    57→        text=json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False),
    58→        content_type="application/json",
    59→        status=status,
    60→    )
    61→
    62→
    63→class ControlAPI:
    64→    """Lightweight control API that holds a reference to the GatewayRunner."""
    65→
    66→    def __init__(self, gateway_runner):
    67→        self.runner = gateway_runner
    68→        self.app = web.Application()
    69→        self._setup_routes()
    70→        self._site = None
    71→
    72→    def _setup_routes(self):
    73→        self.app.router.add_get("/health", self.health)
    74→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions", self.list_sessions)
    75→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions/{key}", self.get_session)
    76→        self.app.router.add_get("/commands", self.list_commands)
    77→        self.app.router.add_post("/sessions/{key}/message", self.send_message)
    78→
    79→    # ── Helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    80→
    81→    def _resolve_agent(self, key: str):
    82→        """Look up a running agent by session key.  '_any' returns the first.
    83→        Returns (resolved_key, agent) or (key, None) if not found."""
    84→        if key == "_any":
    85→            if not self.runner._running_agents:
    86→                return key, None
    87→            key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
    88→        return key, self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
    89→
    90→    # ── Endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    async def health(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    93→        return _json_response({"status": "ok"})
    94→
    95→    async def list_sessions(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    96→        """List all sessions, marking which have a live (running) agent."""
    97→        sessions = []
    98→        for key, agent in self.runner._running_agents.items():
    99→            entry = {
   100→                "session_key": key,
   101→                "status": "running",
   102→                "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   103→                "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   104→                **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   105→            }
   106→            sessions.append(entry)
   107→        return _json_response({"sessions": sessions})
   108→
   109→    async def get_session(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   110→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   111→        key, agent = self._resolve_agent(key)
   112→        if not agent:
   113→            return _json_response(
   114→                {"error": f"No running agent for '{key}'"},
   115→                status=404,
   116→            )
   117→        return _json_response({
   118→            "session_key": key,
   119→            "status": "running",
   120→            "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   121→            "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   122→            **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   123→        })
   124→
   125→    async def list_commands(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   126→        """List available slash commands that can be sent via /message."""
   127→        return _json_response({"commands": AVAILABLE_COMMANDS})
   128→
   129→    async def send_message(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   130→        """Inject a message (or /command) into a session.
   131→
   132→        POST body: {"text": "...", "mode": "interrupt"|"queue"}
   133→
   134→        Modes:
   135→        - interrupt (default): cancel current agent run, process this message
   136→        - queue: let current run finish, then process this message next
   137→        """
   138→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   139→
   140→        try:
   141→            body = await request.json()
   142→        except Exception:
   143→            return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
   144→
   145→        text = body.get("text", "").strip()
   146→        if not text:
   147→            return _json_response({"error": "'text' is required"}, status=400)
   148→
   149→        mode = body.get("mode", "interrupt")
   150→        if mode not in ("interrupt", "queue"):
   151→            return _json_response(
   152→                {"error": f"Invalid mode '{mode}', must be 'interrupt' or 'queue'"},
   153→                status=400,
   154→            )
   155→
   156→        logger.info(
   157→            "Control API: send_message session=%s mode=%s text=%s",
   158→            key, mode, text[:80],
   159→        )
   160→
   161→        try:
   162→            result = self.runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=(mode != "queue"))
   163→            # await if coroutine (GatewayRunner is async, CLI shim is sync)
   164→            if hasattr(result, "__await__"):
   165→                await result
   166→        except LookupError as e:
   167→            return _json_response({"error": str(e)}, status=404)
   168→
   169→        return _json_response({"success": True, "message": "Message submitted"})
   170→
   171→    # ── Lifecycle ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   172→
   173→    async def start(self):
   174→        port = _get_port()
   175→        app_runner = web.AppRunner(self.app, access_log=None)
   176→        await app_runner.setup()
   177→        self._site = web.TCPSite(app_runner, "127.0.0.1", port)
   178→        try:
   179→            await self._site.start()
   180→            logger.info("Control API listening on http://127.0.0.1:%d", port)
   181→            self._write_port_file(port)
   182→        except OSError as e:
   183→            logger.warning("Control API failed to bind port %d: %s", port, e)
   184→
   185→    async def stop(self):
   186→        if self._site:
   187→            await self._site.stop()
   188→            self._remove_port_file()
   189→            logger.info("Control API stopped")
   190→
   191→    @staticmethod
   192→    def _write_port_file(port: int):
   193→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   194→        try:
   195→            with open(path, "w") as f:
   196→                f.write(str(port))
   197→        except OSError:
   198→            pass
   199→
   200→    @staticmethod
   201→    def _remove_port_file():
   202→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   203→        try:
   204→            os.remove(path)
   205→        except OSError:
   206→            pass
   207→
```

```
   329→            return self._honcho_managers[session_key], self._honcho_configs.get(session_key)
   330→
   331→        try:
   332→            from honcho_integration.client import HonchoClientConfig, get_honcho_client
   333→            from honcho_integration.session import HonchoSessionManager
   334→
   335→            hcfg = HonchoClientConfig.from_global_config()
   336→            if not hcfg.enabled or not hcfg.api_key:
   337→                return None, hcfg
   338→
   339→            client = get_honcho_client(hcfg)
   340→            manager = HonchoSessionManager(
   341→                honcho=client,
   342→                config=hcfg,
   343→                context_tokens=hcfg.context_tokens,
   344→            )
   345→            self._honcho_managers[session_key] = manager
   346→            self._honcho_configs[session_key] = hcfg
   347→            return manager, hcfg
   348→        except Exception as e:
   349→            logger.debug("Gateway Honcho init failed for %s: %s", session_key, e)
   350→            return None, None
   351→
   352→    def _shutdown_gateway_honcho(self, session_key: str) -> None:
   353→        """Flush and close the persistent Honcho manager for a gateway session."""
   354→        managers = getattr(self, "_honcho_managers", None)
   355→        configs = getattr(self, "_honcho_configs", None)
   356→        if managers is None or configs is None:
   357→            return
   358→
   359→        manager = managers.pop(session_key, None)
   360→        configs.pop(session_key, None)
   361→        if not manager:
   362→            return
   363→        try:
   364→            manager.shutdown()
   365→        except Exception as e:
   366→            logger.debug("Gateway Honcho shutdown failed for %s: %s", session_key, e)
   367→
   368→    def _shutdown_all_gateway_honcho(self) -> None:
   369→        """Flush and close all persistent Honcho managers."""
   370→        managers = getattr(self, "_honcho_managers", None)
   371→        if not managers:
   372→            return
   373→        for session_key in list(managers.keys()):
   374→            self._shutdown_gateway_honcho(session_key)
   375→    
   376→    def _flush_memories_for_session(self, old_session_id: str):
   377→        """Prompt the agent to save memories/skills before context is lost.
   378→
   379→        Synchronous worker — meant to be called via run_in_executor from
   380→        an async context so it doesn't block the event loop.
   381→        """
   382→        try:
   383→            history = self.session_store.load_transcript(old_session_id)
   384→            if not history or len(history) < 4:
   385→                return
   386→
   387→            from run_agent import AIAgent
   388→            runtime_kwargs = _resolve_runtime_agent_kwargs()
   389→            if not runtime_kwargs.get("api_key"):
   390→                return
   391→
   392→            # Resolve model from config — AIAgent's default is OpenRouter-
   393→            # formatted ("anthropic/claude-opus-4.6") which fails when the
   394→            # active provider is openai-codex.
   395→            model = _resolve_gateway_model()
   396→
   397→            tmp_agent = AIAgent(
   398→                **runtime_kwargs,
   399→                model=model,
   400→                max_iterations=8,
   401→                quiet_mode=True,
   402→                enabled_toolsets=["memory", "skills"],
   403→                session_id=old_session_id,
   404→            )
   405→
   406→            # Build conversation history from transcript
   407→            msgs = [
   408→                {"role": m.get("role"), "content": m.get("content")}
   409→                for m in history
   410→                if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   411→            ]
   412→
   413→            # Give the agent a real turn to think about what to save
   414→            flush_prompt = (
   415→                "[System: This session is about to be automatically reset due to "
   416→                "inactivity or a scheduled daily reset. The conversation context "
   417→                "will be cleared after this turn.\n\n"
   418→                "Review the conversation above and:\n"
   419→                "1. Save any important facts, preferences, or decisions to memory "
   420→                "(user profile or your notes) that would be useful in future sessions.\n"
   421→                "2. If you discovered a reusable workflow or solved a non-trivial "
   422→                "problem, consider saving it as a skill.\n"
   423→                "3. If nothing is worth saving, that's fine — just skip.\n\n"
   424→                "Do NOT respond to the user. Just use the memory and skill_manage "
   425→                "tools if needed, then stop.]"
   426→            )
   427→
   428→            tmp_agent.run_conversation(
   429→                user_message=flush_prompt,
   430→                conversation_history=msgs,
   431→            )
   432→            logger.info("Pre-reset memory flush completed for session %s", old_session_id)
   433→            # Flush any queued Honcho writes before the session is dropped
   434→            if getattr(tmp_agent, '_honcho', None):
   435→                try:
   436→                    tmp_agent._honcho.shutdown()
   437→                except Exception:
   438→                    pass
   439→        except Exception as e:
   440→            logger.debug("Pre-reset memory flush failed for session %s: %s", old_session_id, e)
   441→
   442→    async def _async_flush_memories(self, old_session_id: str):
   443→        """Run the sync memory flush in a thread pool so it won't block the event loop."""
   444→        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   445→        await loop.run_in_executor(None, self._flush_memories_for_session, old_session_id)
   446→    
   447→    @staticmethod
   448→    def _load_prefill_messages() -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
   449→        """Load ephemeral prefill messages from config or env var.
   450→        
   451→        Checks HERMES_PREFILL_MESSAGES_FILE env var first, then falls back to
   452→        the prefill_messages_file key in ~/.hermes/config.yaml.
   453→        Relative paths are resolved from ~/.hermes/.
   454→        """
   455→        import json as _json
   456→        file_path = os.getenv("HERMES_PREFILL_MESSAGES_FILE", "")
   457→        if not file_path:
   458→            try:
   459→                import yaml as _y
   460→                cfg_path = _hermes_home / "config.yaml"
   461→                if cfg_path.exists():
   462→                    with open(cfg_path, encoding="utf-8") as _f:
   463→                        cfg = _y.safe_load(_f) or {}
   464→                    file_path = cfg.get("prefill_messages_file", "")
   465→            except Exception:
   466→                pass
   467→        if not file_path:
   468→            return []
   469→        path = Path(file_path).expanduser()
   470→        if not path.is_absolute():
   471→            path = _hermes_home / path
   472→        if not path.exists():
   473→            logger.warning("Prefill messages file not found: %s", path)
   474→            return []
   475→        try:
   476→            with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   477→                data = _json.load(f)
   478→            if not isinstance(data, list):
   479→                logger.warning("Prefill messages file must contain a JSON array: %s", path)
   480→                return []
   481→            return data
   482→        except Exception as e:
   483→            logger.warning("Failed to load prefill messages from %s: %s", path, e)
   484→            return []
   485→
   486→    @staticmethod
   487→    def _load_ephemeral_system_prompt() -> str:
   488→        """Load ephemeral system prompt from config or env var.
   489→        
   490→        Checks HERMES_EPHEMERAL_SYSTEM_PROMPT env var first, then falls back to
   491→        agent.system_prompt in ~/.hermes/config.yaml.
   492→        """
   493→        prompt = os.getenv("HERMES_EPHEMERAL_SYSTEM_PROMPT", "")
   494→        if prompt:
   495→            return prompt
   496→        try:
   497→            import yaml as _y
   498→            cfg_path = _hermes_home / "config.yaml"
   499→            if cfg_path.exists():
   500→                with open(cfg_path, encoding="utf-8") as _f:
   501→                    cfg = _y.safe_load(_f) or {}
   502→                return (cfg.get("agent", {}).get("system_prompt", "") or "").strip()
   503→        except Exception:
   504→            pass
   505→        return ""
   506→
   507→    @staticmethod
   508→    def _load_reasoning_config() -> dict | None:
   509→        """Load reasoning effort from config or env var.
   510→        
   511→        Checks HERMES_REASONING_EFFORT env var first, then agent.reasoning_effort
   512→        in config.yaml. Valid: "xhigh", "high", "medium", "low", "minimal", "none".
   513→        Returns None to use default (medium).
   514→        """
   515→        effort = os.getenv("HERMES_REASONING_EFFORT", "")
   516→        if not effort:
   517→            try:
   518→                import yaml as _y
   519→                cfg_path = _hermes_home / "config.yaml"
   520→                if cfg_path.exists():
   521→                    with open(cfg_path, encoding="utf-8") as _f:
   522→                        cfg = _y.safe_load(_f) or {}
   523→                    effort = str(cfg.get("agent", {}).get("reasoning_effort", "") or "").strip()
   524→            except Exception:
   525→                pass
   526→        if not effort:
   527→            return None
   528→        effort = effort.lower().strip()
   529→        if effort == "none":
   530→            return {"enabled": False}
   531→        valid = ("xhigh", "high", "medium", "low", "minimal")
   532→        if effort in valid:
   533→            return {"enabled": True, "effort": effort}
   534→        logger.warning("Unknown reasoning_effort '%s', using default (medium)", effort)
   535→        return None
   536→
   537→    @staticmethod
   538→    def _load_show_reasoning() -> bool:
   539→        """Load show_reasoning toggle from config.yaml display section."""
   540→        try:
   541→            import yaml as _y
   542→            cfg_path = _hermes_home / "config.yaml"
   543→            if cfg_path.exists():
   544→                with open(cfg_path, encoding="utf-8") as _f:
   545→                    cfg = _y.safe_load(_f) or {}
   546→                return bool(cfg.get("display", {}).get("show_reasoning", False))
   547→        except Exception:
   548→            pass
   549→        return False
   550→
   551→    @staticmethod
   552→    def _load_background_notifications_mode() -> str:
   553→        """Load background process notification mode from config or env var.
   554→
   555→        Modes:
   556→          - ``all``    — push running-output updates *and* the final message (default)
   557→          - ``result`` — only the final completion message (regardless of exit code)
   558→          - ``error``  — only the final message when exit code is non-zero
   559→          - ``off``    — no watcher messages at all
   560→        """
   561→        mode = os.getenv("HERMES_BACKGROUND_NOTIFICATIONS", "")
   562→        if not mode:
   563→            try:
   564→                import yaml as _y
   565→                cfg_path = _hermes_home / "config.yaml"
   566→                if cfg_path.exists():
   567→                    with open(cfg_path, encoding="utf-8") as _f:
   568→                        cfg = _y.safe_load(_f) or {}
   569→                    raw = cfg.get("display", {}).get("background_process_notifications")
   570→                    if raw is False:
   571→                        mode = "off"
   572→                    elif raw not in (None, ""):
   573→                        mode = str(raw)
   574→            except Exception:
   575→                pass
   576→        mode = (mode or "all").strip().lower()
   577→        valid = {"all", "result", "error", "off"}
   578→        if mode not in valid:
   579→            logger.warning(
   580→                "Unknown background_process_notifications '%s', defaulting to 'all'",
   581→                mode,
   582→            )
   583→            return "all"
   584→        return mode
   585→
   586→    @staticmethod
   587→    def _load_provider_routing() -> dict:
   588→        """Load OpenRouter provider routing preferences from config.yaml."""
   589→        try:
   590→            import yaml as _y
   591→            cfg_path = _hermes_home / "config.yaml"
   592→            if cfg_path.exists():
   593→                with open(cfg_path, encoding="utf-8") as _f:
   594→                    cfg = _y.safe_load(_f) or {}
   595→                return cfg.get("provider_routing", {}) or {}
   596→        except Exception:
   597→            pass
   598→        return {}
   599→
   600→    @staticmethod
   601→    def _load_fallback_model() -> dict | None:
   602→        """Load fallback model config from config.yaml.
   603→
   604→        Returns a dict with 'provider' and 'model' keys, or None if
   605→        not configured / both fields empty.
   606→        """
   607→        try:
   608→            import yaml as _y
   609→            cfg_path = _hermes_home / "config.yaml"
   610→            if cfg_path.exists():
   611→                with open(cfg_path, encoding="utf-8") as _f:
   612→                    cfg = _y.safe_load(_f) or {}
   613→                fb = cfg.get("fallback_model", {}) or {}
   614→                if fb.get("provider") and fb.get("model"):
   615→                    return fb
   616→        except Exception:
   617→            pass
   618→        return None
   619→
   620→    async def start(self) -> bool:
   621→        """
   622→        Start the gateway and all configured platform adapters.
   623→        
   624→        Returns True if at least one adapter connected successfully.
   625→        """
   626→        logger.info("Starting Hermes Gateway...")
   627→        logger.info("Session storage: %s", self.config.sessions_dir)
   628→        
   629→        # Warn if no user allowlists are configured and open access is not opted in
   630→        _any_allowlist = any(
   631→            os.getenv(v)
   632→            for v in ("TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS", "DISCORD_ALLOWED_USERS",
   633→                       "WHATSAPP_ALLOWED_USERS", "SLACK_ALLOWED_USERS",
   634→                       "GATEWAY_ALLOWED_USERS")
   635→        )
   636→        _allow_all = os.getenv("GATEWAY_ALLOW_ALL_USERS", "").lower() in ("true", "1", "yes")
   637→        if not _any_allowlist and not _allow_all:
   638→            logger.warning(
   639→                "No user allowlists configured. All unauthorized users will be denied. "
   640→                "Set GATEWAY_ALLOW_ALL_USERS=true in ~/.hermes/.env to allow open access, "
   641→                "or configure platform allowlists (e.g., TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS=your_id)."
   642→            )
   643→        
   644→        # Discover and load event hooks
   645→        self.hooks.discover_and_load()
   646→        
   647→        # Recover background processes from checkpoint (crash recovery)
   648→        try:
   649→            from tools.process_registry import process_registry
   650→            recovered = process_registry.recover_from_checkpoint()
   651→            if recovered:
   652→                logger.info("Recovered %s background process(es) from previous run", recovered)
   653→        except Exception as e:
   654→            logger.warning("Process checkpoint recovery: %s", e)
   655→        
   656→        connected_count = 0
   657→        
   658→        # Initialize and connect each configured platform
   659→        for platform, platform_config in self.config.platforms.items():
   660→            if not platform_config.enabled:
   661→                continue
   662→            
   663→            adapter = self._create_adapter(platform, platform_config)
   664→            if not adapter:
   665→                logger.warning("No adapter available for %s", platform.value)
   666→                continue
   667→            
   668→            # Set up message handler
   669→            adapter.set_message_handler(self._handle_message)
   670→            
   671→            # Try to connect
   672→            logger.info("Connecting to %s...", platform.value)
   673→            try:
   674→                success = await adapter.connect()
   675→                if success:
   676→                    self.adapters[platform] = adapter
   677→                    connected_count += 1
   678→                    logger.info("✓ %s connected", platform.value)
   679→                else:
   680→                    logger.warning("✗ %s failed to connect", platform.value)
   681→            except Exception as e:
   682→                logger.error("✗ %s error: %s", platform.value, e)
   683→        
   684→        if connected_count == 0:
   685→            logger.warning("No messaging platforms connected.")
   686→            logger.info("Gateway will continue running for cron job execution.")
   687→        
   688→        # Update delivery router with adapters
   689→        self.delivery_router.adapters = self.adapters
   690→        
   691→        self._running = True
   692→        
   693→        # Emit gateway:startup hook
   694→        hook_count = len(self.hooks.loaded_hooks)
   695→        if hook_count:
   696→            logger.info("%s hook(s) loaded", hook_count)
   697→        await self.hooks.emit("gateway:startup", {
   698→            "platforms": [p.value for p in self.adapters.keys()],
   699→        })
```

```
  2068→    async def _generate_autoreply(self, session_key: str, session_id: str):
  2069→        """Generate an auto-reply using the auxiliary LLM client.
  2070→
  2071→        Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).  reply_text is None when
  2072→        auto-reply is inactive or the turn cap was just hit.
  2073→        """
  2074→        from agent.autoreply import check_and_advance, build_autoreply_messages
  2075→
  2076→        config = self._autoreply_configs.get(session_key)
  2077→        if not config:
  2078→            return None, False
  2079→
  2080→        text, cap_reached = check_and_advance(config)
  2081→        if cap_reached:
  2082→            del self._autoreply_configs[session_key]
  2083→            return None, True
  2084→        if text:
  2085→            return text, False
  2086→
  2087→        # LLM-generated: build prompt from transcript and call async LLM
  2088→        history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_id)
  2089→        messages = build_autoreply_messages(config, history)
  2090→
  2091→        from agent.auxiliary_client import async_call_llm
  2092→        call_kwargs = {
  2093→            "task": "autoreply",
  2094→            "messages": messages,
  2095→            "temperature": 0.7,
  2096→            "max_tokens": 1024,
  2097→        }
  2098→        if config.get("model"):
  2099→            call_kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
  2100→
  2101→        response = await async_call_llm(**call_kwargs)
  2102→        reply_text = response.choices[0].message.content.strip()
  2103→        config["turn_count"] += 1
  2104→        return reply_text, False
  2105→
  2106→    async def _process_autoreply(self, source, session_key: str, session_id: str):
  2107→        """Fire-and-forget task: generate an auto-reply and inject it via the adapter."""
  2108→        try:
  2109→            adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
  2110→            if not adapter:
  2111→                return
  2112→
  2113→            autoreply_text, cap_reached = await self._generate_autoreply(
  2114→                session_key, session_id)
  2115→
  2116→            if not autoreply_text:
  2117→                if cap_reached:
  2118→                    metadata = {"thread_id": source.thread_id} if source.thread_id else None
  2119→                    await adapter.send(
  2120→                        chat_id=source.chat_id,
  2121→                        content="_[Auto-reply limit reached. Use `/autoreply` to re-enable.]_",
  2122→                        metadata=metadata,
  2123→                    )
  2124→                return
  2125→
  2126→            synthetic_event = MessageEvent(
  2127→                text=autoreply_text,
  2128→                source=source,
  2129→                message_id=f"autoreply-{time.time()}",
  2130→            )
  2131→            await adapter.handle_message(synthetic_event)
  2132→        except Exception as e:
  2133→            logger.error("[AutoReply] Failed: %s", e)
  2134→
  2135→    async def _handle_retry_command(self, event: MessageEvent) -> str:
  2136→        """Handle /retry command - re-send the last user message."""
  2137→        source = event.source
  2138→        session_entry = self.session_store.get_or_create_session(source)
  2139→        history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_entry.session_id)
  2140→        
  2141→        # Find the last user message
  2142→        last_user_msg = None
  2143→        last_user_idx = None
  2144→        for i in range(len(history) - 1, -1, -1):
  2145→            if history[i].get("role") == "user":
  2146→                last_user_msg = history[i].get("content", "")
  2147→                last_user_idx = i
  2148→                break
  2149→        
  2150→        if not last_user_msg:
  2151→            return "No previous message to retry."
  2152→        
  2153→        # Truncate history to before the last user message and persist
  2154→        truncated = history[:last_user_idx]
  2155→        self.session_store.rewrite_transcript(session_entry.session_id, truncated)
  2156→        # Reset stored token count — transcript was truncated
  2157→        session_entry.last_prompt_tokens = 0
```

```
2068→    async def _generate_autoreply(self, session_key: str, session_id: str):
  2069→        """Generate an auto-reply using the auxiliary LLM client.
  2070→
  2071→        Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).  reply_text is None when
  2072→        auto-reply is inactive or the turn cap was just hit.
  2073→        """
  2074→        from agent.autoreply import check_and_advance, build_autoreply_messages
  2075→
  2076→        config = self._autoreply_configs.get(session_key)
  2077→        if not config:
  2078→            return None, False
  2079→
  2080→        text, cap_reached = check_and_advance(config)
  2081→        if cap_reached:
  2082→            del self._autoreply_configs[session_key]
  2083→            return None, True
  2084→        if text:
  2085→            return text, False
  2086→
  2087→        # LLM-generated: build prompt from transcript and call async LLM
  2088→        history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_id)
  2089→        messages = build_autoreply_messages(config, history)
  2090→
  2091→        from agent.auxiliary_client import async_call_llm
  2092→        call_kwargs = {
  2093→            "task": "autoreply",
  2094→            "messages": messages,
  2095→            "temperature": 0.7,
  2096→            "max_tokens": 1024,
  2097→        }
  2098→        if config.get("model"):
  2099→            call_kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
  2100→
  2101→        response = await async_call_llm(**call_kwargs)
  2102→        reply_text = response.choices[0].message.content.strip()
  2103→        config["turn_count"] += 1
  2104→        return reply_text, False

  2136→        """Handle /retry command - re-send the last user message."""

  2139→        history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_entry.session_id)
  2140→
```

```
  4944→        atexit.register(_run_cleanup)
  4945→        
  4946→        # Run the application with patch_stdout for proper output handling
  4947→        try:
  4948→            with patch_stdout():
  4949→                app.run()
```

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the autoreply implementation. Let me create a summary document of my findings.

## Comprehensive Analysis: Autoreply Feature Implementation

I've completed a thorough exploration of the autoreply feature across the codebase. Here's my detailed analysis:

### 1. **End-to-End Flow**

#### **Enabling Autoreply**
1. User sends `/autoreply <instructions>` command
2. Command handler (`_handle_autoreply_command`) parses args via `parse_autoreply_args()`
3. Config stored in in-memory dict: `_autoreply_configs[session_key]`
4. Supports modes: LLM-generated (default) or literal (direct reply)

#### **Message Processing Loop**
1. User sends a message
2. Message handler detects if it's an autoreply via message_id prefix: `(event.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")`
3. **Real user messages** reset the turn counter to 0
4. **Autoreply-generated messages** don't reset the counter
5. Agent processes message and returns response
6. If autoreply enabled, response is labeled: `**[Auto-reply {turn}/{max}]:** {event.text}\n\n---\n\n{response}`
7. Fire-and-forget async task (`_process_autoreply`) scheduled to generate next reply
8. Next autoreply text injected back via adapter as synthetic MessageEvent

#### **Disabling Autoreply**
- `/autoreply off` - removes config, disables loop
- `/reset` or `/new` - clears config, resets session
- `/stop` - removes config, stops agent
- Turn cap reached - auto-disabled, user notified

### 2. **Architecture & File Organization**

```
agent/autoreply.py (shared logic, no I/O)
├── parse_autoreply_args()        # Parse /autoreply command args
├── build_autoreply_messages()    # Build LLM prompt from history
├── check_and_advance()           # Check turn cap, handle literal mode
└── format_status()               # Format status display

gateway/run.py (async platform loop)
├── _handle_autoreply_command()   # /autoreply command handler
├── _generate_autoreply()         # Generate reply (LLM or literal)
├── _process_autoreply()          # Fire-and-forget task: generate & inject
├── _autoreply_configs            # Dict[session_key] → config
└── Integration in _handle_message()
    ├── Detect autoreply messages by ID prefix
    ├── Reset turn counter on real messages
    ├── Label responses with [Auto-reply N/M]
    └── Schedule _process_autoreply as async task

cli.py (sync REPL with threading)
├── _handle_autoreply_command()   # /autoreply command handler
├── _generate_autoreply_text()    # Generate reply (calls sync LLM)
├── _autoreply_config             # Single config object
└── Integration in message loop
    ├── Reset turn counter on real messages
    ├── Use [autoreply] prefix in pending input queue
    └── Inline generation (no async task spawning)

control_api.py
└── AVAILABLE_COMMANDS (info only)

hermes_cli/commands.py
└── COMMANDS dict (help text)
```

### 3. **Key Design Decisions**

**Config Storage:**
- **Gateway**: Dict keyed by session_key (per-session, ephemeral)
- **CLI**: Single `_autoreply_config` object (per-REPL, ephemeral)
- **Lifespan**: In-memory only, cleared on reset/stop/cap-reached

**Message Injection:**
- **Gateway**: Synthetic MessageEvent with `message_id=f"autoreply-{time.time()}"`
- **CLI**: Queued string with `[autoreply]` prefix, stripped before agent call

**Generation:**
- **Gateway**: Async via `agent.auxiliary_client.async_call_llm` (fire-and-forget)
- **CLI**: Sync via `agent.auxiliary_client.call_llm` (inline, blocks until reply)

**Turn Counter:**
- Reset to 0 when **real user message** arrives
- Incremented only when reply is actually generated
- Checked against `max_turns` before generation

### 4. **Potential Design Issues & Edge Cases**

#### **Critical Issue: Race Condition in Gateway**

**Problem**: Turn counter reset and autoreply generation are not atomic.

**Scenario**:
1. User sends message → turn_count reset to 0
2. Agent processes message, response generated
3. Response labeled with "Auto-reply 1/20" ✓
4. Fire-and-forget task spawned to generate next reply
5. **Before async task runs**, user sends another message
6. Turn counter reset to 0 again
7. Next autoreply generates with turn_count still 0

**Impact**: Turn counter label can be out of sync with actual turn count if user messages arrive while async tasks are pending.

**Code location**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` lines 1474-1476, 1616-1619

#### **Issue: Response Labeling Uses Message Input, Not Generated Text**

The label includes `event.text` (what the user/autoreply said), not the final agent response:
```python
response = (
    f"**[Auto-reply {ar_config['turn_count']}/{cap}]:** "
    f"{event.text}\n\n---\n\n{response}"  # event.text = user input, not the reply
)
```

This is confusing because it shows:
```
**[Auto-reply 1/20]:** Ask about machine learning

---

[Agent's response here]
```

**Expectation**: Users might expect the label to precede the **agent's response**, not the user's input.

#### **Issue: CLI Doesn't Label Autoreply Responses**

The CLI generates autoreply text and injects it, but **does not label the agent's response** with turn count info like the gateway does. The gateway labels responses with `**[Auto-reply N/M]:**` but the CLI only shows a status line `[Auto-reply N/M]` before generating the next turn.

**Code location**: 
- Gateway: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` lines 1607-1614
- CLI: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` lines 4931 (status line only)

#### **Issue: No Error Handling for LLM Failures in Gateway**

If the LLM call fails in `_generate_autoreply()`, the exception is caught silently:
```python
except Exception as e:
    logger.error("[AutoReply] Failed: %s", e)
```

The autoreply loop **silently stops** without notifying the user. The config is not cleaned up, so the user might think autoreply is still running but getting no responses.

**Code location**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` lines 2132-2133

**Missing**: Should send a message like "Auto-reply failed: {error}. Use `/autoreply off` to disable." or auto-disable with notification.

#### **Issue: CLI Autoreply Doesn't Survive Multi-Session Scenarios**

In the CLI, `_autoreply_config` is session-local but not tied to session identity. If a user runs `/resume [session_name]` or switches sessions manually, the autoreply config is not preserved.

**Code location**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` line 1184

#### **Issue: Literal Mode Config Doesn't Persist Model Field**

Literal mode (`--literal`) sets `"model": None` in the config but doesn't have a way to override it later. If the user tries to set a model for a literal autoreply, there's no code path for it (the `/autoreply max` command only updates max_turns).

**Code location**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/agent/autoreply.py` lines 71-79

#### **Issue: No Rate Limiting**

If a user enables autoreply with `--forever` or no `--max` limit, the feature will generate replies indefinitely as long as the user keeps sending messages. There's no built-in rate limiting or safety valve.

**Code location**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/agent/autoreply.py` line 54 (max_turns=0 means unlimited)

#### **Issue: Turn Counter Not Persisted Across Sessions**

Turn counter is reset to 0 on every session reset or container restart. If the gateway/CLI restarts, the counter state is lost. This is probably intentional (ephemeral state), but worth noting.

### 5. **Test Coverage Assessment**

**Excellent coverage** for:
- Command parsing (enable, disable, max, literal, errors)
- Status display
- Turn counting and reset logic
- LLM generation and message injection
- Cap notification
- Cleanup on reset/stop
- Thread safety in tests

**Missing/Weak coverage**:
- Concurrent user messages while autoreply is generating (race condition)
- LLM failure handling
- Edge case: user sends message, then immediately sends `/autoreply off` during generation
- Multi-session/session-switching scenarios (CLI only)

### 6. **Summary Table**

| Aspect | Gateway | CLI | Status |
|--------|---------|-----|--------|
| Command parsing | `parse_autoreply_args()` | `parse_autoreply_args()` | Shared ✓ |
| Config storage | `Dict[session_key]` | Single object | Appropriate ✓ |
| LLM generation | Async (fire-and-forget) | Sync (inline) | Platform-appropriate ✓ |
| Message injection | MessageEvent with autoreply- ID | Queue with [autoreply] prefix | Working ✓ |
| Turn counter reset | On real messages | On real messages | Consistent ✓ |
| Response labeling | With turn count | Status line only | **Inconsistent** ⚠ |
| Error handling | Silent (logged only) | User feedback | **Different** ⚠ |
| Cleanup | Reset/stop commands | Reset command | Both implement ✓ |

---

### **Key Files**

1. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/agent/autoreply.py` - Core logic (170 lines)
2. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` - Gateway integration (~150 lines across multiple methods)
3. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` - CLI integration (~150 lines across multiple methods)
4. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/tests/gateway/test_autoreply_command.py` - Test suite (658 lines)

```
1→"""
     2→Local-only HTTP control API for the Hermes gateway.
     3→
     4→Runs alongside the gateway as a background aiohttp server on 127.0.0.1.
     5→Provides programmatic control over live agent sessions — session listing,
     6→message injection, etc.
     7→
     8→External tools (e.g., desloppify) can call these endpoints to influence
     9→a running Hermes agent without needing direct process access.
    10→
    11→All mutations go through POST /sessions/{key}/message — inject any text
    12→(including /commands) as if the user typed it.
    13→
    14→Default port: 47823 (override with HERMES_CONTROL_PORT env var).
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import logging
    19→import os
    20→
    21→from aiohttp import web
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    26→
    27→# Commands available via /message injection.  This list is informational —
    28→# the message endpoint accepts any text, but GET /commands exposes these
    29→# so external tools know what's available.
    30→AVAILABLE_COMMANDS = [
    31→    {"command": "/reset", "description": "Reset conversation history"},
    32→    {"command": "/new", "description": "Start a new conversation"},
    33→    {"command": "/stop", "description": "Stop the running agent"},
    34→    {"command": "/compact", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    35→    {"command": "/compress", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    36→    {"command": "/status", "description": "Show session info"},
    37→    {"command": "/model <provider:model>", "description": "Switch model"},
    38→    {"command": "/personality <name>", "description": "Switch personality"},
    39→    {"command": "/autoreply <instructions>", "description": "Enable auto-reply loop"},
    40→    {"command": "/autoreply off", "description": "Disable auto-reply loop"},
    41→    {"command": "/reasoning <level>", "description": "Set reasoning effort"},
    42→    {"command": "/rollback [number]", "description": "List or restore checkpoints"},
    43→    {"command": "/background <prompt>", "description": "Run prompt in background session"},
    44→    {"command": "/undo", "description": "Undo last assistant response"},
    45→    {"command": "/retry", "description": "Retry last message"},
    46→    {"command": "/usage", "description": "Show token usage"},
    47→    {"command": "/help", "description": "List commands"},
    48→]
    49→
    50→
    51→def _get_port() -> int:
    52→    return int(os.getenv("HERMES_CONTROL_PORT", str(DEFAULT_PORT)))
    53→
    54→
    55→def _json_response(data: dict, status: int = 200) -> web.Response:
    56→    return web.Response(
    57→        text=json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False),
    58→        content_type="application/json",
    59→        status=status,
    60→    )
    61→
    62→
    63→class ControlAPI:
    64→    """Lightweight control API that holds a reference to the GatewayRunner."""
    65→
    66→    def __init__(self, gateway_runner):
    67→        self.runner = gateway_runner
    68→        self.app = web.Application()
    69→        self._setup_routes()
    70→        self._site = None
    71→
    72→    def _setup_routes(self):
    73→        self.app.router.add_get("/health", self.health)
    74→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions", self.list_sessions)
    75→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions/{key}", self.get_session)
    76→        self.app.router.add_get("/commands", self.list_commands)
    77→        self.app.router.add_post("/sessions/{key}/message", self.send_message)
    78→
    79→    # ── Helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    80→
    81→    def _resolve_agent(self, key: str):
    82→        """Look up a running agent by session key.  '_any' returns the first.
    83→        Returns (resolved_key, agent) or (key, None) if not found."""
    84→        if key == "_any":
    85→            if not self.runner._running_agents:
    86→                return key, None
    87→            key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
    88→        return key, self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
    89→
    90→    # ── Endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    async def health(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    93→        return _json_response({"status": "ok"})
    94→
    95→    async def list_sessions(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    96→        """List all sessions, marking which have a live (running) agent."""
    97→        sessions = []
    98→        for key, agent in self.runner._running_agents.items():
    99→            entry = {
   100→                "session_key": key,
   101→                "status": "running",
   102→                "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   103→                "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   104→                **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   105→            }
   106→            sessions.append(entry)
   107→        return _json_response({"sessions": sessions})
   108→
   109→    async def get_session(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   110→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   111→        key, agent = self._resolve_agent(key)
   112→        if not agent:
   113→            return _json_response(
   114→                {"error": f"No running agent for '{key}'"},
   115→                status=404,
   116→            )
   117→        return _json_response({
   118→            "session_key": key,
   119→            "status": "running",
   120→            "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   121→            "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   122→            **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   123→        })
   124→
   125→    async def list_commands(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   126→        """List available slash commands that can be sent via /message."""
   127→        return _json_response({"commands": AVAILABLE_COMMANDS})
   128→
   129→    async def send_message(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   130→        """Inject a message (or /command) into a session.
   131→
   132→        POST body: {"text": "...", "mode": "interrupt"|"queue"}
   133→
   134→        Modes:
   135→        - interrupt (default): cancel current agent run, process this message
   136→        - queue: let current run finish, then process this message next
   137→        """
   138→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   139→
   140→        try:
   141→            body = await request.json()
   142→        except Exception:
   143→            return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
   144→
   145→        text = body.get("text", "").strip()
   146→        if not text:
   147→            return _json_response({"error": "'text' is required"}, status=400)
   148→
   149→        mode = body.get("mode", "interrupt")
   150→        if mode not in ("interrupt", "queue"):
   151→            return _json_response(
   152→                {"error": f"Invalid mode '{mode}', must be 'interrupt' or 'queue'"},
   153→                status=400,
   154→            )
   155→
   156→        logger.info(
   157→            "Control API: send_message session=%s mode=%s text=%s",
   158→            key, mode, text[:80],
   159→        )
   160→
   161→        try:
   162→            result = self.runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=(mode != "queue"))
   163→            # await if coroutine (GatewayRunner is async, CLI shim is sync)
   164→            if hasattr(result, "__await__"):
   165→                await result
   166→        except LookupError as e:
   167→            return _json_response({"error": str(e)}, status=404)
   168→
   169→        return _json_response({"success": True, "message": "Message submitted"})
   170→
   171→    # ── Lifecycle ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   172→
   173→    async def start(self):
   174→        port = _get_port()
   175→        app_runner = web.AppRunner(self.app, access_log=None)
   176→        await app_runner.setup()
   177→        self._site = web.TCPSite(app_runner, "127.0.0.1", port)
   178→        try:
   179→            await self._site.start()
   180→            logger.info("Control API listening on http://127.0.0.1:%d", port)
   181→            self._write_port_file(port)
   182→        except OSError as e:
   183→            logger.warning("Control API failed to bind port %d: %s", port, e)
   184→
   185→    async def stop(self):
   186→        if self._site:
   187→            await self._site.stop()
   188→            self._remove_port_file()
   189→            logger.info("Control API stopped")
   190→
   191→    @staticmethod
   192→    def _write_port_file(port: int):
   193→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   194→        try:
   195→            with open(path, "w") as f:
   196→                f.write(str(port))
   197→        except OSError:
   198→            pass
   199→
   200→    @staticmethod
   201→    def _remove_port_file():
   202→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   203→        try:
   204→            os.remove(path)
   205→        except OSError:
   206→            pass
   207→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Hermes control client — lightweight module for external tools to control
     3→a running Hermes instance (CLI or gateway).
     4→
     5→Usage:
     6→    from gateway.hermes_control_client import HermesControl
     7→
     8→    ctl = HermesControl()  # auto-discovers port from ~/.hermes/control_api.port
     9→
    10→    # Send a message (interrupt mode — stops current work)
    11→    ctl.send_message("Focus on this instead")
    12→
    13→    # Queue a message (processed after current work finishes)
    14→    ctl.send_message("When you're done, also check logs", mode="queue")
    15→
    16→    # Send a slash command
    17→    ctl.send_message("/reset")
    18→    ctl.send_message("/model openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4")
    19→
    20→    # List running sessions
    21→    sessions = ctl.list_sessions()
    22→
    23→    # List available commands
    24→    commands = ctl.list_commands()
    25→
    26→Zero dependencies beyond stdlib (uses urllib, not requests/aiohttp).
    27→"""
    28→
    29→import json
    30→import os
    31→import urllib.request
    32→import urllib.error
    33→from typing import Optional
    34→
    35→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    36→PORT_FILE = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
    37→
    38→
    39→class HermesControl:
    40→    """Client for the Hermes control API."""
    41→
    42→    def __init__(self, host: str = "127.0.0.1", port: Optional[int] = None):
    43→        if port is None:
    44→            port = self._discover_port()
    45→        self.base_url = f"http://{host}:{port}"
    46→
    47→    @staticmethod
    48→    def _discover_port() -> int:
    49→        try:
    50→            with open(PORT_FILE) as f:
    51→                return int(f.read().strip())
    52→        except (OSError, ValueError):
    53→            return DEFAULT_PORT
    54→
    55→    def _request(self, method: str, path: str, body: dict = None) -> dict:
    56→        url = f"{self.base_url}{path}"
    57→        data = json.dumps(body).encode() if body else None
    58→        req = urllib.request.Request(
    59→            url,
    60→            data=data,
    61→            method=method,
    62→            headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"} if data else {},
    63→        )
    64→        try:
    65→            with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=5) as resp:
    66→                return json.loads(resp.read())
    67→        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
    68→            return json.loads(e.read())
    69→        except urllib.error.URLError as e:
    70→            return {"error": f"Cannot reach Hermes control API: {e.reason}"}
    71→
    72→    # ── Read-only endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────
    73→
    74→    def health(self) -> dict:
    75→        """Check if the control API is running."""
    76→        return self._request("GET", "/health")
    77→
    78→    def list_sessions(self) -> dict:
    79→        """List all running agent sessions."""
    80→        return self._request("GET", "/sessions")
    81→
    82→    def get_session(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> dict:
    83→        """Get info about a specific session."""
    84→        return self._request("GET", f"/sessions/{session_key}")
    85→
    86→    def list_commands(self) -> dict:
    87→        """List available slash commands."""
    88→        return self._request("GET", "/commands")
    89→
    90→    # ── Message injection ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    def send_message(
    93→        self,
    94→        text: str,
    95→        session_key: str = "_any",
    96→        mode: str = "interrupt",
    97→    ) -> dict:
    98→        """Send a message (or /command) into a running session.
    99→
   100→        Args:
   101→            text: Message text or slash command (e.g., "hello", "/reset").
   102→            session_key: Session to target. "_any" targets the first running session.
   103→            mode: "interrupt" (default) stops current work; "queue" waits for it to finish.
   104→        """
   105→        return self._request("POST", f"/sessions/{session_key}/message", {
   106→            "text": text,
   107→            "mode": mode,
   108→        })
   109→
   110→    # ── Convenience methods ────────────────────────────────────────────
   111→
   112→    def switch_model(self, provider: str, model: str, **kw) -> dict:
   113→        """Switch model via /model command."""
   114→        return self.send_message(f"/model {provider}:{model}", **kw)
   115→
   116→    def compact_context(self, **kw) -> dict:
   117→        """Trigger context compaction."""
   118→        return self.send_message("/compact", **kw)
   119→
   120→    def reset(self, **kw) -> dict:
   121→        """Reset conversation history."""
   122→        return self.send_message("/reset", **kw)
   123→
   124→    def stop(self, **kw) -> dict:
   125→        """Stop the running agent."""
   126→        return self.send_message("/stop", **kw)
   127→
   128→    def is_autoreply_enabled(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> bool:
   129→        """Check if autoreply is enabled for a session."""
   130→        info = self.get_session(session_key)
   131→        return bool(info.get("autoreply", {}).get("enabled"))
   132→
   133→    def is_available(self) -> bool:
   134→        """Check if a Hermes instance with control API is reachable."""
   135→        try:
   136→            return self.health().get("status") == "ok"
   137→        except Exception:
   138→            return False
   139→
```

```
292→    async def inject_message(self, key: str, text: str, *, interrupt: bool = True):
   293→        """Inject a synthetic message into a session via its platform adapter.
   294→
   295→        Resolves the session key ('_any' picks the first running session),
   296→        builds a MessageEvent from the session's stored origin, and hands it
   297→        to the adapter's handle_message with the requested interrupt mode.
   298→        """
   299→        if key == "_any":
   300→            if not self._running_agents:
   301→                raise LookupError("No running session to inject into")
   302→            key = next(iter(self._running_agents))
   303→
   304→        self.session_store._ensure_loaded()
   305→        entry = self.session_store._entries.get(key)
   306→        if not entry or not entry.origin:
   307→            raise LookupError(f"No session found for '{key}'")
   308→
   309→        source = entry.origin
   310→        adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
   311→        if not adapter:
   312→            raise LookupError(f"No adapter for platform '{source.platform}'")
   313→
   314→        event = MessageEvent(
   315→            text=text,
   316→            source=source,
   317→            message_id=f"control-{time.time()}",
   318→        )
   319→        await adapter.handle_message(event, interrupt=interrupt)
   320→
   321→    def _get_or_create_gateway_honcho(self, session_key: str):
   322→        """Return a persistent Honcho manager/config pair for this gateway session."""
   323→        if not hasattr(self, "_honcho_managers"):
   324→            self._honcho_managers = {}
   325→        if not hasattr(self, "_honcho_configs"):
   326→            self._honcho_configs = {}
   327→
   328→        if session_key in self._honcho_managers:
   329→            return self._honcho_managers[session_key], self._honcho_configs.get(session_key)
   330→
   331→        try:
   332→            from honcho_integration.client import HonchoClientConfig, get_honcho_client
   333→            from honcho_integration.session import HonchoSessionManager
   334→
   335→            hcfg = HonchoClientConfig.from_global_config()
   336→            if not hcfg.enabled or not hcfg.api_key:
   337→                return None, hcfg
   338→
   339→            client = get_honcho_client(hcfg)
   340→            manager = HonchoSessionManager(
   341→                honcho=client,
   342→                config=hcfg,
   343→                context_tokens=hcfg.context_tokens,
   344→            )
   345→            self._honcho_managers[session_key] = manager
   346→            self._honcho_configs[session_key] = hcfg
   347→            return manager, hcfg
   348→        except Exception as e:
   349→            logger.debug("Gateway Honcho init failed for %s: %s", session_key, e)
   350→            return None, None
   351→
   352→    def _shutdown_gateway_honcho(self, session_key: str) -> None:
   353→        """Flush and close the persistent Honcho manager for a gateway session."""
   354→        managers = getattr(self, "_honcho_managers", None)
   355→        configs = getattr(self, "_honcho_configs", None)
   356→        if managers is None or configs is None:
   357→            return
   358→
   359→        manager = managers.pop(session_key, None)
   360→        configs.pop(session_key, None)
   361→        if not manager:
   362→            return
   363→        try:
   364→            manager.shutdown()
   365→        except Exception as e:
   366→            logger.debug("Gateway Honcho shutdown failed for %s: %s", session_key, e)
   367→
   368→    def _shutdown_all_gateway_honcho(self) -> None:
   369→        """Flush and close all persistent Honcho managers."""
   370→        managers = getattr(self, "_honcho_managers", None)
   371→        if not managers:
   372→            return
   373→        for session_key in list(managers.keys()):
   374→            self._shutdown_gateway_honcho(session_key)
   375→    
   376→    def _flush_memories_for_session(self, old_session_id: str):
   377→        """Prompt the agent to save memories/skills before context is lost.
   378→
   379→        Synchronous worker — meant to be called via run_in_executor from
   380→        an async context so it doesn't block the event loop.
   381→        """
   382→        try:
   383→            history = self.session_store.load_transcript(old_session_id)
   384→            if not history or len(history) < 4:
   385→                return
   386→
   387→            from run_agent import AIAgent
```
