912→    async def _handle_message(self, event: MessageEvent) -> Optional[str]:
   913→        """
   914→        Handle an incoming message from any platform.
   915→        
   916→        This is the core message processing pipeline:
   917→        1. Check user authorization
   918→        2. Check for commands (/new, /reset, etc.)
   919→        3. Check for running agent and interrupt if needed
   920→        4. Get or create session
   921→        5. Build context for agent
   922→        6. Run agent conversation
   923→        7. Return response
   924→        """
   925→        source = event.source
   926→        
   927→        # Check if user is authorized
   928→        if not self._is_user_authorized(source):
   929→            logger.warning("Unauthorized user: %s (%s) on %s", source.user_id, source.user_name, source.platform.value)
   930→            # In DMs: offer pairing code. In groups: silently ignore.
   931→            if source.chat_type == "dm":
   932→                platform_name = source.platform.value if source.platform else "unknown"
   933→                code = self.pairing_store.generate_code(
   934→                    platform_name, source.user_id, source.user_name or ""
   935→                )
   936→                if code:
   937→                    adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
   938→                    if adapter:
   939→                        await adapter.send(
   940→                            source.chat_id,
   941→                            f"Hi~ I don't recognize you yet!\n\n"
   942→                            f"Here's your pairing code: `{code}`\n\n"
   943→                            f"Ask the bot owner to run:\n"
   944→                            f"`hermes pairing approve {platform_name} {code}`"
   945→                        )
   946→                else:
   947→                    adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
   948→                    if adapter:
   949→                        await adapter.send(
   950→                            source.chat_id,
   951→                            "Too many pairing requests right now~ "
   952→                            "Please try again later!"
   953→                        )
   954→            return None
   955→        
   956→        # PRIORITY: If an agent is already running for this session, interrupt it
   957→        # immediately. This is before command parsing to minimize latency -- the
   958→        # user's "stop" message reaches the agent as fast as possible.
   959→        [REDACTED](source)
   960→        if _quick_key in self._running_agents:
   961→            running_agent = self._running_agents[_quick_key]
   962→            logger.debug("PRIORITY interrupt for session %s", _quick_key[:20])
   963→            running_agent.interrupt(event.text)
   964→            return None
   965→        
   966→        # Check for commands
   967→        command = event.get_command()
   968→        
   969→        # Emit command:* hook for any recognized slash command
   970→        _known_commands = {"new", "reset", "help", "status", "stop", "model",
   971→                          "personality", "retry", "undo", "sethome", "set-home",
   972→                          "compress", "usage", "insights", "reload-mcp", "reload_mcp",
   973→                          "update", "title", "resume", "provider", "rollback",
   974→                          "background", "reasoning", "autoreply"}
   975→        if command and command in _known_commands:
   976→            await self.hooks.emit(f"command:{command}", {
   977→                "platform": source.platform.value if source.platform else "",
   978→                "user_id": source.user_id,
   979→                "command": command,
   980→                "args": event.get_command_args().strip(),
   981→            })
   982→        
   983→        if command in ["new", "reset"]:
   984→            return await self._handle_reset_command(event)
   985→        
   986→        if command == "help":
   987→            return await self._handle_help_command(event)
   988→        
   989→        if command == "status":
   990→            return await self._handle_status_command(event)
   991→        
   992→        if command == "stop":
   993→            return await self._handle_stop_command(event)
   994→        
   995→        if command == "model":
   996→            return await self._handle_model_command(event)
   997→        
   998→        if command == "provider":
   999→            return await self._handle_provider_command(event)
  1000→        
  1001→        if command == "personality":
  1002→            return await self._handle_personality_command(event)
  1003→        
  1004→        if command == "retry":
  1005→            return await self._handle_retry_command(event)
  1006→        
  1007→        if command == "undo":
  1008→            return await self._handle_undo_command(event)
  1009→        
  1010→        if command in ["sethome", "set-home"]:
  1011→            return await self._handle_set_home_command(event)

1→"""Auto-reply engine — shared logic for CLI and gateway.
     2→
     3→Handles config parsing, state management, prompt building, and LLM calls.
     4→Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8→
     9→_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 20
    10→
    11→
    12→def parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    13→    """Parse /autoreply command arguments.
    14→
    15→    Returns (status_message, config_or_none).
    16→    - When enabling: returns (message, new_config_dict)
    17→    - When disabling/status/error: returns (message, None)
    18→
    19→    The caller is responsible for storing/clearing the config and
    20→    formatting the message for their platform (markdown vs plain text).
    21→    """
    22→    # /autoreply off
    23→    if args.lower() in ("off", "disable", "stop"):
    24→        return "off", None
    25→
    26→    # /autoreply max <N>
    27→    if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    28→        parts = args.split(None, 1)
    29→        if len(parts) == 2:
    30→            try:
    31→                n = int(parts[1])
    32→                if n < 1:
    33→                    return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
    34→                return f"max:{n}", None
    35→            except ValueError:
    36→                return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    37→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    38→
    39→    # /autoreply (no args)
    40→    if not args:
    41→        return "status", None
    42→
    43→    # Extract flags from args: --literal, --max N, --forever
    44→    literal = False
    45→    max_turns = _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS
    46→    remaining_parts = []
    47→
    48→    parts = args.split()
    49→    i = 0
    50→    while i < len(parts):
    51→        if parts[i] == "--literal":
    52→            literal = True
    53→        elif parts[i] == "--forever":
    54→            max_turns = 0  # 0 = unlimited
    55→        elif parts[i] == "--max" and i + 1 < len(parts):
    56→            i += 1
    57→            try:
    58→                n = int(parts[i])
    59→                if n < 1:
    60→                    return "error:--max must be at least 1.", None
    61→                max_turns = n
    62→            except ValueError:
    63→                return "error:--max requires a number.", None
    64→        else:
    65→            remaining_parts.append(parts[i])
    66→        i += 1
    67→
    68→    prompt = " ".join(remaining_parts).strip()
    69→
    70→    if literal:
    71→        if not prompt:
    72→            return "error:Usage: /autoreply --literal <message>", None
    73→        return "enabled", {
    74→            "prompt": prompt,
    75→            "model": None,
    76→            "max_turns": max_turns,
    77→            "turn_count": 0,
    78→            "literal": True,
    79→        }
    80→
    81→    if not prompt:
    82→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply <instructions>", None
    83→
    84→    return "enabled", {
    85→        "prompt": prompt,
    86→        "model": None,
    87→        "max_turns": max_turns,
    88→        "turn_count": 0,
    89→    }
    90→
    91→
    92→def build_autoreply_messages(config: Dict[str, Any],
    93→                             history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    94→    """Build the message list for the auto-reply LLM call.
    95→
    96→    Filters history to recent user/assistant messages, prepends a system
    97→    prompt with the user's instructions, and appends the generation request.
    98→    """
    99→    recent = [
   100→        m for m in (history or [])
   101→        if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   102→    ][-20:]
   103→
   104→    system_prompt = (
   105→        "You are generating a reply on behalf of the user in a conversation "
   106→        "with an AI assistant.\n\n"
   107→        "THE USER'S INSTRUCTIONS — follow these as your top priority:\n"
   108→        f"{config['prompt']}\n\n"
   109→        "Output ONLY the user's reply message. No labels, no meta-commentary, "
   110→        "no 'User:' prefix."
   111→    )
   112→
   113→    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
   114→    for m in recent:
   115→        content = m["content"]
   116→        # Flatten list-type content to plain text
   117→        if isinstance(content, list):
   118→            content = "\n".join(
   119→                p.get("text", "") for p in content
   120→                if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("type") == "text"
   121→            )
   122→        messages.append({"role": m["role"], "content": content})
   123→    messages.append({
   124→        "role": "user",
   125→        "content": "Based on the conversation above and your instructions, "
   126→                   "generate the next user reply.",
   127→    })
   128→    return messages
   129→
   130→
   131→def check_and_advance(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Tuple[Optional[str], bool]:
   132→    """Check turn cap and handle literal mode.
   133→
   134→    Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).
   135→    - (None, False): config is None / not applicable (caller didn't pass config)
   136→    - (None, True): turn cap reached, config should be removed
   137→    - (text, False): literal mode reply, turn_count incremented
   138→    - None for LLM mode: caller should proceed to call the LLM
   139→
   140→    For LLM mode, returns a sentinel to indicate the caller should proceed:
   141→    the return is (None, False) when turn_count < max_turns and not literal.
   142→    But we can't distinguish "no config" from "LLM needed" with just (None, False).
   143→
   144→    So the contract is: caller checks config existence first, then calls this.
   145→    If this returns (None, True) → cap reached. If it returns a string → use it.
   146→    If it returns (None, False) → proceed to LLM call.
   147→    """
   148→    if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
   149→        return None, True
   150→
   151→    if config.get("literal"):
   152→        config["turn_count"] += 1
   153→        return config["prompt"], False
   154→
   155→    return None, False
   156→
   157→
   158→def format_status(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   159→    """Format the auto-reply status for display."""
   160→    mode = "literal" if config.get("literal") else "LLM-generated"
   161→    label = "Message" if config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
   162→    prompt_preview = config["prompt"][:100]
   163→    if len(config["prompt"]) > 100:
   164→        prompt_preview += "..."
   165→    return (
   166→        f"Auto-reply active ({mode})\n"
   167→        f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}\n"
   168→        f"  Turns: {config['turn_count']}/{'∞' if config['max_turns'] == 0 else config['max_turns']}"
   169→    )
   170→

    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→# Image cache utilities
    38→#
    39→# When users send images on messaging platforms, we download them to a local
    40→# cache directory so they can be analyzed by the vision tool (which accepts
    41→# local file paths). This avoids issues with ephemeral platform URLs
    42→# (e.g. Telegram file URLs expire after ~1 hour).
    43→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    44→
    45→# Default location: ~/.hermes/image_cache/
    46→IMAGE_CACHE_DIR = Path(os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/image_cache"))
    47→
    48→
    49→def get_image_cache_dir() -> Path:
    50→    """Return the image cache directory, creating it if it doesn't exist."""
    51→    IMAGE_CACHE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    52→    return IMAGE_CACHE_DIR
    53→
    54→
    55→def cache_image_from_bytes(data: bytes, ext: str = ".jpg") -> str:
    56→    """
    57→    Save raw image bytes to the cache and return the absolute file path.
    58→
    59→    Args:
    60→        data: Raw image bytes.
    61→        ext:  File extension including the dot (e.g. ".jpg", ".png").
    62→
    63→    Returns:
    64→        Absolute path to the cached image file as a string.
    65→    """
    66→    cache_dir = get_image_cache_dir()
    67→    filename = f"img_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}{ext}"
    68→    filepath = cache_dir / filename
    69→    filepath.write_bytes(data)
    70→    return str(filepath)
    71→
    72→
    73→async def cache_image_from_url(url: str, ext: str = ".jpg") -> str:
    74→    """
    75→    Download an image from a URL and save it to the local cache.
    76→
    77→    Uses httpx for async download with a reasonable timeout.
    78→
    79→    Args:
    80→        url: The HTTP/HTTPS URL to download from.
    81→        ext: File extension including the dot (e.g. ".jpg", ".png").
    82→
    83→    Returns:
    84→        Absolute path to the cached image file as a string.
    85→    """
    86→    import httpx
    87→
    88→    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=30.0, follow_redirects=True) as client:
    89→        response = await client.get(
    90→            url,
    91→            headers={
    92→                "User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; HermesAgent/1.0)",
    93→                "Accept": "image/*,*/*;q=0.8",
    94→            },
    95→        )
    96→        response.raise_for_status()
    97→        return cache_image_from_bytes(response.content, ext)
    98→
    99→
   100→def cleanup_image_cache(max_age_hours: int = 24) -> int:
   101→    """
   102→    Delete cached images older than *max_age_hours*.
   103→
   104→    Returns the number of files removed.
   105→    """
   106→    import time
   107→
   108→    cache_dir = get_image_cache_dir()
   109→    cutoff = time.time() - (max_age_hours * 3600)
   110→    removed = 0
   111→    for f in cache_dir.iterdir():
   112→        if f.is_file() and f.stat().st_mtime < cutoff:
   113→            try:
   114→                f.unlink()
   115→                removed += 1
   116→            except OSError:
   117→                pass
   118→    return removed
   119→

648→    async def handle_message(self, event: MessageEvent, *, interrupt: bool = True) -> None:
   649→        """
   650→        Process an incoming message.
   651→
   652→        This method returns quickly by spawning background tasks.
   653→        This allows new messages to be processed even while an agent is running,
   654→        enabling interruption support.
   655→
   656→        If interrupt=False, the message is queued for after the current run
   657→        finishes without signalling the agent to stop (queue mode).
   658→        """
   659→        if not self._message_handler:
   660→            return
   661→
   662→        [REDACTED](event.source)
   663→
   664→        # Check if there's already an active handler for this session
   665→        if session_key in self._active_sessions:
   666→            # Store this as a pending message — picked up when current run finishes
   667→            self._pending_messages[session_key] = event
   668→            if interrupt:
   669→                print(f"[{self.name}] ⚡ New message while session {session_key} is active - triggering interrupt")
   670→                self._active_sessions[session_key].set()
   671→            else:
   672→                print(f"[{self.name}] 📬 Message queued for session {session_key} (no interrupt)")
   673→            return
   674→
   675→        # Spawn background task to process this message
   676→        asyncio.create_task(self._process_message_background(event, session_key))
   677→    
   678→    @staticmethod
   679→    def _get_human_delay() -> float:
   680→        """
   681→        Return a random delay in seconds for human-like response pacing.
   682→
   683→        Reads from env vars:
   684→          HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MODE: "off" (default) | "natural" | "custom"
   685→          HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MIN_MS: minimum delay in ms (default 800, custom mode)
   686→          HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MAX_MS: maximum delay in ms (default 2500, custom mode)
   687→        """
   688→        import random
   689→
   690→        mode = os.getenv("HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MODE", "off").lower()
   691→        if mode == "off":
   692→            return 0.0
   693→        min_ms = int(os.getenv("HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MIN_MS", "800"))
   694→        max_ms = int(os.getenv("HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MAX_MS", "2500"))
   695→        if mode == "natural":
   696→            min_ms, max_ms = 800, 2500
   697→        return random.uniform(min_ms / 1000.0, max_ms / 1000.0)
   698→
   699→    async def _process_message_background(self, event: MessageEvent, session_key: str) -> None:
   700→        """Background task that actually processes the message."""
   701→        # Create interrupt event for this session
   702→        interrupt_event = asyncio.Event()
   703→        self._active_sessions[session_key] = interrupt_event
   704→        
   705→        # Start continuous typing indicator (refreshes every 2 seconds)
   706→        _thread_metadata = {"thread_id": event.source.thread_id} if event.source.thread_id else None
   707→        typing_task = asyncio.create_task(self._keep_typing(event.source.chat_id, metadata=_thread_metadata))
   708→        
   709→        try:
   710→            # Call the handler (this can take a while with tool calls)
   711→            response = await self._message_handler(event)
   712→            
   713→            # Send response if any
   714→            if not response:
   715→                logger.warning("[%s] Handler returned empty/None response for %s", self.name, event.source.chat_id)
   716→            if response:
   717→                # Extract MEDIA:<path> tags (from TTS tool) before other processing
   718→                media_files, response = self.extract_media(response)
   719→                
   720→                # Extract image URLs and send them as native platform attachments
   721→                images, text_content = self.extract_images(response)
   722→                if images:
   723→                    logger.info("[%s] extract_images found %d image(s) in response (%d chars)", self.name, len(images), len(response))
   724→                
   725→                # Send the text portion first (if any remains after extractions)
   726→                if text_content:
   727→                    logger.info("[%s] Sending response (%d chars) to %s", self.name, len(text_content), event.source.chat_id)
   728→                    result = await self.send(
   729→                        chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
   730→                        content=text_content,
   731→                        reply_to=event.message_id,
   732→                        metadata=_thread_metadata,
   733→                    )
   734→                    
   735→                    # Log send failures (don't raise - user already saw tool progress)
   736→                    if not result.success:
   737→                        print(f"[{self.name}] Failed to send response: {result.error}")
   738→                        # Try sending without markdown as fallback
   739→                        fallback_result = await self.send(
   740→                            chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
   741→                            content=f"(Response formatting failed, plain text:)\n\n{text_content[:3500]}",
   742→                            reply_to=event.message_id,
   743→                            metadata=_thread_metadata,
   744→                        )
   745→                        if not fallback_result.success:
   746→                            print(f"[{self.name}] Fallback send also failed: {fallback_result.error}")
   747→
```

Excellent! Now I have a complete picture. Let me create a comprehensive analysis document:

## Control API & Autoreply Architecture Analysis

Based on my thorough review of the codebase, here's how the control API works and how it compares to the autoreply feature:

---

### 1. **CONTROL API: End-to-End Command Flow**

#### A. Entry Point: Control API HTTP Server
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/control_api.py`

- **Server Setup** (lines 173-183): Runs on `127.0.0.1:47823` (configurable via `HERMES_CONTROL_PORT`)
- **Routes** (lines 72-77):
  - `GET /health` — health check
  - `GET /sessions` — list running sessions
  - `GET /sessions/{key}` — get session info
  - `GET /commands` — list available commands
  - `POST /sessions/{key}/message` — **inject message** (the main mutation endpoint)

#### B. Message Injection Entry Point
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/control_api.py` (lines 129-169)

```
POST /sessions/{key}/message
├─ Parse JSON body: {"text": "...", "mode": "interrupt"|"queue"}
├─ Resolve session key (supports "_any" for first running session)
└─ Call runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=interrupt)
```

#### C. Message Injection in GatewayRunner
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (lines 292-319)

```python
async def inject_message(key, text, interrupt=True):
    1. Resolve session key from runner._running_agents
    2. Load session entry from session_store._entries
    3. Get platform adapter: adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
    4. Build synthetic MessageEvent:
       - text: injected text (can be a /command)
       - source: the session's origin source
       - message_id: prefixed with "control-{timestamp}"
    5. Call: await adapter.handle_message(event, interrupt=interrupt)
```

---

### 2. **How Messages Flow Back Into Conversation**

#### A. From Control API → Platform Adapter → GatewayRunner
The flow is fully integrated into the normal message pipeline:

```
Control API (POST /message)
  ↓
inject_message() creates synthetic MessageEvent
  ↓
adapter.handle_message(event, interrupt=interrupt)
  ↓
Platform adapter's _process_message_background()
  ↓
self._message_handler(event)  [which is GatewayRunner._handle_message]
  ↓
GatewayRunner._handle_message()
```

#### B. _handle_message() Dispatch Flow
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (lines 912-1089)

The core message pipeline:
```
1. Authorization check (_is_user_authorized)
2. PRIORITY: Check if agent running for session
   - If yes, interrupt immediately with running_agent.interrupt(event.text)
   - Return None (don't process further)
3. Extract command: command = event.get_command()
4. Emit hooks: await self.hooks.emit(f"command:{command}", {...})
5. Dispatch to handler:
   if command == "reset": await _handle_reset_command(event)
   if command == "help": await _handle_help_command(event)
   if command == "autoreply": await _handle_autoreply_command(event)
   ... (23 command handlers)
6. If no command or unknown command → Fall through to agent loop
7. Return response string (sent back through platform adapter)
```

#### C. Command Handler Pattern (Example: /autoreply)
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (lines 2022-2066)

```python
async def _handle_autoreply_command(event):
    1. Parse args: action, new_config = parse_autoreply_args(args)
    2. Handle action:
       - "off": pop config from self._autoreply_configs
       - "status": return formatted status
       - "enabled": store new config in self._autoreply_configs[session_key]
    3. Return status message immediately
```

The handler:
- **Does not** call the LLM (quick operation)
- **Returns immediately** (status/response string)
- Response is sent back via platform adapter → user sees it

---

### 3. **AUTOREPLY: Architecture & Patterns**

#### A. Autoreply Configuration Storage
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (line 278)

```python
self._autoreply_configs: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]] = {}
# Ephemeral, in-memory only. Keyed by session_key.
# Value: {"prompt": str, "model": str|None, "max_turns": int, "turn_count": int, "literal": bool}
```

#### B. Autoreply Command Handler
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (lines 2022-2066)

```
User sends: /autoreply <instructions>
↓
_handle_autoreply_command(event)
├─ Parse args (supports --literal, --max N, --forever flags)
├─ Store config: self._autoreply_configs[session_key] = {...}
└─ Return: "🔄 Auto-reply enabled..."
```

#### C. Autoreply Injection During Normal Message Flow
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (lines 1472-1620)

Within `_handle_message()` after agent runs:

```python
1. Check if this message is an autoreply:
   is_autoreply = (event.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")

2. Reset counter on real user messages (not autoreply-generated):
   if session_key in self._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
       self._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0

3. After agent response is generated (line 1616):
   if session_key in self._autoreply_configs:
       asyncio.create_task(
           self._process_autoreply(source, session_key, session_entry.session_id)
       )
```

**Key point:** Autoreply is scheduled AFTER the response is sent to the user.

#### D. Autoreply Generation and Injection
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (lines 2106-2134)

```python
async def _process_autoreply(source, session_key, session_id):
    """Fire-and-forget task: generate auto-reply and inject via adapter."""
    1. Generate reply:
       autoreply_text, cap_reached = await _generate_autoreply(session_key, session_id)
    
    2. Handle caps:
       if cap_reached:
           send: "_[Auto-reply limit reached...]_"
           return
    
    3. Build synthetic MessageEvent:
       synthetic_event = MessageEvent(
           text=autoreply_text,
           source=source,
           message_id=f"autoreply-{time.time()}",  # ← Distinguishes autoreply messages
       )
    
    4. RE-INJECT via adapter:
       await adapter.handle_message(synthetic_event)
```

This loops back through the **same platform adapter** and **same message dispatch** as user messages!

#### E. LLM Call for Autoreply
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (lines 2068-2104)

```python
async def _generate_autoreply(session_key, session_id):
    1. Check config exists
    2. Call check_and_advance(config) → handles turn counter & literal mode
    3. If literal mode: return config["prompt"], False
    4. If LLM needed:
       a. Load transcript history
       b. Build messages via build_autoreply_messages(config, history)
       c. Call async_call_llm() with:
          - task="autoreply"
          - temperature=0.7
          - max_tokens=1024
       d. Extract reply_text from response
       e. Increment turn_count
       f. Return (reply_text, False)
```

---

### 4. **Architecture Comparison: Control API vs Autoreply**

| Aspect | Control API | Autoreply |
|--------|-------------|-----------|
| **Entry Point** | HTTP POST /sessions/{key}/message | Programmatic via /autoreply command |
| **Trigger** | External tool (desloppify, scripts) | User types command or loop continues |
| **Message Construction** | Raw text passed through | Generated by auxiliary LLM or literal |
| **Dispatch** | Via `inject_message()` then adapter | Native message loop in `_handle_message()` |
| **Handler** | Standard command handlers | Scheduled via `asyncio.create_task()` |
| **Interruption** | Supports interrupt/queue modes | Implicit (runs after current response) |
| **State** | None (stateless) | Stores config in `_autoreply_configs` |
| **Message ID** | Prefixed: `"control-{timestamp}"` | Prefixed: `"autoreply-{timestamp}"` |
| **Loop Closure** | Single injection | Recursive: generates → injects → processes → generates |

---

### 5. **Key Architectural Patterns**

#### Pattern 1: Synthetic Message Events
Both mechanisms use the same core abstraction:
- Build a `MessageEvent` object
- Pass through `adapter.handle_message(event)`
- This ensures consistency — no special handling needed in main loop

**Advantages:**
- Single code path for all messages (user, control API, autoreply)
- Interruption works uniformly
- Session state tracking is unified

#### Pattern 2: Fire-and-Forget Scheduling
Both use `asyncio.create_task()` for background work:
- Control API: User gets immediate response
- Autoreply: Loop continues while generating next reply

#### Pattern 3: Command Dispatcher
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (lines 967-1048)

Simple if-elif chain, not a dynamic registry:
```python
if command in ["new", "reset"]:
    return await self._handle_reset_command(event)
if command == "help":
    return await self._handle_help_command(event)
... 23 handlers total ...
if command == "autoreply":
    return await self._handle_autoreply_command(event)
```

**Known commands list** (line 970-974) used for hook emission:
```python
_known_commands = {"new", "reset", "help", "status", "stop", "model",
                  "personality", "retry", "undo", "sethome", "set-home",
                  "compress", "usage", "insights", "reload-mcp", "reload_mcp",
                  "update", "title", "resume", "provider", "rollback",
                  "background", "reasoning", "autoreply"}
```

#### Pattern 4: Session-Level Configuration Storage
Autoreply configs stored in per-session dictionary:
```python
self._autoreply_configs[session_key] = {
    "prompt": "...",
    "model": None,
    "max_turns": 20,
    "turn_count": 0,
    "literal": False,  # optional
}
```

This is ephemeral (lost on restart) but enables per-session isolation.

---

### 6. **Control API Injection Path (Complete Flow)**

```
External Tool (desloppify)
  ↓
HermesControl client
  ↓
POST http://127.0.0.1:47823/sessions/{key}/message
  ↓
ControlAPI.send_message() handler
  ↓
runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=interrupt)
  ↓
Build synthetic MessageEvent with message_id="control-{time}"
  ↓
adapter.handle_message(event, interrupt=interrupt)
  ↓
Platform adapter queues or interrupts session
  ↓
GatewayRunner._handle_message(event)
  ↓
Command dispatch (if message starts with /)
  OR
Agent loop (normal LLM conversation)
  ↓
Response sent back to platform
  ↓
Client receives response (text message in chat)
```

---

### 7. **Autoreply Loop (Complete Flow)**

```
User: /autoreply "think step by step"
  ↓
_handle_autoreply_command() → store config
  ↓
User sends: "Hello"
  ↓
_handle_message() processes normally
  ↓
Agent generates response
  ↓
Response sent to user
  ↓
asyncio.create_task(_process_autoreply(...))  ← Fire-and-forget
  ↓
_generate_autoreply() → calls auxiliary LLM
  ↓
Build synthetic MessageEvent with message_id="autoreply-{time}"
  ↓
adapter.handle_message(synthetic_event)  ← SAME PATH as control API!
  ↓
_handle_message(synthetic_event)
  ↓
Turn counter check: is_autoreply = True, don't reset
  ↓
Agent processes autoreply message
  ↓
Response sent (labeled "[Auto-reply 1/20]: ...")
  ↓
if session_key in _autoreply_configs:
    asyncio.create_task(_process_autoreply(...))  ← LOOP!
```

---

### 8. **How Results Inject Back Into Conversation**

Both mechanisms use **adapter.handle_message()** which:

1. **Session lookup** (base.py line 662-673):
   - Check if session already active
   - If yes: store as pending, set interrupt event, return
   - If no: spawn background task

2. **Background processing** (base.py lines 699-747):
   - Call `self._message_handler(event)` → **GatewayRunner._handle_message()**
   - Gets back response string
   - Sends response to user via `adapter.send()`

3. **For control API**:
   - User sees injected message processed immediately
   - Response arrives as normal chat message
   - HTTP client can poll `/sessions/{key}` to check state

4. **For autoreply**:
   - User sees original response
   - Autoreply triggers asynchronously
   - New message is inserted into conversation as if user typed it
   - Looks like a continuous back-and-forth

---

### 9. **Key Differences in Design Philosophy**

| Control API | Autoreply |
|---|---|
| **External control** — Tools outside the agent can influence it | **Internal loop** — Agent drives its own responses |
| **Stateless** — No persistent config | **Stateful** — Tracks turn count, config |
| **Synchronous** — Returns when message queued | **Asynchronous** — Runs in background |
| **Manual** — Requires external tool to send messages | **Automatic** — Self-triggering loop |
| **Flexible** — Any text can be injected (commands, prose) | **Constrained** — Follows user instructions in config |

---

### 10. **File Locations Summary**

- **Control API**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/control_api.py`
- **Control Client**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/hermes_control_client.py`
- **Main Dispatch**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (lines 912+)
  - Command handlers: lines 1634-2130
  - Autoreply handlers: lines 2022-2134
- **Autoreply Module**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/agent/autoreply.py`
- **Platform Adapter**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/base.py` (lines 648-750)

---

This architecture is elegant because **both control API injection and autoreply loop use the exact same message pipeline** — they just differ in entry point and trigger mechanism.

    86→    import httpx
    87→
    88→    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=30.0, follow_redirects=True) as client:
    89→        response = await client.get(
    90→            url,
    91→            headers={
    92→                "User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; HermesAgent/1.0)",
    93→                "Accept": "image/*,*/*;q=0.8",
    94→            },
    95→        )
    96→        response.raise_for_status()
    97→        return cache_image_from_bytes(response.content, ext)
    98→
    99→
   100→def cleanup_image_cache(max_age_hours: int = 24) -> int:
   101→    """
   102→    Delete cached images older than *max_age_hours*.
   103→
   104→    Returns the number of files removed.
   105→    """
   106→    import time
   107→
   108→    cache_dir = get_image_cache_dir()
   109→    cutoff = time.time() - (max_age_hours * 3600)
   110→    removed = 0
   111→    for f in cache_dir.iterdir():
   112→        if f.is_file() and f.stat().st_mtime < cutoff:
   113→            try:
   114→                f.unlink()
   115→                removed += 1
   116→            except OSError:
   117→                pass
   118→    return removed
   119→
   120→
   121→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   122→# Audio cache utilities
   123→#
   124→# Same pattern as image cache -- voice messages from platforms are downloaded
   125→# here so the STT tool (OpenAI Whisper) can transcribe them from local files.
   126→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   127→
   128→AUDIO_CACHE_DIR = Path(os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/audio_cache"))
   129→
   130→
   131→def get_audio_cache_dir() -> Path:
   132→    """Return the audio cache directory, creating it if it doesn't exist."""
   133→    AUDIO_CACHE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   134→    return AUDIO_CACHE_DIR
   135→
   136→
   137→def cache_audio_from_bytes(data: bytes, ext: str = ".ogg") -> str:
   138→    """
   139→    Save raw audio bytes to the cache and return the absolute file path.
   140→
   141→    Args:
   142→        data: Raw audio bytes.
   143→        ext:  File extension including the dot (e.g. ".ogg", ".mp3").
   144→
   145→    Returns:
   146→        Absolute path to the cached audio file as a string.
   147→    """
   148→    cache_dir = get_audio_cache_dir()
   149→    filename = f"audio_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}{ext}"
   150→    filepath = cache_dir / filename
   151→    filepath.write_bytes(data)
   152→    return str(filepath)
   153→
   154→
   155→async def cache_audio_from_url(url: str, ext: str = ".ogg") -> str:
   156→    """
   157→    Download an audio file from a URL and save it to the local cache.
   158→
   159→    Args:
   160→        url: The HTTP/HTTPS URL to download from.
   161→        ext: File extension including the dot (e.g. ".ogg", ".mp3").
   162→
   163→    Returns:
   164→        Absolute path to the cached audio file as a string.
   165→    """
   166→    import httpx
   167→
   168→    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=30.0, follow_redirects=True) as client:
   169→        response = await client.get(
   170→            url,
   171→            headers={
   172→                "User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; HermesAgent/1.0)",
   173→                "Accept": "audio/*,*/*;q=0.8",
   174→            },
   175→        )
   176→        response.raise_for_status()
   177→        return cache_audio_from_bytes(response.content, ext)
   178→
   179→
   180→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   181→# Document cache utilities
   182→#
   183→# Same pattern as image/audio cache -- documents from platforms are downloaded
   184→# here so the agent can reference them by local file path.
   185→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   186→
   187→DOCUMENT_CACHE_DIR = Path(os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/document_cache"))
   188→
   189→SUPPORTED_DOCUMENT_TYPES = {
   190→    ".pdf": "application/pdf",
   191→    ".md": "text/markdown",
   192→    ".txt": "text/plain",
   193→    ".docx": "application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.wordprocessingml.document",
   194→    ".xlsx": "application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.spreadsheetml.sheet",
   195→    ".pptx": "application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.presentationml.presentation",
   196→}
   197→
   198→
   199→def get_document_cache_dir() -> Path:
   200→    """Return the document cache directory, creating it if it doesn't exist."""
   201→    DOCUMENT_CACHE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   202→    return DOCUMENT_CACHE_DIR
   203→
   204→
   205→def cache_document_from_bytes(data: bytes, filename: str) -> str:
   206→    """
   207→    Save raw document bytes to the cache and return the absolute file path.
   208→
   209→    The cached filename preserves the original human-readable name with a
   210→    unique prefix: ``doc_{uuid12}_{original_filename}``.
   211→
   212→    Args:
   213→        data: Raw document bytes.
   214→        filename: Original filename (e.g. "report.pdf").
   215→
   216→    Returns:
   217→        Absolute path to the cached document file as a string.
   218→
   219→    Raises:
   220→        ValueError: If the sanitized path escapes the cache directory.
   221→    """
   222→    cache_dir = get_document_cache_dir()
   223→    # Sanitize: strip directory components, null bytes, and control characters
   224→    safe_name = Path(filename).name if filename else "document"
   225→    safe_name = safe_name.replace("\x00", "").strip()
   226→    if not safe_name or safe_name in (".", ".."):
   227→        safe_name = "document"
   228→    cached_name = f"doc_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}_{safe_name}"
   229→    filepath = cache_dir / cached_name
   230→    # Final safety check: ensure path stays inside cache dir
   231→    if not filepath.resolve().is_relative_to(cache_dir.resolve()):
   232→        raise ValueError(f"Path traversal rejected: {filename!r}")
   233→    filepath.write_bytes(data)
   234→    return str(filepath)
   235→
   236→
   237→def cleanup_document_cache(max_age_hours: int = 24) -> int:
   238→    """
   239→    Delete cached documents older than *max_age_hours*.
   240→
   241→    Returns the number of files removed.
   242→    """
   243→    import time
   244→
   245→    cache_dir = get_document_cache_dir()
   246→    cutoff = time.time() - (max_age_hours * 3600)
   247→    removed = 0
   248→    for f in cache_dir.iterdir():
   249→        if f.is_file() and f.stat().st_mtime < cutoff:
   250→            try:
   251→                f.unlink()
   252→                removed += 1
   253→            except OSError:
   254→                pass
   255→    return removed
   256→
   257→
   258→class MessageType(Enum):
   259→    """Types of incoming messages."""
   260→    TEXT = "text"
   261→    LOCATION = "location"
   262→    PHOTO = "photo"
   263→    VIDEO = "video"
   264→    AUDIO = "audio"
   265→    VOICE = "voice"
   266→    DOCUMENT = "document"
   267→    STICKER = "sticker"
   268→    COMMAND = "command"  # /command style
   269→
   270→
   271→@dataclass
   272→class MessageEvent:
   273→    """
   274→    Incoming message from a platform.
   275→    
   276→    Normalized representation that all adapters produce.
   277→    """
   278→    # Message content
   279→    text: str
   280→    message_type: MessageType = MessageType.TEXT
   281→    
   282→    # Source information
   283→    source: SessionSource = None
   284→    
   285→    # Original platform data
   286→    raw_message: Any = None
   287→    message_id: Optional[str] = None
   288→    
   289→    # Media attachments
   290→    media_urls: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   291→    media_types: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   292→    
   293→    # Reply context
   294→    reply_to_message_id: Optional[str] = None
   295→    
   296→    # Timestamps
   297→    timestamp: datetime = field(default_factory=datetime.now)
   298→    
   299→    def is_command(self) -> bool:
   300→        """Check if this is a command message (e.g., /new, /reset)."""
   301→        return self.text.startswith("/")
   302→    
   303→    def get_command(self) -> Optional[str]:
   304→        """Extract command name if this is a command message."""
   305→        if not self.is_command():
   306→            return None
   307→        # Split on space and get first word, strip the /
   308→        parts = self.text.split(maxsplit=1)
   309→        return parts[0][1:].lower() if parts else None
   310→    
   311→    def get_command_args(self) -> str:
   312→        """Get the arguments after a command."""
   313→        if not self.is_command():
   314→            return self.text
   315→        parts = self.text.split(maxsplit=1)
   316→        return parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else ""
   317→
   318→
   319→@dataclass 
   320→class SendResult:
   321→    """Result of sending a message."""
   322→    success: bool
   323→    message_id: Optional[str] = None
   324→    error: Optional[str] = None
   325→    raw_response: Any = None
   326→
   327→
   328→# Type for message handlers
   329→MessageHandler = Callable[[MessageEvent], Awaitable[Optional[str]]]
   330→
   331→
   332→class BasePlatformAdapter(ABC):
   333→    """
   334→    Base class for platform adapters.
   335→    
   336→    Subclasses implement platform-specific logic for:
   337→    - Connecting and authenticating
   338→    - Receiving messages
   339→    - Sending messages/responses
   340→    - Handling media
   341→    """
   342→    
   343→    def __init__(self, config: PlatformConfig, platform: Platform):
   344→        self.config = config
   345→        self.platform = platform
   346→        self._message_handler: Optional[MessageHandler] = None
   347→        self._running = False
   348→        
   349→        # Track active message handlers per session for interrupt support
   350→        # Key: session_key (e.g., chat_id), Value: (event, asyncio.Event for interrupt)
   351→        self._active_sessions: Dict[str, asyncio.Event] = {}
   352→        self._pending_messages: Dict[str, MessageEvent] = {}
   353→    
   354→    @property
   355→    def name(self) -> str:
   356→        """Human-readable name for this adapter."""
   357→        return self.platform.value.title()
   358→    
   359→    @property
   360→    def is_connected(self) -> bool:
   361→        """Check if adapter is currently connected."""
   362→        return self._running
   363→    
   364→    def set_message_handler(self, handler: MessageHandler) -> None:
   365→        """
   366→        Set the handler for incoming messages.
   367→        
   368→        The handler receives a MessageEvent and should return
   369→        an optional response string.
   370→        """
   371→        self._message_handler = handler
   372→    
   373→    @abstractmethod
   374→    async def connect(self) -> bool:
   375→        """
   376→        Connect to the platform and start receiving messages.
   377→        
   378→        Returns True if connection was successful.
   379→        """
   380→        pass
   381→    
   382→    @abstractmethod
   383→    async def disconnect(self) -> None:
   384→        """Disconnect from the platform."""
   385→        pass
   386→    
   387→    @abstractmethod
   388→    async def send(
   389→        self,
   390→        chat_id: str,
   391→        content: str,
   392→        reply_to: Optional[str] = None,
   393→        metadata: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
   394→    ) -> SendResult:
   395→        """
   396→        Send a message to a chat.
   397→        
   398→        Args:
   399→            chat_id: The chat/channel ID to send to
   400→            content: Message content (may be markdown)
   401→            reply_to: Optional message ID to reply to
   402→            metadata: Additional platform-specific options
   403→        
   404→        Returns:
   405→            SendResult with success status and message ID
   406→        """
   407→        pass
   408→
   409→    async def edit_message(
   410→        self,
   411→        chat_id: str,
   412→        message_id: str,
   413→        content: str,
   414→    ) -> SendResult:
   415→        """
   416→        Edit a previously sent message. Optional — platforms that don't
   417→        support editing return success=False and callers fall back to
   418→        sending a new message.
   419→        """
   420→        return SendResult(success=False, error="Not supported")
   421→
   422→    async def send_typing(self, chat_id: str, metadata=None) -> None:
   423→        """
   424→        Send a typing indicator.
   425→        
   426→        Override in subclasses if the platform supports it.
   427→        metadata: optional dict with platform-specific context (e.g. thread_id for Slack).
   428→        """
   429→        pass
   430→    
   431→    async def send_image(
   432→        self,
   433→        chat_id: str,
   434→        image_url: str,
   435→        caption: Optional[str] = None,
   436→        reply_to: Optional[str] = None,
   437→    ) -> SendResult:
   438→        """
   439→        Send an image natively via the platform API.
   440→        
   441→        Override in subclasses to send images as proper attachments
   442→        instead of plain-text URLs. Default falls back to sending the
   443→        URL as a text message.
   444→        """
   445→        # Fallback: send URL as text (subclasses override for native images)
   446→        text = f"{caption}\n{image_url}" if caption else image_url
   447→        return await self.send(chat_id=chat_id, content=text, reply_to=reply_to)
   448→    
   449→    async def send_animation(
   450→        self,
   451→        chat_id: str,
   452→        animation_url: str,
   453→        caption: Optional[str] = None,
   454→        reply_to: Optional[str] = None,
   455→    ) -> SendResult:
   456→        """
   457→        Send an animated GIF natively via the platform API.
   458→        
   459→        Override in subclasses to send GIFs as proper animations
   460→        (e.g., Telegram send_animation) so they auto-play inline.
   461→        Default falls back to send_image.
   462→        """
   463→        return await self.send_image(chat_id=chat_id, image_url=animation_url, caption=caption, reply_to=reply_to)
   464→    
   465→    @staticmethod
   466→    def _is_animation_url(url: str) -> bool:
   467→        """Check if a URL points to an animated GIF (vs a static image)."""
   468→        lower = url.lower().split('?')[0]  # Strip query params
   469→        return lower.endswith('.gif')
   470→
   471→    @staticmethod
   472→    def extract_images(content: str) -> Tuple[List[Tuple[str, str]], str]:
   473→        """
   474→        Extract image URLs from markdown and HTML image tags in a response.
   475→        
   476→        Finds patterns like:
   477→        - ![alt text](https://example.com/image.png)
   478→        - <img src="https://example.com/image.png">
   479→        - <img src="https://example.com/image.png"></img>
   480→        
   481→        Args:
   482→            content: The response text to scan.
   483→        
   484→        Returns:
   485→            Tuple of (list of (url, alt_text) pairs, cleaned content with image tags removed).
   486→        """
   487→        images = []
   488→        cleaned = content
   489→        
   490→        # Match markdown images: ![alt](url)
   491→        md_pattern = r'!\[([^\]]*)\]\((https?://[^\s\)]+)\)'
   492→        for match in re.finditer(md_pattern, content):
   493→            alt_text = match.group(1)
   494→            url = match.group(2)
   495→            # Only extract URLs that look like actual images
   496→            if any(url.lower().endswith(ext) or ext in url.lower() for ext in
   497→                   ['.png', '.jpg', '.jpeg', '.gif', '.webp', 'fal.media', 'fal-cdn', 'replicate.delivery']):
   498→                images.append((url, alt_text))
   499→        
   500→        # Match HTML img tags: <img src="url"> or <img src="url"></img> or <img src="url"/>
   501→        html_pattern = r'<img\s+src=["\']?(https?://[^\s"\'<>]+)["\']?\s*/?>\s*(?:</img>)?'
   502→        for match in re.finditer(html_pattern, content):
   503→            url = match.group(1)
   504→            images.append((url, ""))
   505→        
   506→        # Remove only the matched image tags from content (not all markdown images)
   507→        if images:
   508→            extracted_urls = {url for url, _ in images}
   509→            def _remove_if_extracted(match):
   510→                url = match.group(2) if match.lastindex >= 2 else match.group(1)
   511→                return '' if url in extracted_urls else match.group(0)
   512→            cleaned = re.sub(md_pattern, _remove_if_extracted, cleaned)
   513→            cleaned = re.sub(html_pattern, _remove_if_extracted, cleaned)
   514→            # Clean up leftover blank lines
   515→            cleaned = re.sub(r'\n{3,}', '\n\n', cleaned).strip()
   516→        
   517→        return images, cleaned
   518→    
   519→    async def send_voice(
   520→        self,
   521→        chat_id: str,
   522→        audio_path: str,
   523→        caption: Optional[str] = None,
   524→        reply_to: Optional[str] = None,
   525→        **kwargs,
   526→    ) -> SendResult:
   527→        """
   528→        Send an audio file as a native voice message via the platform API.
   529→        
   530→        Override in subclasses to send audio as voice bubbles (Telegram)
   531→        or file attachments (Discord). Default falls back to sending the
   532→        file path as text.
   533→        """
   534→        text = f"🔊 Audio: {audio_path}"
   535→        if caption:
   536→            text = f"{caption}\n{text}"
   537→        return await self.send(chat_id=chat_id, content=text, reply_to=reply_to)
   538→
   539→    async def send_video(
   540→        self,
   541→        chat_id: str,
   542→        video_path: str,
   543→        caption: Optional[str] = None,
   544→        reply_to: Optional[str] = None,
   545→        **kwargs,
   546→    ) -> SendResult:
   547→        """
   548→        Send a video natively via the platform API.
   549→
   550→        Override in subclasses to send videos as inline playable media.
   551→        Default falls back to sending the file path as text.
   552→        """
   553→        text = f"🎬 Video: {video_path}"
   554→        if caption:
   555→            text = f"{caption}\n{text}"
   556→        return await self.send(chat_id=chat_id, content=text, reply_to=reply_to)
   557→
   558→    async def send_document(
   559→        self,
   560→        chat_id: str,
   561→        file_path: str,
   562→        caption: Optional[str] = None,
   563→        file_name: Optional[str] = None,
   564→        reply_to: Optional[str] = None,
   565→        **kwargs,
   566→    ) -> SendResult:
   567→        """
   568→        Send a document/file natively via the platform API.
   569→
   570→        Override in subclasses to send files as downloadable attachments.
   571→        Default falls back to sending the file path as text.
   572→        """
   573→        text = f"📎 File: {file_path}"
   574→        if caption:
   575→            text = f"{caption}\n{text}"
   576→        return await self.send(chat_id=chat_id, content=text, reply_to=reply_to)
   577→
   578→    async def send_image_file(
   579→        self,
   580→        chat_id: str,
   581→        image_path: str,
   582→        caption: Optional[str] = None,
   583→        reply_to: Optional[str] = None,
   584→        **kwargs,
   585→    ) -> SendResult:
   586→        """
   587→        Send a local image file natively via the platform API.
   588→
   589→        Unlike send_image() which takes a URL, this takes a local file path.
   590→        Override in subclasses for native photo attachments.
   591→        Default falls back to sending the file path as text.
   592→        """
   593→        text = f"🖼️ Image: {image_path}"
   594→        if caption:
   595→            text = f"{caption}\n{text}"
   596→        return await self.send(chat_id=chat_id, content=text, reply_to=reply_to)
   597→
   598→    @staticmethod
   599→    def extract_media(content: str) -> Tuple[List[Tuple[str, bool]], str]:
   600→        """
   601→        Extract MEDIA:<path> tags and [[audio_as_voice]] directives from response text.
   602→        
   603→        The TTS tool returns responses like:
   604→            [[audio_as_voice]]
   605→            MEDIA:/path/to/audio.ogg
   606→        
   607→        Args:
   608→            content: The response text to scan.
   609→        
   610→        Returns:
   611→            Tuple of (list of (path, is_voice) pairs, cleaned content with tags removed).
   612→        """
   613→        media = []
   614→        cleaned = content
   615→        
   616→        # Check for [[audio_as_voice]] directive
   617→        has_voice_tag = "[[audio_as_voice]]" in content
   618→        cleaned = cleaned.replace("[[audio_as_voice]]", "")
   619→        
   620→        # Extract MEDIA:<path> tags (path may contain spaces)
   621→        media_pattern = r'MEDIA:(\S+)'
   622→        for match in re.finditer(media_pattern, content):
   623→            path = match.group(1).strip()
   624→            if path:
   625→                media.append((path, has_voice_tag))
   626→        
   627→        # Remove MEDIA tags from content
   628→        if media:
   629→            cleaned = re.sub(media_pattern, '', cleaned)
   630→            cleaned = re.sub(r'\n{3,}', '\n\n', cleaned).strip()
   631→        
   632→        return media, cleaned
   633→    
   634→    async def _keep_typing(self, chat_id: str, interval: float = 2.0, metadata=None) -> None:
   635→        """
   636→        Continuously send typing indicator until cancelled.
   637→        
   638→        Telegram/Discord typing status expires after ~5 seconds, so we refresh every 2
   639→        to recover quickly after progress messages interrupt it.
   640→        """
   641→        try:
   642→            while True:
   643→                await self.send_typing(chat_id, metadata=metadata)
   644→                await asyncio.sleep(interval)
   645→        except asyncio.CancelledError:
   646→            pass  # Normal cancellation when handler completes
   647→    
   648→    async def handle_message(self, event: MessageEvent, *, interrupt: bool = True) -> None:
   649→        """
   650→        Process an incoming message.
   651→
   652→        This method returns quickly by spawning background tasks.
   653→        This allows new messages to be processed even while an agent is running,
   654→        enabling interruption support.
   655→
   656→        If interrupt=False, the message is queued for after the current run
   657→        finishes without signalling the agent to stop (queue mode).
   658→        """
   659→        if not self._message_handler:
   660→            return
   661→
   662→        [REDACTED](event.source)
   663→
   664→        # Check if there's already an active handler for this session
   665→        if session_key in self._active_sessions:
   666→            # Store this as a pending message — picked up when current run finishes
   667→            self._pending_messages[session_key] = event
   668→            if interrupt:
   669→                print(f"[{self.name}] ⚡ New message while session {session_key} is active - triggering interrupt")
   670→                self._active_sessions[session_key].set()
   671→            else:
   672→                print(f"[{self.name}] 📬 Message queued for session {session_key} (no interrupt)")
   673→            return
   674→
   675→        # Spawn background task to process this message
   676→        asyncio.create_task(self._process_message_background(event, session_key))
   677→    
   678→    @staticmethod
   679→    def _get_human_delay() -> float:
   680→        """
   681→        Return a random delay in seconds for human-like response pacing.
   682→
   683→        Reads from env vars:
   684→          HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MODE: "off" (default) | "natural" | "custom"
   685→          HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MIN_MS: minimum delay in ms (default 800, custom mode)
   686→          HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MAX_MS: maximum delay in ms (default 2500, custom mode)
   687→        """
   688→        import random
   689→
   690→        mode = os.getenv("HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MODE", "off").lower()
   691→        if mode == "off":
   692→            return 0.0
   693→        min_ms = int(os.getenv("HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MIN_MS", "800"))
   694→        max_ms = int(os.getenv("HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MAX_MS", "2500"))
   695→        if mode == "natural":
   696→            min_ms, max_ms = 800, 2500
   697→        return random.uniform(min_ms / 1000.0, max_ms / 1000.0)
   698→
   699→    async def _process_message_background(self, event: MessageEvent, session_key: str) -> None:
   700→        """Background task that actually processes the message."""
   701→        # Create interrupt event for this session
   702→        interrupt_event = asyncio.Event()
   703→        self._active_sessions[session_key] = interrupt_event
   704→        
   705→        # Start continuous typing indicator (refreshes every 2 seconds)
   706→        _thread_metadata = {"thread_id": event.source.thread_id} if event.source.thread_id else None
   707→        typing_task = asyncio.create_task(self._keep_typing(event.source.chat_id, metadata=_thread_metadata))
   708→        
   709→        try:
   710→            # Call the handler (this can take a while with tool calls)
   711→            response = await self._message_handler(event)
   712→            
   713→            # Send response if any
   714→            if not response:
   715→                logger.warning("[%s] Handler returned empty/None response for %s", self.name, event.source.chat_id)
   716→            if response:
   717→                # Extract MEDIA:<path> tags (from TTS tool) before other processing
   718→                media_files, response = self.extract_media(response)
   719→                
   720→                # Extract image URLs and send them as native platform attachments
   721→                images, text_content = self.extract_images(response)
   722→                if images:
   723→                    logger.info("[%s] extract_images found %d image(s) in response (%d chars)", self.name, len(images), len(response))
   724→                
   725→                # Send the text portion first (if any remains after extractions)
   726→                if text_content:
   727→                    logger.info("[%s] Sending response (%d chars) to %s", self.name, len(text_content), event.source.chat_id)
   728→                    result = await self.send(
   729→                        chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
   730→                        content=text_content,
   731→                        reply_to=event.message_id,
   732→                        metadata=_thread_metadata,
   733→                    )
   734→                    
   735→                    # Log send failures (don't raise - user already saw tool progress)
   736→                    if not result.success:
   737→                        print(f"[{self.name}] Failed to send response: {result.error}")
   738→                        # Try sending without markdown as fallback
   739→                        fallback_result = await self.send(
   740→                            chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
   741→                            content=f"(Response formatting failed, plain text:)\n\n{text_content[:3500]}",
   742→                            reply_to=event.message_id,
   743→                            metadata=_thread_metadata,
   744→                        )
   745→                        if not fallback_result.success:
   746→                            print(f"[{self.name}] Fallback send also failed: {fallback_result.error}")
   747→                
   748→                # Human-like pacing delay between text and media
   749→                human_delay = self._get_human_delay()
   750→                
   751→                # Send extracted images as native attachments
   752→                if images:
   753→                    logger.info("[%s] Extracted %d image(s) to send as attachments", self.name, len(images))
   754→                for image_url, alt_text in images:
   755→                    if human_delay > 0:
   756→                        await asyncio.sleep(human_delay)
   757→                    try:
   758→                        logger.info("[%s] Sending image: %s (alt=%s)", self.name, image_url[:80], alt_text[:30] if alt_text else "")
   759→                        # Route animated GIFs through send_animation for proper playback
   760→                        if self._is_animation_url(image_url):
   761→                            img_result = await self.send_animation(
   762→                                chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
   763→                                animation_url=image_url,
   764→                                caption=alt_text if alt_text else None,
   765→                                metadata=_thread_metadata,
   766→                            )
   767→                        else:
   768→                            img_result = await self.send_image(
   769→                                chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
   770→                                image_url=image_url,
   771→                                caption=alt_text if alt_text else None,
   772→                                metadata=_thread_metadata,
   773→                            )
   774→                        if not img_result.success:
   775→                            logger.error("[%s] Failed to send image: %s", self.name, img_result.error)
   776→                    except Exception as img_err:
   777→                        logger.error("[%s] Error sending image: %s", self.name, img_err, exc_info=True)
   778→                
   779→                # Send extracted media files — route by file type
   780→                _AUDIO_EXTS = {'.ogg', '.opus', '.mp3', '.wav', '.m4a'}
   781→                _VIDEO_EXTS = {'.mp4', '.mov', '.avi', '.mkv', '.3gp'}
   782→                _IMAGE_EXTS = {'.jpg', '.jpeg', '.png', '.webp', '.gif'}
   783→
   784→                for media_path, is_voice in media_files:
   785→                    if human_delay > 0:
   786→                        await asyncio.sleep(human_delay)
   787→                    try:
   788→                        ext = Path(media_path).suffix.lower()
   789→                        if ext in _AUDIO_EXTS:
   790→                            media_result = await self.send_voice(
   791→                                chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
   792→                                audio_path=media_path,
   793→                                metadata=_thread_metadata,
   794→                            )
   795→                        elif ext in _VIDEO_EXTS:
   796→                            media_result = await self.send_video(
   797→                                chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
   798→                                video_path=media_path,
   799→                                metadata=_thread_metadata,
   800→                            )
   801→                        elif ext in _IMAGE_EXTS:
   802→                            media_result = await self.send_image_file(
   803→                                chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
   804→                                image_path=media_path,
   805→                                metadata=_thread_metadata,
   806→                            )
   807→                        else:
   808→                            media_result = await self.send_document(
   809→                                chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
   810→                                file_path=media_path,
   811→                                metadata=_thread_metadata,
   812→                            )
   813→
   814→                        if not media_result.success:
   815→                            print(f"[{self.name}] Failed to send media ({ext}): {media_result.error}")
   816→                    except Exception as media_err:
   817→                        print(f"[{self.name}] Error sending media: {media_err}")
   818→            
   819→            # Check if there's a pending message that was queued during our processing
   820→            if session_key in self._pending_messages:
   821→                pending_event = self._pending_messages.pop(session_key)
   822→                print(f"[{self.name}] 📨 Processing queued message from interrupt")
   823→                # Clean up current session before processing pending
   824→                if session_key in self._active_sessions:
   825→                    del self._active_sessions[session_key]
   826→                typing_task.cancel()
   827→                try:
   828→                    await typing_task
   829→                except asyncio.CancelledError:
   830→                    pass
   831→                # Process pending message in new background task
   832→                await self._process_message_background(pending_event, session_key)
   833→                return  # Already cleaned up
   834→                
   835→        except Exception as e:
   836→            print(f"[{self.name}] Error handling message: {e}")
   837→            import traceback
   838→            traceback.print_exc()
   839→        finally:
   840→            # Stop typing indicator
   841→            typing_task.cancel()
   842→            try:
   843→                await typing_task
   844→            except asyncio.CancelledError:
   845→                pass
   846→            # Clean up session tracking
   847→            if session_key in self._active_sessions:
   848→                del self._active_sessions[session_key]
   849→    
   850→    def has_pending_interrupt(self, session_key: str) -> bool:
   851→        """Check if there's a pending interrupt for a session."""
   852→        return session_key in self._active_sessions and self._active_sessions[session_key].is_set()
   853→    
   854→    def get_pending_message(self, session_key: str) -> Optional[MessageEvent]:
   855→        """Get and clear any pending message for a session."""
   856→        return self._pending_messages.pop(session_key, None)
   857→    
   858→    def build_source(
   859→        self,
   860→        chat_id: str,
   861→        chat_name: Optional[str] = None,
   862→        chat_type: str = "dm",
   863→        user_id: Optional[str] = None,
   864→        user_name: Optional[str] = None,
   865→        thread_id: Optional[str] = None,
   866→        chat_topic: Optional[str] = None,
   867→        user_id_alt: Optional[str] = None,
   868→        chat_id_alt: Optional[str] = None,
   869→    ) -> SessionSource:
   870→        """Helper to build a SessionSource for this platform."""
   871→        # Normalize empty topic to None
   872→        if chat_topic is not None and not chat_topic.strip():
   873→            chat_topic = None
   874→        return SessionSource(
   875→            platform=self.platform,
   876→            chat_id=str(chat_id),
   877→            chat_name=chat_name,
   878→            chat_type=chat_type,
   879→            user_id=str(user_id) if user_id else None,
   880→            user_name=user_name,
   881→            thread_id=str(thread_id) if thread_id else None,
   882→            chat_topic=chat_topic.strip() if chat_topic else None,
   883→            user_id_alt=user_id_alt,
   884→            chat_id_alt=chat_id_alt,
   885→        )
   886→    
   887→    @abstractmethod
   888→    async def get_chat_info(self, chat_id: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   889→        """
   890→        Get information about a chat/channel.
   891→        
   892→        Returns dict with at least:
   893→        - name: Chat name
   894→        - type: "dm", "group", "channel"
   895→        """
   896→        pass
   897→    
   898→    def format_message(self, content: str) -> str:
   899→        """
   900→        Format a message for this platform.
   901→        
   902→        Override in subclasses to handle platform-specific formatting
   903→        (e.g., Telegram MarkdownV2, Discord markdown).
   904→        
   905→        Default implementation returns content as-is.
   906→        """
   907→        return content
   908→    
   909→    def truncate_message(self, content: str, max_length: int = 4096) -> List[str]:
   910→        """
   911→        Split a long message into chunks, preserving code block boundaries.
   912→
   913→        When a split falls inside a triple-backtick code block, the fence is
   914→        closed at the end of the current chunk and reopened (with the original
   915→        language tag) at the start of the next chunk.  Multi-chunk responses
   916→        receive indicators like ``(1/3)``.
   917→
   918→        Args:
   919→            content: The full message content
   920→            max_length: Maximum length per chunk (platform-specific)
   921→
   922→        Returns:
   923→            List of message chunks
   924→        """
   925→        if len(content) <= max_length:
   926→            return [content]
   927→
   928→        INDICATOR_RESERVE = 10   # room for " (XX/XX)"
   929→        FENCE_CLOSE = "\n```"
   930→
   931→        chunks: List[str] = []
   932→        remaining = content
   933→        # When the previous chunk ended mid-code-block, this holds the
   934→        # language tag (possibly "") so we can reopen the fence.
   935→        carry_lang: Optional[str] = None
   936→
   937→        while remaining:
   938→            # If we're continuing a code block from the previous chunk,
   939→            # prepend a new opening fence with the same language tag.
   940→            prefix = f"```{carry_lang}\n" if carry_lang is not None else ""
   941→
   942→            # How much body text we can fit after accounting for the prefix,
   943→            # a potential closing fence, and the chunk indicator.
   944→            headroom = max_length - INDICATOR_RESERVE - len(prefix) - len(FENCE_CLOSE)
   945→            if headroom < 1:
   946→                headroom = max_length // 2
   947→
   948→            # Everything remaining fits in one final chunk
   949→            if len(prefix) + len(remaining) <= max_length - INDICATOR_RESERVE:
   950→                chunks.append(prefix + remaining)
   951→                break
   952→
   953→            # Find a natural split point (prefer newlines, then spaces)
   954→            region = remaining[:headroom]
   955→            split_at = region.rfind("\n")
   956→            if split_at < headroom // 2:
   957→                split_at = region.rfind(" ")
   958→            if split_at < 1:
   959→                split_at = headroom
   960→
   961→            chunk_body = remaining[:split_at]
   962→            remaining = remaining[split_at:].lstrip()
   963→
   964→            full_chunk = prefix + chunk_body
   965→
   966→            # Walk only the chunk_body (not the prefix we prepended) to
   967→            # determine whether we end inside an open code block.
   968→            in_code = carry_lang is not None
   969→            lang = carry_lang or ""
   970→            for line in chunk_body.split("\n"):
   971→                stripped = line.strip()
   972→                if stripped.startswith("```"):
   973→                    if in_code:
   974→                        in_code = False
   975→                        lang = ""
   976→                    else:
   977→                        in_code = True
   978→                        tag = stripped[3:].strip()
   979→                        lang = tag.split()[0] if tag else ""
   980→
   981→            if in_code:
   982→                # Close the orphaned fence so the chunk is valid on its own
   983→                full_chunk += FENCE_CLOSE
   984→                carry_lang = lang
   985→            else:
   986→                carry_lang = None
   987→
   988→            chunks.append(full_chunk)
   989→
   990→        # Append chunk indicators when the response spans multiple messages
   991→        if len(chunks) > 1:
   992→            total = len(chunks)
   993→            chunks = [
   994→                f"{chunk} ({i + 1}/{total})" for i, chunk in enumerate(chunks)
   995→            ]
   996→
   997→        return chunks
   998→
```
