```
292→    async def inject_message(self, key: str, text: str, *, interrupt: bool = True):
   293→        """Inject a synthetic message into a session via its platform adapter.
   294→
   295→        Resolves the session key ('_any' picks the first running session),
   296→        builds a MessageEvent from the session's stored origin, and hands it
   297→        to the adapter's handle_message with the requested interrupt mode.
   298→        """
   299→        if key == "_any":
   300→            if not self._running_agents:
   301→                raise LookupError("No running session to inject into")
   302→            key = next(iter(self._running_agents))
   303→
   304→        self.session_store._ensure_loaded()
   305→        entry = self.session_store._entries.get(key)
   306→        if not entry or not entry.origin:
   307→            raise LookupError(f"No session found for '{key}'")
   308→
   309→        source = entry.origin
   310→        adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
   311→        if not adapter:
   312→            raise LookupError(f"No adapter for platform '{source.platform}'")
   313→
   314→        event = MessageEvent(
   315→            text=text,
   316→            source=source,
   317→            message_id=f"control-{time.time()}",
   318→        )
   319→        await adapter.handle_message(event, interrupt=interrupt)
   320→
   321→    def _get_or_create_gateway_honcho(self, session_key: str):
   322→        """Return a persistent Honcho manager/config pair for this gateway session."""
   323→        if not hasattr(self, "_honcho_managers"):
   324→            self._honcho_managers = {}
   325→        if not hasattr(self, "_honcho_configs"):
   326→            self._honcho_configs = {}
   327→
   328→        if session_key in self._honcho_managers:
   329→            return self._honcho_managers[session_key], self._honcho_configs.get(session_key)
   330→
   331→        try:
   332→            from honcho_integration.client import HonchoClientConfig, get_honcho_client
   333→            from honcho_integration.session import HonchoSessionManager
   334→
   335→            hcfg = HonchoClientConfig.from_global_config()
   336→            if not hcfg.enabled or not hcfg.api_key:
   337→                return None, hcfg
   338→
   339→            client = get_honcho_client(hcfg)
   340→            manager = HonchoSessionManager(
   341→                honcho=client,
   342→                config=hcfg,
   343→                context_tokens=hcfg.context_tokens,
   344→            )
   345→            self._honcho_managers[session_key] = manager
   346→            self._honcho_configs[session_key] = hcfg
   347→            return manager, hcfg
   348→        except Exception as e:
   349→            logger.debug("Gateway Honcho init failed for %s: %s", session_key, e)
   350→            return None, None
   351→
   352→    def _shutdown_gateway_honcho(self, session_key: str) -> None:
   353→        """Flush and close the persistent Honcho manager for a gateway session."""
   354→        managers = getattr(self, "_honcho_managers", None)
   355→        configs = getattr(self, "_honcho_configs", None)
   356→        if managers is None or configs is None:
   357→            return
   358→
   359→        manager = managers.pop(session_key, None)
   360→        configs.pop(session_key, None)
   361→        if not manager:
   362→            return
   363→        try:
   364→            manager.shutdown()
   365→        except Exception as e:
   366→            logger.debug("Gateway Honcho shutdown failed for %s: %s", session_key, e)
   367→
   368→    def _shutdown_all_gateway_honcho(self) -> None:
   369→        """Flush and close all persistent Honcho managers."""
   370→        managers = getattr(self, "_honcho_managers", None)
   371→        if not managers:
   372→            return
   373→        for session_key in list(managers.keys()):
   374→            self._shutdown_gateway_honcho(session_key)
   375→    
   376→    def _flush_memories_for_session(self, old_session_id: str):
   377→        """Prompt the agent to save memories/skills before context is lost.
   378→
   379→        Synchronous worker — meant to be called via run_in_executor from
   380→        an async context so it doesn't block the event loop.
   381→        """
   382→        try:
   383→            history = self.session_store.load_transcript(old_session_id)
   384→            if not history or len(history) < 4:
   385→                return
   386→
   387→            from run_agent import AIAgent
   388→            runtime_kwargs = _resolve_runtime_agent_kwargs()
   389→            if not runtime_kwargs.get("api_key"):
   390→                return
   391→
   392→            # Resolve model from config — AIAgent's default is OpenRouter-
   393→            # formatted ("anthropic/claude-opus-4.6") which fails when the
   394→            # active provider is openai-codex.
   395→            model = _resolve_gateway_model()
   396→
   397→            tmp_agent = AIAgent(
   398→                **runtime_kwargs,
   399→                model=model,
   400→                max_iterations=8,
   401→                quiet_mode=True,
   402→                enabled_toolsets=["memory", "skills"],
   403→                session_id=old_session_id,
   404→            )
   405→
   406→            # Build conversation history from transcript
   407→            msgs = [
   408→                {"role": m.get("role"), "content": m.get("content")}
   409→                for m in history
   410→                if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   411→            ]
   412→
   413→            # Give the agent a real turn to think about what to save
   414→            flush_prompt = (
   415→                "[System: This session is about to be automatically reset due to "
   416→                "inactivity or a scheduled daily reset. The conversation context "
   417→                "will be cleared after this turn.\n\n"
   418→                "Review the conversation above and:\n"
   419→                "1. Save any important facts, preferences, or decisions to memory "
   420→                "(user profile or your notes) that would be useful in future sessions.\n"
   421→                "2. If you discovered a reusable workflow or solved a non-trivial "
   422→                "problem, consider saving it as a skill.\n"
   423→                "3. If nothing is worth saving, that's fine — just skip.\n\n"
   424→                "Do NOT respond to the user. Just use the memory and skill_manage "
   425→                "tools if needed, then stop.]"
   426→            )
   427→
   428→            tmp_agent.run_conversation(
   429→                user_message=flush_prompt,
   430→                conversation_history=msgs,
   431→            )
   432→            logger.info("Pre-reset memory flush completed for session %s", old_session_id)
   433→            # Flush any queued Honcho writes before the session is dropped
   434→            if getattr(tmp_agent, '_honcho', None):
   435→                try:
   436→                    tmp_agent._honcho.shutdown()
   437→                except Exception:
   438→                    pass
   439→        except Exception as e:
   440→            logger.debug("Pre-reset memory flush failed for session %s: %s", old_session_id, e)
   441→
```
```

912→    async def _handle_message(self, event: MessageEvent) -> Optional[str]:
   913→        """
   914→        Handle an incoming message from any platform.
   915→        
   916→        This is the core message processing pipeline:
   917→        1. Check user authorization
   918→        2. Check for commands (/new, /reset, etc.)
   919→        3. Check for running agent and interrupt if needed
   920→        4. Get or create session

   923→        7. Return response

   927→        # Check if user is authorized
   928→        if not self._is_user_authorized(source):
   929→            logger.warning("Unauthorized user: %s (%s) on %s", source.user_id, source.user_name, source.platform.value)
   930→            # In DMs: offer pairing code. In groups: silently ignore.

   946→                else:
```

```
1→"""Auto-reply engine — shared logic for CLI and gateway.
     2→
     3→Handles config parsing, state management, prompt building, and LLM calls.
     4→Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8→
     9→_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 20
    10→
    11→
    12→def parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    13→    """Parse /autoreply command arguments.
    14→
    15→    Returns (status_message, config_or_none).
    16→    - When enabling: returns (message, new_config_dict)
    17→    - When disabling/status/error: returns (message, None)
    18→
    19→    The caller is responsible for storing/clearing the config and
    20→    formatting the message for their platform (markdown vs plain text).
    21→    """
    22→    # /autoreply off
    23→    if args.lower() in ("off", "disable", "stop"):
    24→        return "off", None
    25→
    26→    # /autoreply max <N>
    27→    if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    28→        parts = args.split(None, 1)
    29→        if len(parts) == 2:
    30→            try:
    31→                n = int(parts[1])
    32→                if n < 1:
    33→                    return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
    34→                return f"max:{n}", None
    35→            except ValueError:
    36→                return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    37→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    38→
    39→    # /autoreply (no args)
    40→    if not args:
    41→        return "status", None
    42→
    43→    # Extract flags from args: --literal, --max N, --forever
    44→    literal = False
    45→    max_turns = _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS
    46→    remaining_parts = []
    47→
    48→    parts = args.split()
    49→    i = 0
    50→    while i < len(parts):
    51→        if parts[i] == "--literal":
    52→            literal = True
    53→        elif parts[i] == "--forever":
    54→            max_turns = 0  # 0 = unlimited
    55→        elif parts[i] == "--max" and i + 1 < len(parts):
    56→            i += 1
    57→            try:
    58→                n = int(parts[i])
    59→                if n < 1:
    60→                    return "error:--max must be at least 1.", None
    61→                max_turns = n
    62→            except ValueError:
    63→                return "error:--max requires a number.", None
    64→        else:
    65→            remaining_parts.append(parts[i])
    66→        i += 1
    67→
    68→    prompt = " ".join(remaining_parts).strip()
    69→
    70→    if literal:
    71→        if not prompt:
    72→            return "error:Usage: /autoreply --literal <message>", None
    73→        return "enabled", {
    74→            "prompt": prompt,
    75→            "model": None,
    76→            "max_turns": max_turns,
    77→            "turn_count": 0,
    78→            "literal": True,
    79→        }
    80→
    81→    if not prompt:
    82→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply <instructions>", None
    83→
    84→    return "enabled", {
    85→        "prompt": prompt,
    86→        "model": None,
    87→        "max_turns": max_turns,
    88→        "turn_count": 0,
    89→    }
    90→
    91→
    92→def build_autoreply_messages(config: Dict[str, Any],
    93→                             history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    94→    """Build the message list for the auto-reply LLM call.
    95→
    96→    Filters history to recent user/assistant messages, prepends a system
    97→    prompt with the user's instructions, and appends the generation request.
    98→    """
    99→    recent = [
   100→        m for m in (history or [])
   101→        if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   102→    ][-20:]
   103→
   104→    system_prompt = (
   105→        "You are generating a reply on behalf of the user in a conversation "
   106→        "with an AI assistant.\n\n"
   107→        "THE USER'S INSTRUCTIONS — follow these as your top priority:\n"
   108→        f"{config['prompt']}\n\n"
   109→        "Output ONLY the user's reply message. No labels, no meta-commentary, "
   110→        "no 'User:' prefix."
   111→    )
   112→
   113→    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
   114→    for m in recent:
   115→        content = m["content"]
   116→        # Flatten list-type content to plain text
   117→        if isinstance(content, list):
   118→            content = "\n".join(
   119→                p.get("text", "") for p in content
   120→                if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("type") == "text"
   121→            )
   122→        messages.append({"role": m["role"], "content": content})
   123→    messages.append({
   124→        "role": "user",
   125→        "content": "Based on the conversation above and your instructions, "
   126→                   "generate the next user reply.",
   127→    })
   128→    return messages
   129→
   130→
   131→def check_and_advance(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Tuple[Optional[str], bool]:
   132→    """Check turn cap and handle literal mode.
   133→
   134→    Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).
   135→    - (None, False): config is None / not applicable (caller didn't pass config)
   136→    - (None, True): turn cap reached, config should be removed
   137→    - (text, False): literal mode reply, turn_count incremented
   138→    - None for LLM mode: caller should proceed to call the LLM
   139→
   140→    For LLM mode, returns a sentinel to indicate the caller should proceed:
   141→    the return is (None, False) when turn_count < max_turns and not literal.
   142→    But we can't distinguish "no config" from "LLM needed" with just (None, False).
   143→
   144→    So the contract is: caller checks config existence first, then calls this.
   145→    If this returns (None, True) → cap reached. If it returns a string → use it.
   146→    If it returns (None, False) → proceed to LLM call.
   147→    """
   148→    if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
   149→        return None, True
   150→
   151→    if config.get("literal"):
   152→        config["turn_count"] += 1
   153→        return config["prompt"], False
   154→
   155→    return None, False
   156→
   157→
   158→def format_status(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   159→    """Format the auto-reply status for display."""
   160→    mode = "literal" if config.get("literal") else "LLM-generated"
   161→    label = "Message" if config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
   162→    prompt_preview = config["prompt"][:100]
   163→    if len(config["prompt"]) > 100:
   164→        prompt_preview += "..."
   165→    return (
   166→        f"Auto-reply active ({mode})\n"
   167→        f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}\n"
   168→        f"  Turns: {config['turn_count']}/{'∞' if config['max_turns'] == 0 else config['max_turns']}"
   169→    )
   170→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Local-only HTTP control API for the Hermes gateway.
     3→
     4→Runs alongside the gateway as a background aiohttp server on 127.0.0.1.
     5→Provides programmatic control over live agent sessions — session listing,
     6→message injection, etc.
     7→
     8→External tools (e.g., desloppify) can call these endpoints to influence
     9→a running Hermes agent without needing direct process access.
    10→
    11→All mutations go through POST /sessions/{key}/message — inject any text
    12→(including /commands) as if the user typed it.
    13→
    14→Default port: 47823 (override with HERMES_CONTROL_PORT env var).
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import logging
    19→import os
    20→
    21→from aiohttp import web
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    26→
    27→# Commands available via /message injection.  This list is informational —
    28→# the message endpoint accepts any text, but GET /commands exposes these
    29→# so external tools know what's available.
    30→AVAILABLE_COMMANDS = [
    31→    {"command": "/reset", "description": "Reset conversation history"},
    32→    {"command": "/new", "description": "Start a new conversation"},
    33→    {"command": "/stop", "description": "Stop the running agent"},
    34→    {"command": "/compact", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    35→    {"command": "/compress", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    36→    {"command": "/status", "description": "Show session info"},
    37→    {"command": "/model <provider:model>", "description": "Switch model"},
    38→    {"command": "/personality <name>", "description": "Switch personality"},
    39→    {"command": "/autoreply <instructions>", "description": "Enable auto-reply loop"},
    40→    {"command": "/autoreply off", "description": "Disable auto-reply loop"},
    41→    {"command": "/reasoning <level>", "description": "Set reasoning effort"},
    42→    {"command": "/rollback [number]", "description": "List or restore checkpoints"},
    43→    {"command": "/background <prompt>", "description": "Run prompt in background session"},
    44→    {"command": "/undo", "description": "Undo last assistant response"},
    45→    {"command": "/retry", "description": "Retry last message"},
    46→    {"command": "/usage", "description": "Show token usage"},
    47→    {"command": "/help", "description": "List commands"},
    48→]
    49→
    50→
    51→def _get_port() -> int:
    52→    return int(os.getenv("HERMES_CONTROL_PORT", str(DEFAULT_PORT)))
    53→
    54→
    55→def _json_response(data: dict, status: int = 200) -> web.Response:
    56→    return web.Response(
    57→        text=json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False),
    58→        content_type="application/json",
    59→        status=status,
    60→    )
    61→
    62→
    63→class ControlAPI:
    64→    """Lightweight control API that holds a reference to the GatewayRunner."""
    65→
    66→    def __init__(self, gateway_runner):
    67→        self.runner = gateway_runner
    68→        self.app = web.Application()
    69→        self._setup_routes()
    70→        self._site = None
    71→
    72→    def _setup_routes(self):
    73→        self.app.router.add_get("/health", self.health)
    74→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions", self.list_sessions)
    75→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions/{key}", self.get_session)
    76→        self.app.router.add_get("/commands", self.list_commands)
    77→        self.app.router.add_post("/sessions/{key}/message", self.send_message)
    78→
    79→    # ── Helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    80→
    81→    def _resolve_agent(self, key: str):
    82→        """Look up a running agent by session key.  '_any' returns the first.
    83→        Returns (resolved_key, agent) or (key, None) if not found."""
    84→        if key == "_any":
    85→            if not self.runner._running_agents:
    86→                return key, None
    87→            key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
    88→        return key, self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
    89→
    90→    # ── Endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    async def health(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    93→        return _json_response({"status": "ok"})
    94→
    95→    async def list_sessions(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    96→        """List all sessions, marking which have a live (running) agent."""
    97→        sessions = []
    98→        for key, agent in self.runner._running_agents.items():
    99→            entry = {
   100→                "session_key": key,
   101→                "status": "running",
   102→                "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   103→                "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   104→                **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   105→            }
   106→            sessions.append(entry)
   107→        return _json_response({"sessions": sessions})
   108→
   109→    async def get_session(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   110→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   111→        key, agent = self._resolve_agent(key)
   112→        if not agent:
   113→            return _json_response(
   114→                {"error": f"No running agent for '{key}'"},
   115→                status=404,
   116→            )
   117→        return _json_response({
   118→            "session_key": key,
   119→            "status": "running",
   120→            "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   121→            "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   122→            **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   123→        })
   124→
   125→    async def list_commands(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   126→        """List available slash commands that can be sent via /message."""
   127→        return _json_response({"commands": AVAILABLE_COMMANDS})
   128→
   129→    async def send_message(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   130→        """Inject a message (or /command) into a session.
   131→
   132→        POST body: {"text": "...", "mode": "interrupt"|"queue"}
   133→
   134→        Modes:
   135→        - interrupt (default): cancel current agent run, process this message
   136→        - queue: let current run finish, then process this message next
   137→        """
   138→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   139→
   140→        try:
   141→            body = await request.json()
   142→        except Exception:
   143→            return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
   144→
   145→        text = body.get("text", "").strip()
   146→        if not text:
   147→            return _json_response({"error": "'text' is required"}, status=400)
   148→
   149→        mode = body.get("mode", "interrupt")
   150→        if mode not in ("interrupt", "queue"):
   151→            return _json_response(
   152→                {"error": f"Invalid mode '{mode}', must be 'interrupt' or 'queue'"},
   153→                status=400,
   154→            )
   155→
   156→        logger.info(
   157→            "Control API: send_message session=%s mode=%s text=%s",
   158→            key, mode, text[:80],
   159→        )
   160→
   161→        try:
   162→            result = self.runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=(mode != "queue"))
   163→            # await if coroutine (GatewayRunner is async, CLI shim is sync)
   164→            if hasattr(result, "__await__"):
   165→                await result
   166→        except LookupError as e:
   167→            return _json_response({"error": str(e)}, status=404)
   168→
   169→        return _json_response({"success": True, "message": "Message submitted"})
   170→
   171→    # ── Lifecycle ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   172→
   173→    async def start(self):
   174→        port = _get_port()
   175→        app_runner = web.AppRunner(self.app, access_log=None)
   176→        await app_runner.setup()
   177→        self._site = web.TCPSite(app_runner, "127.0.0.1", port)
   178→        try:
   179→            await self._site.start()
   180→            logger.info("Control API listening on http://127.0.0.1:%d", port)
   181→            self._write_port_file(port)
   182→        except OSError as e:
   183→            logger.warning("Control API failed to bind port %d: %s", port, e)
   184→
   185→    async def stop(self):
   186→        if self._site:
   187→            await self._site.stop()
   188→            self._remove_port_file()
   189→            logger.info("Control API stopped")
   190→
   191→    @staticmethod
   192→    def _write_port_file(port: int):
   193→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   194→        try:
   195→            with open(path, "w") as f:
   196→                f.write(str(port))
   197→        except OSError:
   198→            pass
   199→
   200→    @staticmethod
   201→    def _remove_port_file():
   202→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   203→        try:
   204→            os.remove(path)
   205→        except OSError:
   206→            pass
   207→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Hermes control client — lightweight module for external tools to control
     3→a running Hermes instance (CLI or gateway).
     4→
     5→Usage:
     6→    from gateway.hermes_control_client import HermesControl
     7→
     8→    ctl = HermesControl()  # auto-discovers port from ~/.hermes/control_api.port
     9→
    10→    # Send a message (interrupt mode — stops current work)
    11→    ctl.send_message("Focus on this instead")
    12→
    13→    # Queue a message (processed after current work finishes)
    14→    ctl.send_message("When you're done, also check logs", mode="queue")
    15→
    16→    # Send a slash command
    17→    ctl.send_message("/reset")
    18→    ctl.send_message("/model openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4")
    19→
    20→    # List running sessions
    21→    sessions = ctl.list_sessions()
    22→
    23→    # List available commands
    24→    commands = ctl.list_commands()
    25→
    26→Zero dependencies beyond stdlib (uses urllib, not requests/aiohttp).
    27→"""
    28→
    29→import json
    30→import os
    31→import urllib.request
    32→import urllib.error
    33→from typing import Optional
    34→
    35→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    36→PORT_FILE = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
    37→
    38→
    39→class HermesControl:
    40→    """Client for the Hermes control API."""
    41→
    42→    def __init__(self, host: str = "127.0.0.1", port: Optional[int] = None):
    43→        if port is None:
    44→            port = self._discover_port()
    45→        self.base_url = f"http://{host}:{port}"
    46→
    47→    @staticmethod
    48→    def _discover_port() -> int:
    49→        try:
    50→            with open(PORT_FILE) as f:
    51→                return int(f.read().strip())
    52→        except (OSError, ValueError):
    53→            return DEFAULT_PORT
    54→
    55→    def _request(self, method: str, path: str, body: dict = None) -> dict:
    56→        url = f"{self.base_url}{path}"
    57→        data = json.dumps(body).encode() if body else None
    58→        req = urllib.request.Request(
    59→            url,
    60→            data=data,
    61→            method=method,
    62→            headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"} if data else {},
    63→        )
    64→        try:
    65→            with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=5) as resp:
    66→                return json.loads(resp.read())
    67→        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
    68→            return json.loads(e.read())
    69→        except urllib.error.URLError as e:
    70→            return {"error": f"Cannot reach Hermes control API: {e.reason}"}
    71→
    72→    # ── Read-only endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────
    73→
    74→    def health(self) -> dict:
    75→        """Check if the control API is running."""
    76→        return self._request("GET", "/health")
    77→
    78→    def list_sessions(self) -> dict:
    79→        """List all running agent sessions."""
    80→        return self._request("GET", "/sessions")
    81→
    82→    def get_session(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> dict:
    83→        """Get info about a specific session."""
    84→        return self._request("GET", f"/sessions/{session_key}")
    85→
    86→    def list_commands(self) -> dict:
    87→        """List available slash commands."""
    88→        return self._request("GET", "/commands")
    89→
    90→    # ── Message injection ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    def send_message(
    93→        self,
    94→        text: str,
    95→        session_key: str = "_any",
    96→        mode: str = "interrupt",
    97→    ) -> dict:
    98→        """Send a message (or /command) into a running session.
    99→
   100→        Args:
   101→            text: Message text or slash command (e.g., "hello", "/reset").
   102→            session_key: Session to target. "_any" targets the first running session.
   103→            mode: "interrupt" (default) stops current work; "queue" waits for it to finish.
   104→        """
   105→        return self._request("POST", f"/sessions/{session_key}/message", {
   106→            "text": text,
   107→            "mode": mode,
   108→        })
   109→
   110→    # ── Convenience methods ────────────────────────────────────────────
   111→
   112→    def switch_model(self, provider: str, model: str, **kw) -> dict:
   113→        """Switch model via /model command."""
   114→        return self.send_message(f"/model {provider}:{model}", **kw)
   115→
   116→    def compact_context(self, **kw) -> dict:
   117→        """Trigger context compaction."""
   118→        return self.send_message("/compact", **kw)
   119→
   120→    def reset(self, **kw) -> dict:
   121→        """Reset conversation history."""
   122→        return self.send_message("/reset", **kw)
   123→
   124→    def stop(self, **kw) -> dict:
   125→        """Stop the running agent."""
   126→        return self.send_message("/stop", **kw)
   127→
   128→    def is_autoreply_enabled(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> bool:
   129→        """Check if autoreply is enabled for a session."""
   130→        info = self.get_session(session_key)
   131→        return bool(info.get("autoreply", {}).get("enabled"))
   132→
   133→    def is_available(self) -> bool:
   134→        """Check if a Hermes instance with control API is reachable."""
   135→        try:
   136→            return self.health().get("status") == "ok"
   137→        except Exception:
   138→            return False
   139→
```

```
2068→    async def _generate_autoreply(self, session_key: str, session_id: str):
  2069→        """Generate an auto-reply using the auxiliary LLM client.
  2070→
  2071→        Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).  reply_text is None when
  2072→        auto-reply is inactive or the turn cap was just hit.
  2073→        """
  2074→        from agent.autoreply import check_and_advance, build_autoreply_messages
  2075→
  2076→        config = self._autoreply_configs.get(session_key)
  2077→        if not config:
  2078→            return None, False
  2079→
  2080→        text, cap_reached = check_and_advance(config)
  2081→        if cap_reached:
  2082→            del self._autoreply_configs[session_key]
  2083→            return None, True
  2084→        if text:
  2085→            return text, False
  2086→
  2087→        # LLM-generated: build prompt from transcript and call async LLM
  2088→        history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_id)
  2089→        messages = build_autoreply_messages(config, history)
  2090→
  2091→        from agent.auxiliary_client import async_call_llm
  2092→        call_kwargs = {
  2093→            "task": "autoreply",
  2094→            "messages": messages,
  2095→            "temperature": 0.7,
  2096→            "max_tokens": 1024,
  2097→        }
  2098→        if config.get("model"):
  2099→            call_kwargs["model"] = config["model"]
  2100→
  2101→        response = await async_call_llm(**call_kwargs)
  2102→        reply_text = response.choices[0].message.content.strip()
  2103→        config["turn_count"] += 1
  2104→        return reply_text, False
  2105→
  2106→    async def _process_autoreply(self, source, session_key: str, session_id: str):
  2107→        """Fire-and-forget task: generate an auto-reply and inject it via the adapter."""
  2108→        try:
  2109→            adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
  2110→            if not adapter:
  2111→                return
  2112→
  2113→            autoreply_text, cap_reached = await self._generate_autoreply(
  2114→                session_key, session_id)
  2115→
  2116→            if not autoreply_text:
  2117→                if cap_reached:
  2118→                    metadata = {"thread_id": source.thread_id} if source.thread_id else None
  2119→                    await adapter.send(
  2120→                        chat_id=source.chat_id,
  2121→                        content="_[Auto-reply limit reached. Use `/autoreply` to re-enable.]_",
  2122→                        metadata=metadata,
  2123→                    )
  2124→                return
  2125→

  2136→        """Handle /retry command - re-send the last user message."""
  2137→        source = event.source
  2138→        session_entry = self.session_store.get_or_create_session(source)
  2139→        history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_entry.session_id)
  2140→        
  2141→        # Find the last user message

  2143→        last_user_idx = None
  2144→        for i in range(len(history) - 1, -1, -1):
  2145→            if history[i].get("role") == "user":
  2146→                last_user_msg = history[i].get("content", "")
  2147→                last_user_idx = i
  2148→                break
  2149→        
  2150→        if not last_user_msg:
  2151→            return "No previous message to retry."
  2152→        
  2153→        # Truncate history to before the last user message and persist
  2154→        truncated = history[:last_user_idx]
  2155→        self.session_store.rewrite_transcript(session_entry.session_id, truncated)
  2156→        # Reset stored token count — transcript was truncated
  2157→        session_entry.last_prompt_tokens = 0
  2158→        
  2159→        # Re-send by creating a fake text event with the old message
  2160→        retry_event = MessageEvent(
  2161→            text=last_user_msg,
  2162→            message_type=MessageType.TEXT,
  2163→            source=source,
  2164→            raw_message=event.raw_message,
  2165→        )
  2166→        
  2167→        # Let the normal message handler process it
```

```
commit 894f4a96886785e948b61e35123ed7d8a7ca62b1
Author: POM <[REDACTED]>
Date:   Sun Mar 15 17:23:23 2026 +0100

    refactor: flatten control queue to 3 methods, remove indirection
    
    The control system was 5 layers deep: HTTP endpoint → execute_control →
    _run_control_handler → _ctrl_* wrapper → actual method. Flattened to:
    
    - execute_control: single entry point, handles immediate vs queue
    - _drain_control_queue: processes deferred commands from the queue
    - _run_ctrl: calls handler, prints notification — 3 lines
    
    Removed enqueue_control (folded into execute_control), removed
    _run_control_handler (replaced by _run_ctrl). Handler methods are
    unchanged — they're the only layer that should exist since they
    normalize JSON kwargs to method calls.
```

```
296-        builds a MessageEvent from the session's stored origin, and hands it
```

```
1→"""
     2→Local-only HTTP control API for the Hermes gateway.
     3→
     4→Runs alongside the gateway as a background aiohttp server on 127.0.0.1.
     5→Provides programmatic control over live agent sessions — session listing,
     6→message injection, etc.
     7→
     8→External tools (e.g., desloppify) can call these endpoints to influence
     9→a running Hermes agent without needing direct process access.
    10→
    11→All mutations go through POST /sessions/{key}/message — inject any text
    12→(including /commands) as if the user typed it.
    13→
    14→Default port: 47823 (override with HERMES_CONTROL_PORT env var).
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import logging
    19→import os
    20→
    21→from aiohttp import web
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    26→
    27→# Commands available via /message injection.  This list is informational —
    28→# the message endpoint accepts any text, but GET /commands exposes these
    29→# so external tools know what's available.
    30→AVAILABLE_COMMANDS = [
    31→    {"command": "/reset", "description": "Reset conversation history"},
    32→    {"command": "/new", "description": "Start a new conversation"},
    33→    {"command": "/stop", "description": "Stop the running agent"},
    34→    {"command": "/compact", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    35→    {"command": "/compress", "description": "Compress conversation context"},
    36→    {"command": "/status", "description": "Show session info"},
    37→    {"command": "/model <provider:model>", "description": "Switch model"},
    38→    {"command": "/personality <name>", "description": "Switch personality"},
    39→    {"command": "/autoreply <instructions>", "description": "Enable auto-reply loop"},
    40→    {"command": "/autoreply off", "description": "Disable auto-reply loop"},
    41→    {"command": "/reasoning <level>", "description": "Set reasoning effort"},
    42→    {"command": "/rollback [number]", "description": "List or restore checkpoints"},
    43→    {"command": "/background <prompt>", "description": "Run prompt in background session"},
    44→    {"command": "/undo", "description": "Undo last assistant response"},
    45→    {"command": "/retry", "description": "Retry last message"},
    46→    {"command": "/usage", "description": "Show token usage"},
    47→    {"command": "/help", "description": "List commands"},
    48→]
    49→
    50→
    51→def _get_port() -> int:
    52→    return int(os.getenv("HERMES_CONTROL_PORT", str(DEFAULT_PORT)))
    53→
    54→
    55→def _json_response(data: dict, status: int = 200) -> web.Response:
    56→    return web.Response(
    57→        text=json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False),
    58→        content_type="application/json",
    59→        status=status,
    60→    )
    61→
    62→
    63→class ControlAPI:
    64→    """Lightweight control API that holds a reference to the GatewayRunner."""
    65→
    66→    def __init__(self, gateway_runner):
    67→        self.runner = gateway_runner
    68→        self.app = web.Application()
    69→        self._setup_routes()
    70→        self._site = None
    71→
    72→    def _setup_routes(self):
    73→        self.app.router.add_get("/health", self.health)
    74→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions", self.list_sessions)
    75→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions/{key}", self.get_session)
    76→        self.app.router.add_get("/commands", self.list_commands)
    77→        self.app.router.add_post("/sessions/{key}/message", self.send_message)
    78→
    79→    # ── Helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    80→
    81→    def _resolve_agent(self, key: str):
    82→        """Look up a running agent by session key.  '_any' returns the first.
    83→        Returns (resolved_key, agent) or (key, None) if not found."""
    84→        if key == "_any":
    85→            if not self.runner._running_agents:
    86→                return key, None
    87→            key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
    88→        return key, self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
    89→
    90→    # ── Endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    async def health(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    93→        return _json_response({"status": "ok"})
    94→
    95→    async def list_sessions(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    96→        """List all sessions, marking which have a live (running) agent."""
    97→        sessions = []
    98→        for key, agent in self.runner._running_agents.items():
    99→            entry = {
   100→                "session_key": key,
   101→                "status": "running",
   102→                "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   103→                "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   104→                **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   105→            }
   106→            sessions.append(entry)
   107→        return _json_response({"sessions": sessions})
   108→
   109→    async def get_session(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   110→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   111→        key, agent = self._resolve_agent(key)
   112→        if not agent:
   113→            return _json_response(
   114→                {"error": f"No running agent for '{key}'"},
   115→                status=404,
   116→            )
   117→        return _json_response({
   118→            "session_key": key,
   119→            "status": "running",
   120→            "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   121→            "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   122→            **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   123→        })
   124→
   125→    async def list_commands(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   126→        """List available slash commands that can be sent via /message."""
   127→        return _json_response({"commands": AVAILABLE_COMMANDS})
   128→
   129→    async def send_message(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   130→        """Inject a message (or /command) into a session.
   131→
   132→        POST body: {"text": "...", "mode": "interrupt"|"queue"}
   133→
   134→        Modes:
   135→        - interrupt (default): cancel current agent run, process this message
   136→        - queue: let current run finish, then process this message next
   137→        """
   138→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   139→
   140→        try:
   141→            body = await request.json()
   142→        except Exception:
   143→            return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
   144→
   145→        text = body.get("text", "").strip()
   146→        if not text:
   147→            return _json_response({"error": "'text' is required"}, status=400)
   148→
   149→        mode = body.get("mode", "interrupt")
   150→        if mode not in ("interrupt", "queue"):
   151→            return _json_response(
   152→                {"error": f"Invalid mode '{mode}', must be 'interrupt' or 'queue'"},
   153→                status=400,
   154→            )
   155→
   156→        logger.info(
   157→            "Control API: send_message session=%s mode=%s text=%s",
   158→            key, mode, text[:80],
   159→        )
   160→
   161→        try:
   162→            result = self.runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=(mode != "queue"))
   163→            # await if coroutine (GatewayRunner is async, CLI shim is sync)
   164→            if hasattr(result, "__await__"):
   165→                await result
   166→        except LookupError as e:
   167→            return _json_response({"error": str(e)}, status=404)
   168→
   169→        return _json_response({"success": True, "message": "Message submitted"})
   170→
   171→    # ── Lifecycle ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   172→
   173→    async def start(self):
   174→        port = _get_port()
   175→        app_runner = web.AppRunner(self.app, access_log=None)
   176→        await app_runner.setup()
   177→        self._site = web.TCPSite(app_runner, "127.0.0.1", port)
   178→        try:
   179→            await self._site.start()
   180→            logger.info("Control API listening on http://127.0.0.1:%d", port)
   181→            self._write_port_file(port)
   182→        except OSError as e:
   183→            logger.warning("Control API failed to bind port %d: %s", port, e)
   184→
   185→    async def stop(self):
   186→        if self._site:
   187→            await self._site.stop()
   188→            self._remove_port_file()
   189→            logger.info("Control API stopped")
   190→
   191→    @staticmethod
   192→    def _write_port_file(port: int):
   193→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   194→        try:
   195→            with open(path, "w") as f:
   196→                f.write(str(port))
   197→        except OSError:
   198→            pass
   199→
   200→    @staticmethod
   201→    def _remove_port_file():
   202→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   203→        try:
   204→            os.remove(path)
   205→        except OSError:
   206→            pass
```

```
    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→# Image cache utilities
    38→#
    39→# When users send images on messaging platforms, we download them to a local
    40→# cache directory so they can be analyzed by the vision tool (which accepts
    41→# local file paths). This avoids issues with ephemeral platform URLs
    42→# (e.g. Telegram file URLs expire after ~1 hour).
    43→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    44→
    45→# Default location: ~/.hermes/image_cache/
    46→IMAGE_CACHE_DIR = Path(os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/image_cache"))
    47→
    48→
    49→def get_image_cache_dir() -> Path:
    50→    """Return the image cache directory, creating it if it doesn't exist."""
    51→    IMAGE_CACHE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    52→    return IMAGE_CACHE_DIR
    53→
    54→
    55→def cache_image_from_bytes(data: bytes, ext: str = ".jpg") -> str:
    56→    """
    57→    Save raw image bytes to the cache and return the absolute file path.
    58→
    59→    Args:
    60→        data: Raw image bytes.
    61→        ext:  File extension including the dot (e.g. ".jpg", ".png").
    62→
    63→    Returns:
    64→        Absolute path to the cached image file as a string.
    65→    """
    66→    cache_dir = get_image_cache_dir()
    67→    filename = f"img_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}{ext}"
    68→    filepath = cache_dir / filename
    69→    filepath.write_bytes(data)
    70→    return str(filepath)
    71→
    72→
    73→async def cache_image_from_url(url: str, ext: str = ".jpg") -> str:
    74→    """
    75→    Download an image from a URL and save it to the local cache.
    76→
    77→    Uses httpx for async download with a reasonable timeout.
    78→
    79→    Args:
    80→        url: The HTTP/HTTPS URL to download from.
    81→        ext: File extension including the dot (e.g. ".jpg", ".png").
    82→
    83→    Returns:
    84→        Absolute path to the cached image file as a string.
    85→    """
    86→    import httpx
    87→
    88→    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=30.0, follow_redirects=True) as client:
    89→        response = await client.get(
    90→            url,
    91→            headers={
    92→                "User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; HermesAgent/1.0)",
    93→                "Accept": "image/*,*/*;q=0.8",
    94→            },
    95→        )
    96→        response.raise_for_status()
    97→        return cache_image_from_bytes(response.content, ext)
    98→
    99→
   100→def cleanup_image_cache(max_age_hours: int = 24) -> int:
   101→    """
   102→    Delete cached images older than *max_age_hours*.
   103→
   104→    Returns the number of files removed.
   105→    """
   106→    import time
   107→
   108→    cache_dir = get_image_cache_dir()
   109→    cutoff = time.time() - (max_age_hours * 3600)
   110→    removed = 0
   111→    for f in cache_dir.iterdir():
   112→        if f.is_file() and f.stat().st_mtime < cutoff:
   113→            try:
   114→                f.unlink()
   115→                removed += 1
   116→            except OSError:
   117→                pass
   118→    return removed
   119→
   120→
```

```
    29→def _make_event(text="/autoreply", platform=Platform.TELEGRAM,
    30→                user_id="12345", chat_id="67890", message_id=None):
    31→    """Build a MessageEvent for testing."""
    32→    source = _make_source(platform, user_id, chat_id)
    33→    return MessageEvent(text=text, source=source, message_id=message_id)
    34→
    35→
    36→def _make_runner():
    37→    """Create a bare GatewayRunner with minimal mocks."""
    38→    from gateway.run import GatewayRunner
    39→    runner = object.__new__(GatewayRunner)
    40→    runner.adapters = {}
    41→    runner._session_db = None
    42→    runner._reasoning_config = None
    43→    runner._provider_routing = {}
    44→    runner._fallback_model = None
    45→    runner._running_agents = {}
    46→    runner._pending_approvals = {}
    47→    runner._autoreply_configs = {}
    48→    runner._honcho_managers = {}
    49→    runner._honcho_configs = {}
    50→
    51→    mock_store = MagicMock()
    52→    mock_store.get_or_create_session.return_value = MagicMock(
    53→        session_id="sess_123",
    54→        session_key="agent:main:telegram:dm:12345",
    55→        created_at=MagicMock(strftime=lambda f: "2026-01-01 00:00"),
    56→        updated_at=MagicMock(strftime=lambda f: "2026-01-01 00:00"),
    57→        total_tokens=0,
    58→        last_prompt_tokens=0,
    59→        was_auto_reset=False,
    60→    )
    61→    mock_store._generate_session_key.return_value = "agent:main:telegram:dm:12345"
    62→    mock_store.load_transcript.return_value = []
    63→    runner.session_store = mock_store
    64→
    65→    from gateway.hooks import HookRegistry
    66→    runner.hooks = HookRegistry()
    67→
    68→    return runner
    69→

    76→class TestHandleAutoreplyCommand:
    77→    """Tests for GatewayRunner._handle_autoreply_command."""
    78→
    79→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
    80→    async def test_no_args_shows_inactive_status(self):
    81→        runner = _make_runner()
    82→        event = _make_event(text="/autoreply")
    83→        result = await runner._handle_autoreply_command(event)
    84→        assert "not active" in result.lower()
    85→
    86→    @pytest.mark.asyncio

    88→        runner = _make_runner()
    89→        [REDACTED](_make_source())

    91→            "prompt": "Ask follow-up questions",

    93→            "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS,
    94→            "turn_count": 3,
    95→        }
    96→        event = _make_event(text="/autoreply")
    97→        result = await runner._handle_autoreply_command(event)
    98→        assert "active" in result.lower()
    99→        assert f"3/{_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS}" in result
   100→        assert "Ask follow-up" in result

   247→
   248→class TestGenerateAutoreply:
   249→    """Tests for GatewayRunner._generate_autoreply."""
   250→
   251→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   252→    async def test_returns_none_when_not_configured(self):
   253→        runner = _make_runner()
   254→        text, cap = await runner._generate_autoreply("no_such_key", "sess_123")
   255→        assert text is None
   256→        assert cap is False

   258→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   259→    async def test_returns_none_and_cap_when_limit_reached(self):
   260→        runner = _make_runner()
   261→        runner._autoreply_configs["key"] = {
   262→            "prompt": "test", "model": None, "max_turns": 5, "turn_count": 5,
   263→        }
   264→        text, cap = await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   265→        assert text is None
   266→        assert cap is True
   267→        assert "key" not in runner._autoreply_configs
   268→
   269→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   270→    async def test_literal_mode_skips_llm(self):
   271→        """Literal mode returns the prompt directly without calling the LLM."""
   272→        runner = _make_runner()
   273→        runner._autoreply_configs["key"] = {
   274→            "prompt": "continue", "model": None,
   275→            "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 0, "literal": True,
   276→        }
   277→        # No LLM mock needed — literal mode shouldn't call it
   278→        text, cap = await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   279→        assert text == "continue"
   280→        assert cap is False
   281→        assert runner._autoreply_configs["key"]["turn_count"] == 1
   282→
   283→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   284→    async def test_generates_reply_and_increments_counter(self):
   285→        runner = _make_runner()
   286→        runner._autoreply_configs["key"] = {
   287→            "prompt": "Ask follow-up questions",
   288→            "model": None,
   289→            "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS,
   290→            "turn_count": 2,
   291→        }
   292→        runner.session_store.load_transcript.return_value = [
   293→            {"role": "user", "content": "Tell me about Python"},
   294→            {"role": "assistant", "content": "Python is a programming language."},
   295→        ]
   296→
   297→        mock_response = MagicMock()
   298→        mock_response.choices = [MagicMock()]
   299→        mock_response.choices[0].message.content = "What about its type system?"
   300→
   301→        with patch("agent.auxiliary_client.async_call_llm", return_value=mock_response) as mock_llm:
   302→            text, cap = await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   303→
   304→        assert text == "What about its type system?"
   305→        assert cap is False
   306→        assert runner._autoreply_configs["key"]["turn_count"] == 3
   307→
   308→        # Verify the LLM was called with task="autoreply"
   309→        call_kwargs = mock_llm.call_args[1]
   310→        assert call_kwargs["task"] == "autoreply"
   311→        assert call_kwargs["temperature"] == 0.7
   312→        # System prompt should contain the user's instructions
   313→        system_msg = call_kwargs["messages"][0]
   314→        assert system_msg["role"] == "system"
   315→        assert "Ask follow-up questions" in system_msg["content"]
   316→
   317→    @pytest.mark.asyncio
   318→    async def test_flattens_list_content(self):
   319→        """Messages with list-type content are flattened to text."""
   320→        runner = _make_runner()
   321→        runner._autoreply_configs["key"] = {
   322→            "prompt": "Continue", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 0,
   323→        }
   324→        runner.session_store.load_transcript.return_value = [
   325→            {"role": "user", "content": [
   326→                {"type": "text", "text": "Look at this image"},
   327→                {"type": "image_url", "image_url": {"url": "http://example.com/img.png"}},
   328→            ]},
   329→            {"role": "assistant", "content": "I see the image."},
   330→        ]
   331→
   332→        mock_response = MagicMock()
   333→        mock_response.choices = [MagicMock()]
   334→        mock_response.choices[0].message.content = "What else can you tell me?"
   335→
   336→        with patch("agent.auxiliary_client.async_call_llm", return_value=mock_response) as mock_llm:
   337→            await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   338→
   339→        # The user message should have been flattened to text only
   340→        messages = mock_llm.call_args[1]["messages"]
   341→        user_msg = [m for m in messages if m["role"] == "user" and "Look at" in m.get("content", "")]
   342→        assert len(user_msg) == 1
   343→        assert user_msg[0]["content"] == "Look at this image"
   344→
   345→    @pytest.mark.asyncio

   351→        }

   357→        mock_response = MagicMock()

   360→
   361→        with patch("agent.auxiliary_client.async_call_llm", return_value=mock_response) as mock_llm:
   362→            await runner._generate_autoreply("key", "sess_123")
   363→
   364→        assert mock_llm.call_args[1]["model"] == "openai/gpt-4o-mini"

   375→    @pytest.mark.asyncio

   498→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   499→# Turn counter reset & auto-reply label in _handle_message
   500→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   501→
   502→
   503→class TestAutoreplyInHandleMessage:
   504→    """Tests for auto-reply wiring in _handle_message.
   505→
   506→    These test the auto-reply logic directly rather than going through the
   507→    full _handle_message pipeline, which requires too much setup. The
   508→    individual methods (_generate_autoreply, _process_autoreply) are
   509→    thoroughly tested above; here we verify the wiring code itself.
   510→    """
   511→
   512→    def test_autoreply_message_detected_by_message_id(self):
   513→        """Messages with autoreply- prefix in message_id are detected."""
   514→        event = _make_event(text="test", message_id="autoreply-123.456")
   515→        assert (event.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   516→
   517→        event2 = _make_event(text="test", message_id="regular-123")
   518→        assert not (event2.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   519→
   520→    def test_turn_counter_reset_logic(self):
   521→        """Real messages reset counter; autoreply messages don't."""
   522→        runner = _make_runner()
   523→        session_key = "agent:main:telegram:dm:12345"
   524→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key] = {
   525→            "prompt": "test", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 7,
   526→        }
   527→
   528→        # Real message — should reset
   529→        is_autoreply = ("regular-msg" or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   530→        if session_key in runner._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
   531→            runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
   532→        assert runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] == 0
   533→
   534→        # Restore and test autoreply message — should NOT reset
   535→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 7
   536→        is_autoreply = ("autoreply-123.456" or "").startswith("autoreply-")
   537→        if session_key in runner._autoreply_configs and not is_autoreply:
   538→            runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] = 0
   539→        assert runner._autoreply_configs[session_key]["turn_count"] == 7
   540→
   541→    def test_response_labeling_logic(self):
   542→        """Auto-reply responses get labeled with turn count."""
   543→        runner = _make_runner()
   544→        session_key = "test_key"
   545→        runner._autoreply_configs[session_key] = {
   546→            "prompt": "test", "model": None, "max_turns": _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, "turn_count": 3,
   547→        }
   548→
   549→        # Simulate the labeling code from _handle_message
   550→        is_autoreply = True
   551→        response = "Agent response here"
   552→        event_text = "Generated question"
   553→
   554→        if is_autoreply and response:
   555→            ar_config = runner._autoreply_configs.get(session_key)
   556→            if ar_config:
   557→                response = (
   558→                    f"**[Auto-reply {ar_config['turn_count']}/{ar_config['max_turns']}]:** "
   559→                    f"{event_text}\n\n---\n\n{response}"
   560→                )
   561→
   562→        assert f"**[Auto-reply 3/{_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS}]:**" in response
   563→        assert "Generated question" in response
   564→        assert "Agent response here" in response
   565→
   566→    def test_no_label_for_regular_messages(self):
   567→        """Regular messages are not labeled."""
   568→        response = "Agent response"
   569→        is_autoreply = False
   570→
   571→        if is_autoreply and response:
   572→            response = "SHOULD NOT HAPPEN"
   573→
   574→        assert response == "Agent response"
   575→
   576→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Hermes control client — lightweight module for external tools to control
     3→a running Hermes instance (CLI or gateway).
     4→
     5→Usage:
     6→    from gateway.hermes_control_client import HermesControl
     7→
     8→    ctl = HermesControl()  # auto-discovers port from ~/.hermes/control_api.port
     9→
    10→    # Send a message (interrupt mode — stops current work)
    11→    ctl.send_message("Focus on this instead")
    12→
    13→    # Queue a message (processed after current work finishes)
    14→    ctl.send_message("When you're done, also check logs", mode="queue")
    15→
    16→    # Send a slash command
    17→    ctl.send_message("/reset")
    18→    ctl.send_message("/model openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4")
    19→
    20→    # List running sessions
    21→    sessions = ctl.list_sessions()
    22→
    23→    # List available commands
    24→    commands = ctl.list_commands()
    25→
    26→Zero dependencies beyond stdlib (uses urllib, not requests/aiohttp).
    27→"""
    28→
    29→import json
    30→import os
    31→import urllib.request
    32→import urllib.error
    33→from typing import Optional
    34→
    35→DEFAULT_PORT = 47823
    36→PORT_FILE = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
    37→
    38→
    39→class HermesControl:
    40→    """Client for the Hermes control API."""
    41→
    42→    def __init__(self, host: str = "127.0.0.1", port: Optional[int] = None):
    43→        if port is None:
    44→            port = self._discover_port()
    45→        self.base_url = f"http://{host}:{port}"
    46→
    47→    @staticmethod
    48→    def _discover_port() -> int:
    49→        try:
    50→            with open(PORT_FILE) as f:
    51→                return int(f.read().strip())
    52→        except (OSError, ValueError):
    53→            return DEFAULT_PORT
    54→
    55→    def _request(self, method: str, path: str, body: dict = None) -> dict:
    56→        url = f"{self.base_url}{path}"
    57→        data = json.dumps(body).encode() if body else None
    58→        req = urllib.request.Request(
    59→            url,
    60→            data=data,
    61→            method=method,
    62→            headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"} if data else {},
    63→        )
    64→        try:
    65→            with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=5) as resp:
    66→                return json.loads(resp.read())
    67→        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
    68→            return json.loads(e.read())
    69→        except urllib.error.URLError as e:
    70→            return {"error": f"Cannot reach Hermes control API: {e.reason}"}
    71→
    72→    # ── Read-only endpoints ────────────────────────────────────────────
    73→
    74→    def health(self) -> dict:
    75→        """Check if the control API is running."""
    76→        return self._request("GET", "/health")
    77→
    78→    def list_sessions(self) -> dict:
    79→        """List all running agent sessions."""
    80→        return self._request("GET", "/sessions")
    81→
    82→    def get_session(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> dict:
    83→        """Get info about a specific session."""
    84→        return self._request("GET", f"/sessions/{session_key}")
    85→
    86→    def list_commands(self) -> dict:
    87→        """List available slash commands."""
    88→        return self._request("GET", "/commands")
    89→
    90→    # ── Message injection ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→    def send_message(
    93→        self,
    94→        text: str,
    95→        session_key: str = "_any",
    96→        mode: str = "interrupt",
    97→    ) -> dict:
    98→        """Send a message (or /command) into a running session.
    99→
   100→        Args:
   101→            text: Message text or slash command (e.g., "hello", "/reset").
   102→            session_key: Session to target. "_any" targets the first running session.
   103→            mode: "interrupt" (default) stops current work; "queue" waits for it to finish.
   104→        """
   105→        return self._request("POST", f"/sessions/{session_key}/message", {
   106→            "text": text,
   107→            "mode": mode,
   108→        })
   109→
   110→    # ── Convenience methods ────────────────────────────────────────────
   111→
   112→    def switch_model(self, provider: str, model: str, **kw) -> dict:
   113→        """Switch model via /model command."""
   114→        return self.send_message(f"/model {provider}:{model}", **kw)
   115→
   116→    def compact_context(self, **kw) -> dict:
   117→        """Trigger context compaction."""
   118→        return self.send_message("/compact", **kw)
   119→
   120→    def reset(self, **kw) -> dict:
   121→        """Reset conversation history."""
   122→        return self.send_message("/reset", **kw)
   123→
   124→    def stop(self, **kw) -> dict:
   125→        """Stop the running agent."""
   126→        return self.send_message("/stop", **kw)
   127→
   128→    def is_autoreply_enabled(self, session_key: str = "_any") -> bool:
   129→        """Check if autoreply is enabled for a session."""
   130→        info = self.get_session(session_key)
   131→        return bool(info.get("autoreply", {}).get("enabled"))
   132→
   133→    def is_available(self) -> bool:
   134→        """Check if a Hermes instance with control API is reachable."""
   135→        try:
   136→            return self.health().get("status") == "ok"
   137→        except Exception:
   138→            return False
   139→
```

```
1752→    async def _handle_model_command(self, event: MessageEvent) -> str:
  1753→        """Handle /model command - show or change the current model."""
  1754→        import yaml
  1755→        from hermes_cli.models import (
  1756→            parse_model_input,
  1757→            validate_requested_model,
  1758→            curated_models_for_provider,
  1759→            normalize_provider,
  1760→            _PROVIDER_LABELS,
  1761→        )
  1762→
  1763→        args = event.get_command_args().strip()
  1764→        config_path = _hermes_home / 'config.yaml'
  1765→
  1766→        # Resolve current model and provider from config
  1767→        current = os.getenv("HERMES_MODEL") or "anthropic/claude-opus-4.6"
  1768→        current_provider = "openrouter"
  1769→        try:
  1770→            if config_path.exists():
  1771→                with open(config_path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
  1772→                    cfg = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
  1773→                model_cfg = cfg.get("model", {})
  1774→                if isinstance(model_cfg, str):
  1775→                    current = model_cfg
  1776→                elif isinstance(model_cfg, dict):
  1777→                    current = model_cfg.get("default", current)
  1778→                    current_provider = model_cfg.get("provider", current_provider)
  1779→        except Exception:
  1780→            pass
  1781→
  1782→        # Resolve "auto" to the actual provider using credential detection
  1783→        current_provider = normalize_provider(current_provider)
  1784→        if current_provider == "auto":
  1785→            try:
  1786→                from hermes_cli.auth import resolve_provider as _resolve_provider
  1787→                current_provider = _resolve_provider(current_provider)
  1788→            except Exception:
  1789→                current_provider = "openrouter"
  1790→
  1791→        # Detect custom endpoint: provider resolved to openrouter but a custom
  1792→        # base URL is configured — the user set up a custom endpoint.
  1793→        if current_provider == "openrouter" and os.getenv("OPENAI_BASE_URL", "").strip():
  1794→            current_provider = "custom"
  1795→
  1796→        if not args:
  1797→            provider_label = _PROVIDER_LABELS.get(current_provider, current_provider)
  1798→            lines = [
  1799→                f"🤖 **Current model:** `{current}`",
  1800→                f"**Provider:** {provider_label}",
  1801→                "",
  1802→            ]
  1803→            curated = curated_models_for_provider(current_provider)
  1804→            if curated:
  1805→                lines.append(f"**Available models ({provider_label}):**")
  1806→                for mid, desc in curated:
  1807→                    marker = " ←" if mid == current else ""
  1808→                    label = f"  _{desc}_" if desc else ""
  1809→                    lines.append(f"• `{mid}`{label}{marker}")
  1810→                lines.append("")
  1811→            lines.append("To change: `/model model-name`")
  1812→            lines.append("Switch provider: `/model provider:model-name`")
  1813→            return "\n".join(lines)
  1814→
  1815→        # Parse provider:model syntax
  1816→        target_provider, new_model = parse_model_input(args, current_provider)
  1817→        provider_changed = target_provider != current_provider
  1818→
  1819→        # Resolve credentials for the target provider (for API probe)
  1820→        api_key = os.getenv("OPENROUTER_API_KEY") or os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY") or ""
  1821→        base_url = "https://openrouter.ai/api/v1"
  1822→        if provider_changed:
  1823→            try:
  1824→                from hermes_cli.runtime_provider import resolve_runtime_provider
  1825→                runtime = resolve_runtime_provider(requested=target_provider)
  1826→                api_key = runtime.get("api_key", "")
  1827→                base_url = runtime.get("base_url", "")
  1828→            except Exception as e:
  1829→                provider_label = _PROVIDER_LABELS.get(target_provider, target_provider)
  1830→                return f"⚠️ Could not resolve credentials for provider '{provider_label}': {e}"
  1831→        else:
  1832→            # Use current provider's base_url from config or registry
  1833→            try:
  1834→                from hermes_cli.runtime_provider import resolve_runtime_provider
  1835→                runtime = resolve_runtime_provider(requested=current_provider)
  1836→                api_key = runtime.get("api_key", "")
  1837→                base_url = runtime.get("base_url", "")
  1838→            except Exception:
  1839→                pass
  1840→
  1841→        # Validate the model against the live API
  1842→        try:
  1843→            validation = validate_requested_model(
  1844→                new_model,
  1845→                target_provider,
  1846→                api_key=api_key,
  1847→                base_url=base_url,
  1848→            )
  1849→        except Exception:
  1850→            validation = {"accepted": True, "persist": True, "recognized": False, "message": None}
  1851→
  1852→        if not validation.get("accepted"):
  1853→            msg = validation.get("message", "Invalid model")
  1854→            tip = "\n\nUse `/model` to see available models, `/provider` to see providers" if "Did you mean" not in msg else ""
  1855→            return f"⚠️ {msg}{tip}"
  1856→
  1857→        # Persist to config only if validation approves
  1858→        if validation.get("persist"):
  1859→            try:
  1860→                user_config = {}
  1861→                if config_path.exists():
  1862→                    with open(config_path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
  1863→                        user_config = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
  1864→                if "model" not in user_config or not isinstance(user_config["model"], dict):
  1865→                    user_config["model"] = {}
  1866→                user_config["model"]["default"] = new_model
  1867→                if provider_changed:
  1868→                    user_config["model"]["provider"] = target_provider
  1869→                with open(config_path, 'w', encoding="utf-8") as f:
  1870→                    yaml.dump(user_config, f, default_flow_style=False, sort_keys=False)
  1871→            except Exception as e:
  1872→                return f"⚠️ Failed to save model change: {e}"
  1873→
  1874→        # Set env vars so the next agent run picks up the change
  1875→        os.environ["HERMES_MODEL"] = new_model
  1876→        if provider_changed:
  1877→            os.environ["HERMES_INFERENCE_PROVIDER"] = target_provider
  1878→
  1879→        provider_label = _PROVIDER_LABELS.get(target_provider, target_provider)
  1880→        provider_note = f"\n**Provider:** {provider_label}" if provider_changed else ""
  1881→
  1882→        warning = ""
  1883→        if validation.get("message"):
  1884→            warning = f"\n⚠️ {validation['message']}"
  1885→
  1886→        if validation.get("persist"):
  1887→            persist_note = "saved to config"
  1888→        else:
  1889→            persist_note = "this session only — will revert on restart"
  1890→        return f"🤖 Model changed to `{new_model}` ({persist_note}){provider_note}{warning}\n_(takes effect on next message)_"
  1891→
```

```
diff --git a/commits.md b/commits.md

--- a/commits.md
+++ b/commits.md
@@ -232,3 +232,31 @@
 **Good:** Fixes a real bug found during live testing. Two-line fix, clear cause and effect.
 
 **Bad:** This should have been caught in the original control queue commit (`8a7ce57`). The pre-loop drain was deliberately left argument-free with the rationale that "compact can't fire there (no messages passed)" — but that reasoning was wrong once the queue became externally accessible. External callers can queue compact at any time.
+
+---
+
+## `65eb67f` — feat: add interrupt, get_status, flush_memories control commands
+
+**What it does:** Registers three more control handlers: `interrupt` (break agent loop, immediate), `get_status` (return model/session/state, immediate), `flush_memories` (save pending memories, queued).
+
+**Good:** All three are useful for external tools. `interrupt` and `get_status` are correctly marked immediate since they don't need message context. `flush_memories` correctly queues because it needs the messages list.
+
+**Bad:** Nothing.
+
+---
+
+## `3166462` + `894f4a9` — refactor: flatten control queue structure
+
+**What these do:** Two commits that should have been one. `3166462` renamed `_handle_ctrl_*` → `_ctrl_*` and trimmed docstrings. `894f4a9` (2 minutes later) restructured the dispatch: merged `enqueue_control` into `execute_control`, replaced `_run_control_handler` with 3-line `_run_ctrl`, added a block comment documenting the convention.
+
+**Good (final state):**
+- Call chain is now: `execute_control` → `_run_ctrl` → `_ctrl_*` → actual method. Clean.
+- Single entry point (`execute_control`) for both immediate and deferred.
+- `_drain_control_queue` builds a `ctx` dict for loop context instead of threading 3 kwargs through every call.
+- Convention is documented in one block comment rather than repeated per-handler.
+
+**Bad:**
+- Two commits for one logical change. `3166462` was immediately superseded.
+- The control system had been restructured ~4 times across the session (handler dict → external flag → immediate flag → notification wrapper → flatten). The final state is good but took too many iterations to get there.
+
+**Action taken:** None — final state is clean.
```

```
1→"""Automatic context window compression for long conversations.
     2→
     3→Self-contained class with its own OpenAI client for summarization.
     4→Uses Gemini Flash (cheap/fast) to summarize middle turns while
     5→protecting head and tail context.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→import logging
     9→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
    10→
    11→from agent.auxiliary_client import call_llm
    12→from agent.model_metadata import (
    13→    get_model_context_length,
    14→    estimate_messages_tokens_rough,
    15→)
    16→
    17→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    18→
    19→
    20→class ContextCompressor:
    21→    """Compresses conversation context when approaching the model's context limit.
    22→
    23→    Algorithm: protect first N + last N turns, summarize everything in between.
    24→    Token tracking uses actual counts from API responses for accuracy.
    25→    """
    26→
    27→    def __init__(
    28→        self,
    29→        model: str,
    30→        threshold_percent: float = 0.50,
    31→        protect_first_n: int = 3,
    32→        protect_last_n: int = 4,
    33→        summary_target_tokens: int = 2500,
    34→        quiet_mode: bool = False,
    35→        summary_model_override: str = None,
    36→        base_url: str = "",
    37→    ):
    38→        self.model = model
    39→        self.base_url = base_url
    40→        self.threshold_percent = threshold_percent
    41→        self.protect_first_n = protect_first_n
    42→        self.protect_last_n = protect_last_n
    43→        self.summary_target_tokens = summary_target_tokens
    44→        self.quiet_mode = quiet_mode
    45→
    46→        self.context_length = get_model_context_length(model, base_url=base_url)
    47→        self.threshold_tokens = int(self.context_length * threshold_percent)
    48→        self.compression_count = 0
    49→        self._context_probed = False  # True after a step-down from context error
    50→
    51→        self.last_prompt_tokens = 0
    52→        self.last_completion_tokens = 0
    53→        self.last_total_tokens = 0
    54→
    55→        self.summary_model = summary_model_override or ""
    56→
    57→    def update_from_response(self, usage: Dict[str, Any]):
    58→        """Update tracked token usage from API response."""
    59→        self.last_prompt_tokens = usage.get("prompt_tokens", 0)
    60→        self.last_completion_tokens = usage.get("completion_tokens", 0)
    61→        self.last_total_tokens = usage.get("total_tokens", 0)
    62→
    63→    def should_compress(self, prompt_tokens: int = None) -> bool:
    64→        """Check if context exceeds the compression threshold."""
    65→        tokens = prompt_tokens if prompt_tokens is not None else self.last_prompt_tokens
    66→        return tokens >= self.threshold_tokens
    67→
    68→    def should_compress_preflight(self, messages: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
    69→        """Quick pre-flight check using rough estimate (before API call)."""
    70→        rough_estimate = estimate_messages_tokens_rough(messages)
    71→        return rough_estimate >= self.threshold_tokens
    72→
    73→    def get_status(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    74→        """Get current compression status for display/logging."""
    75→        return {
    76→            "last_prompt_tokens": self.last_prompt_tokens,
    77→            "threshold_tokens": self.threshold_tokens,
    78→            "context_length": self.context_length,
    79→            "usage_percent": (self.last_prompt_tokens / self.context_length * 100) if self.context_length else 0,
    80→            "compression_count": self.compression_count,
    81→        }
    82→
    83→    def _generate_summary(self, turns_to_summarize: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> Optional[str]:
    84→        """Generate a concise summary of conversation turns.
    85→
    86→        Tries the auxiliary model first, then falls back to the user's main
    87→        model.  Returns None if all attempts fail — the caller should drop
    88→        the middle turns without a summary rather than inject a useless
    89→        placeholder.
    90→        """
    91→        parts = []
    92→        for msg in turns_to_summarize:
    93→            role = msg.get("role", "unknown")
    94→            content = msg.get("content") or ""
    95→            if len(content) > 2000:
    96→                content = content[:1000] + "\n...[truncated]...\n" + content[-500:]
    97→            tool_calls = msg.get("tool_calls", [])
    98→            if tool_calls:
    99→                tool_names = [tc.get("function", {}).get("name", "?") for tc in tool_calls if isinstance(tc, dict)]
   100→                content += f"\n[Tool calls: {', '.join(tool_names)}]"
```
