    72→}
    73→
    74→# Fallback: unknown/custom models get zero cost (we can't assume pricing
    75→# for self-hosted models, custom OAI endpoints, local inference, etc.)
    76→_DEFAULT_PRICING = {"input": 0.0, "output": 0.0}
    77→
    78→
    79→def _has_known_pricing(model_name: str) -> bool:
    80→    """Check if a model has known pricing (vs unknown/custom endpoint)."""
    81→    return _get_pricing(model_name) is not _DEFAULT_PRICING
    82→
    83→
    84→def _get_pricing(model_name: str) -> Dict[str, float]:
    85→    """Look up pricing for a model. Uses fuzzy matching on model name.
    86→
    87→    Returns _DEFAULT_PRICING (zero cost) for unknown/custom models —
    88→    we can't assume costs for self-hosted endpoints, local inference, etc.
    89→    """
    90→    if not model_name:
    91→        return _DEFAULT_PRICING
    92→
    93→    # Strip provider prefix (e.g., "anthropic/claude-..." -> "claude-...")
    94→    bare = model_name.split("/")[-1].lower()
    95→
    96→    # Exact match first
    97→    if bare in MODEL_PRICING:
    98→        return MODEL_PRICING[bare]
    99→
   100→    # Fuzzy prefix match — prefer the LONGEST matching key to avoid
```

2753→    def execute_control(self, command: str, **params) -> dict:
  2754→        """Run a control command. Immediate commands execute now; others queue."""
  2755→        entry = self._control_handlers.get(command)
  2756→        if not entry:
  2757→            return {"success": False, "error": f"Unknown command: '{command}'"}
  2758→        if not entry.get("immediate"):
  2759→            with self._control_lock:
  2760→                self._control_queue.append({"command": command, **params})
  2761→            return {"success": True, "queued": True,
  2762→                    "message": f"'{command}' queued — will apply on next turn"}
  2763→        try:
  2764→            self._run_ctrl(entry["fn"], **params)
  2765→            return {"success": True, "message": f"'{command}' executed"}
  2766→        except Exception as e:
  2767→            logging.error("Control command '%s' failed: %s", command, e)
  2768→            return {"success": False, "error": str(e)}
  2769→
  2770→    def _drain_control_queue(self, messages: list = None,
  2771→                             system_message: str = None, task_id: str = "default"):
  2772→        """Process queued (deferred) commands. Called at safe points in the loop."""
  2773→        with self._control_lock:
  2774→            pending = list(self._control_queue)
  2775→            self._control_queue.clear()
  2776→        ctx = dict(messages=messages, system_message=system_message, task_id=task_id)
  2777→        for cmd in pending:
  2778→            name = cmd.get("command")
  2779→            entry = self._control_handlers.get(name)
  2780→            if not entry:
  2781→                logging.warning("Unknown control command: %s", name)
  2782→                continue
  2783→            params = {k: v for k, v in cmd.items() if k != "command"}
  2784→            try:
  2785→                self._run_ctrl(entry["fn"], **ctx, **params)
  2786→            except Exception as e:
  2787→                logging.error("Control command '%s' failed: %s", name, e)
  2788→
  2789→    def _run_ctrl(self, fn, **kwargs):
  2790→        """Call a handler and print its notification (if any)."""
  2791→        result = fn(**kwargs)
  2792→        if result is not None:
  2793→            print(f"{self.log_prefix}⚙️  {result}")
  2794→

# Handlers marked immediate=True execute in the caller's thread (no queuing);
        # non-immediate handlers queue and drain when the agent loop is active.
        # All handlers are exposed via the HTTP control API unless external=False.
        self._control_queue = collections.deque()
        self._control_lock = threading.Lock()
        self._control_handlers = {
            "switch_model":    {"fn": self._ctrl_switch_model, "immediate": True},
            "compact_context": {"fn": self._ctrl_compact},
            "interrupt":       {"fn": self._ctrl_interrupt, "immediate": True},
            "get_status":      {"fn": self._ctrl_get_status, "immediate": True},
            "flush_memories":  {"fn": self._ctrl_flush_memories},
        }
        
        # Subagent delegation state
        self._delegate_depth = 0        # 0 = top-level agent, incremented for children
        self._active_children = []      # Running child AIAgents (for interrupt propagation)
```

commit 8a7ce57e747e68f852c82883495cbee3c5ab66aa
Author: POM <[REDACTED]>
Date:   Sun Mar 15 15:10:00 2026 +0100

    refactor: replace _pending_model_switch with generic control queue
    
    Replace the single-purpose _pending_model_switch field with an
    extensible control queue (deque + lock + handler dispatch). External
    callers enqueue commands; the agent loop drains them at safe points.
    
    Fixes:
    - Stale active_system_prompt after compact-via-drain: the local
      variable wasn't refreshed after _drain_control_queue mutated
      _cached_system_prompt through _compress_context.
    - Remove redundant _cached_system_prompt write in compact handler —
      _compress_context already sets it internally (line 3209).

## COMPREHENSIVE ANALYSIS: Message Injection and Dispatch Patterns

Based on my thorough review of the codebase, here's what I found:

### 1. **`inject_message()` Method - Full Signature and Behavior**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` lines 292-319

**Signature:**
```python
async def inject_message(self, key: str, text: str, *, interrupt: bool = True)
```

**What it does:**
- Injects a synthetic message into a running session via its platform adapter
- Resolves the session key (supports `"_any"` to pick the first running session)
- Looks up the session's stored `origin` (SessionSource) from the session store
- Gets the appropriate platform adapter for that source's platform
- **Creates a MessageEvent with:**
  - `text`: the injected message text
  - `source`: the session's origin (SessionSource)
  - `message_id`: prefixed with `"control-"` timestamp format (`f"control-{time.time()}"`)
- Calls `adapter.handle_message(event, interrupt=interrupt)`

**Key parameters:**
- `interrupt` (keyword-only, default=True): Controls whether the message should interrupt the current agent run or queue for processing after completion

---

### 2. **`_handle_message()` - Full Dispatch Logic**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` lines 912-1630

**The complete flow:**

1. **Authorization check** (lines 927-954): Verifies user is in the allow-list; offers pairing code in DMs for unauthorized users

2. **PRIORITY: Interrupt existing agent** (lines 956-964):
   - If an agent is already running for this session, interrupt it immediately
   - This happens BEFORE command parsing to minimize latency
   - Calls `running_agent.interrupt(event.text)`

3. **Command detection and routing** (lines 966-1044):
   - Checks if message is a command via `event.get_command()` (looks for `/` prefix)
   - Emits `command:*` hooks for recognized slash commands
   - Routes to specific handlers: `/reset`, `/help`, `/status`, `/stop`, `/model`, `/personality`, `/retry`, `/undo`, `/autoreply`, etc.
   - Supports user-defined quick commands and skill slash commands

4. **Approval responses** (lines 1089-1110): Handles yes/no/cancel responses for dangerous command approvals

5. **Session management** (lines 1112-1150):
   - Gets or creates session
   - Builds session context
   - Loads conversation history
   - Detects if it's a new/auto-reset session

6. **Auto-compress on pathological history** (lines 1151-1309):
   - Detects if transcript is too large (85% of context window by default)
   - Runs compression pre-agent to prevent API failures

7. **Autoreply turn counter reset** (lines 1472-1476):
   - **CRITICAL: Checks `event.message_id` prefix to detect autoreply messages**
   - If message_id does NOT start with `"autoreply-"` AND autoreply is active, resets turn counter to 0
   - Real user messages reset the counter; autoreply-injected messages do not

8. **Agent execution** (lines 1478-1505):
   - Emits `agent:start` hook
   - Runs agent conversation
   - Handles session_id changes (from compression)

9. **Response handling** (lines 1506-1621):
   - Displays reasoning if enabled
   - Emits `agent:end` hook
   - Saves conversation to transcript
   - **If `is_autoreply` is True AND response exists:** Prepends `**[Auto-reply N/MAX]:** {event.text}\n\n---\n\n{response}`
   - Schedules next autoreply via `_process_autoreply()` if autoreply is active

---

### 3. **Message Prefix Handling: "control-" vs "autoreply-"**

**Where they differ:**

| Aspect | control- prefix | autoreply- prefix |
|--------|-----------------|-------------------|
| **Created by** | `inject_message()` method (line 317) | `_process_autoreply()` method (line 2129) |
| **Checked anywhere?** | **NO** - never explicitly checked in code | **YES** - checked at line 1474: `is_autoreply = (event.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")` |
| **Affects behavior** | Used for debugging/logging only; no conditional logic | **Critical**: Controls turn counter reset and response labeling |
| **Turn counter effect** | Resets to 0 (treated as real user message) | Does NOT reset (auto-reply messages preserve counter) |
| **Response label** | No special label | Prepended with `**[Auto-reply N/MAX]:** {user_text}---` |
| **Next autoreply scheduling** | Scheduled if autoreply config exists | Same (scheduled if config exists) |

**Critical insight:** The `"control-"` prefix is purely for identification/logging. There is **NO code that checks for or behaves differently based on the "control-" prefix**. All synthetic messages (control or autoreply) go through the same `_handle_message()` dispatch. Only autoreply messages have special behavior because they're explicitly detected.

---

### 4. **`_process_autoreply()` and `_generate_autoreply()` - Autoreply Event Injection**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` lines 2068-2133

**`_generate_autoreply()` (lines 2068-2104):**
- Checks if autoreply is configured for the session
- If literal mode: returns `config["prompt"]` directly
- If LLM mode: 
  - Loads transcript
  - Builds prompt from transcript via `build_autoreply_messages()`
  - Calls `async_call_llm()` with task="autoreply", temperature=0.7, max_tokens=1024
  - Increments `turn_count`
- Returns `(reply_text, cap_reached)` tuple
- If cap is reached, removes the config and returns `(None, True)`

**`_process_autoreply()` (lines 2106-2133):**
- Fire-and-forget async task spawned at line 1617
- Gets adapter for the source platform
- Calls `_generate_autoreply()` to get the text
- If cap reached: sends notification message via `adapter.send()`, does NOT inject
- Otherwise: **Creates synthetic MessageEvent:**
  ```python
  synthetic_event = MessageEvent(
      text=autoreply_text,
      source=source,
      message_id=f"autoreply-{time.time()}",
  )
  await adapter.handle_message(synthetic_event)
  ```
- Note: Unlike `inject_message()`, `_process_autoreply()` does NOT pass an `interrupt` parameter, so it defaults to `True`

---

### 5. **Parameters `inject_message()` Supports vs Autoreply Doesn't**

**`inject_message()` supports:**
- `interrupt` (keyword-only): Controls interrupt vs queue mode
  - `True` (default): Stops current agent, processes message immediately
  - `False`: Queues message for after current run completes

**`_process_autoreply()` does NOT support:**
- `interrupt` parameter - it always calls `adapter.handle_message(synthetic_event)` without any interrupt parameter
- This means autoreply messages **always default to interrupt=True**
- If an agent is running when autoreply fires, it will interrupt and process the autoreply message

**Control API integration:**
- The control API's `/message` endpoint (lines 129-169 in control_api.py) accepts `mode` parameter:
  - `"interrupt"` (default) → `interrupt=True`
  - `"queue"` → `interrupt=False`
- This allows external tools to choose whether injected messages interrupt or queue

---

### 6. **How `handle_message()` in base.py Processes Events**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/base.py` lines 648-676

**Signature:**
```python
async def handle_message(self, event: MessageEvent, *, interrupt: bool = True) -> None
```

**Logic:**
1. Resolves session key from event's source
2. **If session already has active handler:**
   - Stores event as pending message in `_pending_messages[session_key]`
   - If `interrupt=True`: Sets interrupt event via `self._active_sessions[session_key].set()` (triggers `⚡ interrupt` message)
   - If `interrupt=False`: Just queues without interrupt (shows `📬 Message queued` message)
   - Returns early
3. **If no active handler:** Spawns background task `_process_message_background()` to handle it

**The interrupt mechanism:**
- Each session has an `asyncio.Event` in `_active_sessions`
- When a new message arrives with `interrupt=True`, the event is set
- The running agent checks `has_pending_interrupt()` and gracefully stops
- After agent completes, pending message is picked up via `get_pending_message()`

---

### 7. **Other Synthetic MessageEvent Creation Sites**

I found **three places** where MessageEvent objects are created synthetically:

1. **`inject_message()` - line 314-318:**
   - Creates with `message_id=f"control-{time.time()}"`
   - Used by control API and external tools

2. **`_process_autoreply()` - line 2126-2130:**
   - Creates with `message_id=f"autoreply-{time.time()}"`
   - Used for autonomous reply generation

3. **`_handle_retry_command()` - line 2160-2165:**
   - Creates with no message_id (defaults to None)
   - Re-sends last user message from transcript
   - Used for `/retry` command

---

### 8. **Test Evidence**

From `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/tests/gateway/test_autoreply_command.py`:

- **Line 514-518**: Tests verify autoreply messages are detected by `message_id.startswith("autoreply-")`
- **Line 520-539**: Tests confirm turn counter reset logic: real messages reset, autoreply messages don't
- **Line 541-564**: Tests verify response labeling: autoreply responses get `**[Auto-reply N/MAX]:**` prefix
- **Line 376-403**: Tests show `_process_autoreply()` injects with `message_id.startswith("autoreply-")`

From `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/tests/gateway/test_control_api.py`:

- **Line 35-43**: Control API delegates to `runner.inject_message()` with interrupt parameter
- **Line 47-55**: Queue mode passes `interrupt=False`
- **Line 59-67**: Defaults to `interrupt=True`

---

### SUMMARY TABLE

| Feature | inject_message (control-) | _process_autoreply (autoreply-) | _handle_retry (None) |
|---------|--------------------------|----------------------------------|---------------------|
| **Entry point** | Control API or external tools | Scheduled after agent completes | /retry command |
| **Message ID prefix** | control-{timestamp} | autoreply-{timestamp} | None |
| **Detected by name check?** | NO | YES (line 1474) |  NO |
| **Interrupt parameter** | User-controlled (interrupt=) | Always interrupts (default=True) | No parameter |
| **Turn counter behavior** | Resets to 0 | Doesn't reset | Resets to 0 |
| **Response label** | None | [Auto-reply N/MAX]: ... | None |
| **Next autoreply scheduled** | Only if config exists | Only if config exists | No |

---

This analysis shows a clean separation of concerns: **control messages** are for external tool integration with explicit interrupt/queue control, while **autoreply messages** are for autonomous conversation loops with special detection and labeling based on the `message_id` prefix.

## Comprehensive Architectural Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's the complete architectural picture:

### 1. **Command Handler Patterns in `gateway/run.py`**

The codebase has 22 command handlers (all async), following a consistent naming and structure pattern:

**Pattern:**
```python
async def _handle_<command>_command(self, event: MessageEvent) -> str:
    """Handle /<command> command."""
    source = event.source
    session_entry = self.session_store.get_or_create_session(source)
    [REDACTED](source)
    # ... handler logic
    return response_message  # Markdown string sent to user
```

**Key handlers examined:**
- `/reset` and `/new` (1634-1668): Cleans up autoreply config, shuts down honcho, flushes memories async, emits hooks
- `/model` (1752-1890): Complex multi-step validation and persistence to config.yaml
- `/compress` (2557-2614): Instantiates temporary AIAgent, runs compression in executor
- `/autoreply` (2022-2066): Delegate to pure logic module (`agent.autoreply.parse_autoreply_args`), stores config in `self._autoreply_configs`

**State storage patterns:**
- Per-session configs stored in instance dicts: `self._autoreply_configs[session_key]`
- Session info retrieved via session store: `self.session_store.get_or_create_session(source)`
- Config changes persisted to `~/.hermes/config.yaml` when relevant
- Immediate feedback via return string (not async operations)

### 2. **Autoreply: The Shared Pure Logic Module Pattern**

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/agent/autoreply.py` is the gold standard for feature architecture:

**Design philosophy (stated in docstring):**
> "Handles config parsing, state management, prompt building, and LLM calls. Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops."

**Functions (all pure/stateless):**
- `parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict]]`: Parse CLI args, return action + config
- `build_autoreply_messages(config, history) -> List[Dict]`: Build LLM prompt from history
- `check_and_advance(config) -> Tuple[Optional[str], bool]`: Check turn cap, handle literal mode
- `format_status(config) -> str`: Format status display

**No LLM calls in the module** — they're delegated to the caller. This keeps the module testable and platform-agnostic.

**Integration pattern in gateway** (2022-2134):
1. Parse args with `parse_autoreply_args()` → get (action, config)
2. Store config in `self._autoreply_configs[session_key]` if enabled
3. On reset (`_handle_reset_command`), pop the config from the dict
4. On stop (`_handle_stop_command`), pop the config from the dict
5. Generate replies via async task `_generate_autoreply()` which calls `check_and_advance()` and builds messages
6. Inject synthetic message via adapter (`_process_autoreply()`)

### 3. **Control Handler Architecture (`run_agent.py` lines 2753-2823)**

This is the **system for internal/external control** (different from user command handlers):

**Registry pattern:**
```python
self._control_handlers = {
    "switch_model":    {"fn": self._ctrl_switch_model, "immediate": True},
    "compact_context": {"fn": self._ctrl_compact},
    "interrupt":       {"fn": self._ctrl_interrupt, "immediate": True},
    "get_status":      {"fn": self._ctrl_get_status, "immediate": True},
    "flush_memories":  {"fn": self._ctrl_flush_memories},
}
```

**Three-method dispatch system** (evolved through 4 refactorings on poms-refactoring):
1. **`execute_control(command, **params)`** — Single entry point
   - Routes to registry
   - If `immediate=True`: calls `_run_ctrl()` immediately in calling thread
   - If not immediate: queues to `self._control_queue` (thread-safe with lock)
   - Returns dict with `{"success": bool, "message": str}` or `{"success": bool, "queued": True}`

2. **`_run_ctrl(fn, **kwargs)`** — Thin execution wrapper (3 lines)
   - Calls handler function
   - Prints notification if handler returns non-None string
   - All handlers receive `**_` to ignore extra kwargs

3. **`_drain_control_queue(messages, system_message, task_id)`** — Process queued commands
   - Called at safe points in the agent loop (after certain iterations)
   - Builds context dict with message/system_message/task_id
   - Executes each queued command with merged context
   - Thread-safe deque clear

**Handler signature pattern:**
```python
def _ctrl_<name>(self, **_):  # or (self, messages: list = None, **_): for non-immediate
    # Do work
    return "notification string"  # or None
```

**Key design decisions:**
- **Immediate vs queued**: Immediate commands (interrupt, get_status, switch_model) execute in caller's thread and can't access loop context. Queued commands (compact_context, flush_memories) access messages/system_message.
- **Notification wrapping**: Handler return values automatically prefixed with `"⚙️ "` in `_run_ctrl()`
- **No external flag anymore**: All control handlers are exposed to the HTTP API by default (earlier refactoring removed the `external=False` distinction)

### 4. **Session Cleanup Patterns**

On `/reset` command (`_handle_reset_command`, 1634-1668):
```python
# 1. Background memory flush (non-blocking)
asyncio.create_task(self._async_flush_memories(old_entry.session_id))

# 2. Cleanup per-session state
self._shutdown_gateway_honcho(session_key)  # Stop honcho subprocess
self._autoreply_configs.pop(session_key, None)  # Remove autoreply config

# 3. Reset session in store
new_entry = self.session_store.reset_session(session_key)

# 4. Emit hook
await self.hooks.emit("session:reset", {...})
```

On `/stop` command (`_handle_stop_command`, 1695-1712):
```python
# Pop state dicts
was_autoreply = self._autoreply_configs.pop(session_key, None)

# Call agent interrupt
if session_key in self._running_agents:
    agent = self._running_agents[session_key]
    agent.interrupt()
```

On agent teardown (after run completes):
```python
# Cleanup tracking
if session_key and session_key in self._running_agents:
    del self._running_agents[session_key]
```

### 5. **Shared Logic Module Pattern**

Modules in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/agent/` follow a pattern of **pure, stateless logic**:

- **`autoreply.py`**: Config parsing, message building, turn counting
- **`context_compressor.py`**: Compression algorithm with token tracking (instantiated per session)
- **`auxiliary_client.py`**: Wrapper around cheap/fast LLM calls
- **`insights.py`**: Historical analysis from SQLite database
- **`model_metadata.py`**: Token estimation, context length lookup
- **`prompt_builder.py`**: System prompt assembly

These are **not** instantiated per session — they're pure functions or utilities. Only `context_compressor.py` maintains instance state (compression count, token tracking).

### 6. **Recent Refactoring Direction** (commits 8a7ce57 → 4ad201e)

The branch shows a clear evolution toward **architectural clarity**:

**8a7ce57** (Mar 15 15:10): Introduce generic control queue (replace `_pending_model_switch`)
- Added flexibility for external commands
- Fixed: stale `_cached_system_prompt` after compress-via-drain

**65eb67f** (Mar 15 17:18): Add interrupt/get_status/flush_memories handlers
- Established immediate vs queued semantics
- All handlers exposed to API

**3166462** (Mar 15 17:20): Rename `_handle_ctrl_*` → `_ctrl_*`, trim docstrings
- Reduced boilerplate (handlers are "thin dispatch wrappers")
- Convention documented in block comment once, not per-handler

**894f4a9** (Mar 15 17:23): Flatten control queue to 3 methods
- Merged `enqueue_control` into `execute_control`
- Replaced `_run_control_handler` with 3-line `_run_ctrl`
- Build context dict once in `_drain_control_queue` instead of threading kwargs

**05f4793** (Mar 15 18:09): Docs reviewing the refactoring trajectory
- Notes that 4 iterations happened (handler dict → external flag → immediate flag → notification → flatten)
- Final state is clean, but took multiple passes

**4ad201e** (Mar 15 17:28): Add /autoreply command
- Uses the established patterns (parse via pure module, store in instance dict, cleanup on reset)
- Async background injection via adapter

---

### 7. **Conventions Summary**

**State Storage:**
- Per-session ephemeral configs: instance dicts keyed by `session_key` (e.g., `self._autoreply_configs`)
- Persistent user config: `~/.hermes/config.yaml` (YAML, read at gateway startup)
- Session metadata: `SessionStore` (in-memory dict, keyed by session_key)
- Running agent refs: `self._running_agents[session_key]`

**Background Tasks:**
- Fire-and-forget via `asyncio.create_task()` (e.g., memory flush, autoreply injection)
- No background daemon threads — all async tasks
- Cleanup via deque + lock pattern for control queue

**Cleanup on Session Reset:**
- Pop from all per-session dicts
- Call `_shutdown_gateway_honcho()` (stops subprocess)
- Emit `session:reset` hook
- Reset session in store

**Handler Exposure:**
- User commands: async `_handle_*_command()` methods (gateway-only)
- Control commands: registered in `_control_handlers` dict with immediate/queued flags (AIAgent, exposed to HTTP API)
- Both patterns: handlers are thin dispatch → delegate to pure logic modules or direct method calls

This is the architectural foundation. All new features should follow these patterns.

Based on thorough codebase exploration, I need to evaluate whether autoreply should be unified with the control API injection path, and identify other improvements that fit the codebase conventions.

## Current Architecture

### Two separate "control" systems exist:

**1. Control API** (`gateway/control_api.py` + `gateway/run.py`):
- HTTP server on port 47823
- Calls `runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=True)` 
- `inject_message()` builds a `MessageEvent` with `message_id="control-{timestamp}"` and calls `adapter.handle_message(event, interrupt=interrupt)`
- The "control-" prefix is NEVER checked anywhere - it's purely cosmetic

**2. Control handlers** (`_control_handlers` dict in the AIAgent class):
- Registry: `{"switch_model": {"fn": ..., "immediate": True}, "compact_context": {...}, ...}`
- `execute_control(command, **params)` dispatches
- Immediate handlers run in caller thread; queued ones drain at safe points in agent loop
- Recently refactored through 4 iterations to current clean 3-method pattern

### Autoreply (`agent/autoreply.py` + integration in `gateway/run.py`):
- Shared pure logic in `agent/autoreply.py` (parse, build messages, check_and_advance, format_status)
- Gateway integration: `_handle_autoreply_command()`, `_generate_autoreply()`, `_process_autoreply()`
- `_process_autoreply()` creates its own `MessageEvent` with `message_id="autoreply-{timestamp}"` and calls `adapter.handle_message(synthetic_event)` directly
- The "autoreply-" prefix IS checked in `_handle_message()` (line 1474) to:
  - NOT reset the turn counter for autoreply messages (real user messages reset it to 0)
  - Label responses with `[Auto-reply N/MAX]`

### Key difference between inject_message and autoreply injection:
- `inject_message()` supports `interrupt` parameter; autoreply always uses default (interrupt=True)
- `inject_message()` uses "control-" prefix (never checked); autoreply uses "autoreply-" prefix (actively checked)
- Both end up calling `adapter.handle_message(event)`

## Codebase Conventions:
- Recent refactoring direction: flatten, simplify, reduce indirection
- State stored in per-session instance dicts keyed by session_key
- Background work via `asyncio.create_task()` (fire-and-forget)
- Cleanup on /reset pops from all state dicts
- Pure logic modules in `agent/` with no I/O
- Handlers are thin dispatch wrappers

## Questions to evaluate:

1. **Should autoreply use `inject_message()` instead of building its own events?**
   - Pro: Less duplication, gains interrupt/queue mode support
   - Con: `inject_message()` uses "control-" prefix, but autoreply needs "autoreply-" for behavior detection. Would need to add a prefix parameter or use a different detection mechanism.
   - Is this worth the coupling? Or is the current approach (both call adapter.handle_message independently) actually cleaner?

2. **Should autoreply detection use something other than message_id prefix?**
   - Currently fragile string prefix matching
   - Could use a field on MessageEvent, or pass context differently

3. **What other improvements are natural fits?**
   - Silent LLM failure handling
   - Missing interrupt=False for autoreply (should queue, not interrupt)
   - CLI/gateway inconsistency
   - Rate limiting safety valve

Please evaluate these options and recommend what changes would be most valuable while fitting the codebase's conventions and direction. Consider: what's the simplest change that provides the most value? Don't over-engineer.

     2→Local-only HTTP control API for the Hermes gateway.

     4→Runs alongside the gateway as a background aiohttp server on 127.0.0.1.

    63→class ControlAPI:
    64→    """Lightweight control API that holds a reference to the GatewayRunner."""
    65→
    66→    def __init__(self, gateway_runner):
    67→        self.runner = gateway_runner
    68→        self.app = web.Application()
    69→        self._setup_routes()
    70→        self._site = None
    71→
    72→    def _setup_routes(self):
    73→        self.app.router.add_get("/health", self.health)
    74→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions", self.list_sessions)
    75→        self.app.router.add_get("/sessions/{key}", self.get_session)
    76→        self.app.router.add_get("/commands", self.list_commands)
    77→        self.app.router.add_post("/sessions/{key}/message", self.send_message)
    78→
    79→    # ── Helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    80→
    81→    def _resolve_agent(self, key: str):
    82→        """Look up a running agent by session key.  '_any' returns the first.
    83→        Returns (resolved_key, agent) or (key, None) if not found."""
    84→        if key == "_any":
    85→            if not self.runner._running_agents:
    86→                return key, None
    87→            key = next(iter(self.runner._running_agents))
    88→        return key, self.runner._running_agents.get(key)
    89→

    91→
    92→    async def health(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    93→        return _json_response({"status": "ok"})
    94→
    95→    async def list_sessions(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    96→        """List all sessions, marking which have a live (running) agent."""
    97→        sessions = []
    98→        for key, agent in self.runner._running_agents.items():
    99→            entry = {
   100→                "session_key": key,
   101→                "status": "running",
   102→                "model": getattr(agent, "model", None),
   103→                "provider": getattr(agent, "provider", None),
   104→                **self.runner.get_session_info(key),
   105→            }
   106→            sessions.append(entry)
   107→        return _json_response({"sessions": sessions})

   110→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   111→        key, agent = self._resolve_agent(key)
   112→        if not agent:
   113→            return _json_response(
   114→                {"error": f"No running agent for '{key}'"},
   115→                status=404,
   116→            )
   117→        return _json_response({
   118→            "session_key": key,

   126→        """List available slash commands that can be sent via /message."""
   127→        return _json_response({"commands": AVAILABLE_COMMANDS})
   128→
   129→    async def send_message(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
   130→        """Inject a message (or /command) into a session.
   131→
   132→        POST body: {"text": "...", "mode": "interrupt"|"queue"}
   133→
   134→        Modes:
   135→        - interrupt (default): cancel current agent run, process this message
   136→        - queue: let current run finish, then process this message next
   137→        """
   138→        [REDACTED]["key"]
   139→
   140→        try:
   141→            body = await request.json()
   142→        except Exception:
   143→            return _json_response({"error": "Invalid JSON body"}, status=400)
   144→
   145→        text = body.get("text", "").strip()
   146→        if not text:
   147→            return _json_response({"error": "'text' is required"}, status=400)
   148→
   149→        mode = body.get("mode", "interrupt")
   150→        if mode not in ("interrupt", "queue"):
   151→            return _json_response(
   152→                {"error": f"Invalid mode '{mode}', must be 'interrupt' or 'queue'"},
   153→                status=400,
   154→            )
   155→
   156→        logger.info(
   157→            "Control API: send_message session=%s mode=%s text=%s",
   158→            key, mode, text[:80],
   159→        )
   160→
   161→        try:
   162→            result = self.runner.inject_message(key, text, interrupt=(mode != "queue"))
   163→            # await if coroutine (GatewayRunner is async, CLI shim is sync)
   164→            if hasattr(result, "__await__"):
   165→                await result
   166→        except LookupError as e:
   167→            return _json_response({"error": str(e)}, status=404)
   168→
   169→        return _json_response({"success": True, "message": "Message submitted"})
   170→
   171→    # ── Lifecycle ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   172→
   173→    async def start(self):
   174→        port = _get_port()
   175→        app_runner = web.AppRunner(self.app, access_log=None)
   176→        await app_runner.setup()
   177→        self._site = web.TCPSite(app_runner, "127.0.0.1", port)
   178→        try:
   179→            await self._site.start()
   180→            logger.info("Control API listening on http://127.0.0.1:%d", port)
   181→            self._write_port_file(port)
   182→        except OSError as e:
   183→            logger.warning("Control API failed to bind port %d: %s", port, e)
   184→
   185→    async def stop(self):
   186→        if self._site:
   187→            await self._site.stop()
   188→            self._remove_port_file()
   189→            logger.info("Control API stopped")
   190→
   191→    @staticmethod
   192→    def _write_port_file(port: int):
   193→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   194→        try:
   195→            with open(path, "w") as f:
   196→                f.write(str(port))
   197→        except OSError:
   198→            pass

   200→    @staticmethod
   201→    def _remove_port_file():
   202→        path = os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/control_api.port")
   203→        try:

1→"""Auto-reply engine — shared logic for CLI and gateway.
     2→
     3→Handles config parsing, state management, prompt building, and LLM calls.
     4→Consumers (CLI, gateway) only handle injection into their respective message loops.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
     8→
     9→_DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 20
    10→
    11→
    12→def parse_autoreply_args(args: str) -> Tuple[str, Optional[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    13→    """Parse /autoreply command arguments.
    14→
    15→    Returns (status_message, config_or_none).
    16→    - When enabling: returns (message, new_config_dict)
    17→    - When disabling/status/error: returns (message, None)
    18→
    19→    The caller is responsible for storing/clearing the config and
    20→    formatting the message for their platform (markdown vs plain text).
    21→    """
    22→    # /autoreply off
    23→    if args.lower() in ("off", "disable", "stop"):
    24→        return "off", None
    25→
    26→    # /autoreply max <N>
    27→    if args.lower() == "max" or (args.lower().startswith("max ") and len(args) > 4):
    28→        parts = args.split(None, 1)
    29→        if len(parts) == 2:
    30→            try:
    31→                n = int(parts[1])
    32→                if n < 1:
    33→                    return "error:Max turns must be at least 1.", None
    34→                return f"max:{n}", None
    35→            except ValueError:
    36→                return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    37→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply max <number>", None
    38→
    39→    # /autoreply (no args)
    40→    if not args:
    41→        return "status", None
    42→
    43→    # Extract flags from args: --literal, --max N, --forever
    44→    literal = False
    45→    max_turns = _DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS
    46→    remaining_parts = []
    47→
    48→    parts = args.split()
    49→    i = 0
    50→    while i < len(parts):
    51→        if parts[i] == "--literal":
    52→            literal = True
    53→        elif parts[i] == "--forever":
    54→            max_turns = 0  # 0 = unlimited
    55→        elif parts[i] == "--max" and i + 1 < len(parts):
    56→            i += 1
    57→            try:
    58→                n = int(parts[i])
    59→                if n < 1:
    60→                    return "error:--max must be at least 1.", None
    61→                max_turns = n
    62→            except ValueError:
    63→                return "error:--max requires a number.", None
    64→        else:
    65→            remaining_parts.append(parts[i])
    66→        i += 1
    67→
    68→    prompt = " ".join(remaining_parts).strip()
    69→
    70→    if literal:
    71→        if not prompt:
    72→            return "error:Usage: /autoreply --literal <message>", None
    73→        return "enabled", {
    74→            "prompt": prompt,
    75→            "model": None,
    76→            "max_turns": max_turns,
    77→            "turn_count": 0,
    78→            "literal": True,
    79→        }
    80→
    81→    if not prompt:
    82→        return "error:Usage: /autoreply <instructions>", None
    83→
    84→    return "enabled", {
    85→        "prompt": prompt,
    86→        "model": None,
    87→        "max_turns": max_turns,
    88→        "turn_count": 0,
    89→    }
    90→
    91→
    92→def build_autoreply_messages(config: Dict[str, Any],
    93→                             history: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    94→    """Build the message list for the auto-reply LLM call.
    95→
    96→    Filters history to recent user/assistant messages, prepends a system
    97→    prompt with the user's instructions, and appends the generation request.
    98→    """
    99→    recent = [
   100→        m for m in (history or [])
   101→        if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")
   102→    ][-20:]
   103→
   104→    system_prompt = (
   105→        "You are generating a reply on behalf of the user in a conversation "
   106→        "with an AI assistant.\n\n"
   107→        "THE USER'S INSTRUCTIONS — follow these as your top priority:\n"
   108→        f"{config['prompt']}\n\n"
   109→        "Output ONLY the user's reply message. No labels, no meta-commentary, "
   110→        "no 'User:' prefix."
   111→    )
   112→
   113→    messages = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt}]
   114→    for m in recent:
   115→        content = m["content"]
   116→        # Flatten list-type content to plain text
   117→        if isinstance(content, list):
   118→            content = "\n".join(
   119→                p.get("text", "") for p in content
   120→                if isinstance(p, dict) and p.get("type") == "text"
   121→            )
   122→        messages.append({"role": m["role"], "content": content})
   123→    messages.append({
   124→        "role": "user",
   125→        "content": "Based on the conversation above and your instructions, "
   126→                   "generate the next user reply.",
   127→    })
   128→    return messages
   129→
   130→
   131→def check_and_advance(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Tuple[Optional[str], bool]:
   132→    """Check turn cap and handle literal mode.
   133→
   134→    Returns (reply_text, cap_reached).
   135→    - (None, False): config is None / not applicable (caller didn't pass config)
   136→    - (None, True): turn cap reached, config should be removed
   137→    - (text, False): literal mode reply, turn_count incremented
   138→    - None for LLM mode: caller should proceed to call the LLM
   139→
   140→    For LLM mode, returns a sentinel to indicate the caller should proceed:
   141→    the return is (None, False) when turn_count < max_turns and not literal.
   142→    But we can't distinguish "no config" from "LLM needed" with just (None, False).
   143→
   144→    So the contract is: caller checks config existence first, then calls this.
   145→    If this returns (None, True) → cap reached. If it returns a string → use it.
   146→    If it returns (None, False) → proceed to LLM call.
   147→    """
   148→    if config["max_turns"] > 0 and config["turn_count"] >= config["max_turns"]:
   149→        return None, True
   150→
   151→    if config.get("literal"):
   152→        config["turn_count"] += 1
   153→        return config["prompt"], False
   154→
   155→    return None, False
   156→
   157→
   158→def format_status(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> str:
   159→    """Format the auto-reply status for display."""
   160→    mode = "literal" if config.get("literal") else "LLM-generated"
   161→    label = "Message" if config.get("literal") else "Prompt"
   162→    prompt_preview = config["prompt"][:100]
   163→    if len(config["prompt"]) > 100:
   164→        prompt_preview += "..."
   165→    return (
   166→        f"Auto-reply active ({mode})\n"
   167→        f"  {label}: {prompt_preview}\n"
   168→        f"  Turns: {config['turn_count']}/{'∞' if config['max_turns'] == 0 else config['max_turns']}"
   169→    )
   170→

  1514→                        display_reasoning += f"\n_... ({len(lines) - 15} more lines)_"
  1515→                    else:
  1516→                        display_reasoning = last_reasoning.strip()
  1517→                    response = f"💭 **Reasoning:**\n```\n{display_reasoning}\n```\n\n{response}"
  1518→
  1519→            # Emit agent:end hook
  1520→            await self.hooks.emit("agent:end", {
  1521→                **hook_ctx,
  1522→                "response": (response or "")[:500],
  1523→            })
  1524→            
  1525→            # Check for pending process watchers (check_interval on background processes)
  1526→            try:
  1527→                from tools.process_registry import process_registry
  1528→                while process_registry.pending_watchers:
  1529→                    watcher = process_registry.pending_watchers.pop(0)
  1530→                    asyncio.create_task(self._run_process_watcher(watcher))
  1531→            except Exception as e:
  1532→                logger.error("Process watcher setup error: %s", e)
  1533→
  1534→            # Check if the agent encountered a dangerous command needing approval
  1535→            try:
  1536→                from tools.approval import pop_pending
  1537→                pending = pop_pending(session_key)
  1538→                if pending:
  1539→                    self._pending_approvals[session_key] = pending
  1540→            except Exception as e:
  1541→                logger.debug("Failed to check pending approvals: %s", e)
  1542→            
  1543→            # Save the full conversation to the transcript, including tool calls.
  1544→            # This preserves the complete agent loop (tool_calls, tool results,
  1545→            # intermediate reasoning) so sessions can be resumed with full context
  1546→            # and transcripts are useful for debugging and training data.
  1547→            ts = datetime.now().isoformat()
  1548→            
  1549→            # If this is a fresh session (no history), write the full tool
  1550→            # definitions as the first entry so the transcript is self-describing
  1551→            # -- the same list of dicts sent as tools=[...] in the API request.
  1552→            if not history:
  1553→                tool_defs = agent_result.get("tools", [])
  1554→                self.session_store.append_to_transcript(
  1555→                    session_entry.session_id,
  1556→                    {
  1557→                        "role": "session_meta",
  1558→                        "tools": tool_defs or [],
  1559→                        "model": os.getenv("HERMES_MODEL", ""),
  1560→                        "platform": source.platform.value if source.platform else "",
  1561→                        "timestamp": ts,
  1562→                    }
  1563→                )
  1564→            
  1565→            # Find only the NEW messages from this turn (skip history we loaded).
  1566→            # Use the filtered history length (history_offset) that was actually
  1567→            # passed to the agent, not len(history) which includes session_meta
  1568→            # entries that were stripped before the agent saw them.
  1569→            history_len = agent_result.get("history_offset", len(history))
  1570→            new_messages = agent_messages[history_len:] if len(agent_messages) > history_len else []
  1571→            
  1572→            # If no new messages found (edge case), fall back to simple user/assistant
  1573→            if not new_messages:
  1574→                self.session_store.append_to_transcript(
  1575→                    session_entry.session_id,
  1576→                    {"role": "user", "content": message_text, "timestamp": ts}
  1577→                )
  1578→                if response:
  1579→                    self.session_store.append_to_transcript(
  1580→                        session_entry.session_id,
  1581→                        {"role": "assistant", "content": response, "timestamp": ts}
  1582→                    )
  1583→            else:
  1584→                # The agent already persisted these messages to SQLite via
  1585→                # _flush_messages_to_session_db(), so skip the DB write here
  1586→                # to prevent the duplicate-write bug (#860).  We still write
  1587→                # to JSONL for backward compatibility and as a backup.
  1588→                agent_persisted = self._session_db is not None
  1589→                for msg in new_messages:
  1590→                    # Skip system messages (they're rebuilt each run)
  1591→                    if msg.get("role") == "system":
  1592→                        continue
  1593→                    # Add timestamp to each message for debugging
  1594→                    entry = {**msg, "timestamp": ts}
  1595→                    self.session_store.append_to_transcript(
  1596→                        session_entry.session_id, entry,
  1597→                        skip_db=agent_persisted,
  1598→                    )
  1599→            
  1600→            # Update session with actual prompt token count from the agent
  1601→            self.session_store.update_session(
  1602→                session_entry.session_key,
  1603→                last_prompt_tokens=agent_result.get("last_prompt_tokens", 0),
  1604→            )
  1605→
  1606→            # Auto-reply: label the response and schedule next auto-reply
  1607→            if is_autoreply and response:
  1608→                ar_config = self._autoreply_configs.get(session_key)
  1609→                if ar_config:
  1610→                    cap = "∞" if ar_config["max_turns"] == 0 else ar_config["max_turns"]
  1611→                    response = (
  1612→                        f"**[Auto-reply {ar_config['turn_count']}/{cap}]:** "
  1613→                        f"{event.text}\n\n---\n\n{response}"
  1614→                    )
  1615→
  1616→            if session_key in self._autoreply_configs:
  1617→                asyncio.create_task(
  1618→                    self._process_autoreply(source, session_key, session_entry.session_id)
  1619→                )
  1620→
  1621→            return response
  1622→            
  1623→        except Exception:
  1624→            logger.exception("Agent error in session %s", session_key)
  1625→            return (
  1626→                "Sorry, I encountered an unexpected error. "
  1627→                "The details have been logged for debugging. "

Now I have a complete picture. Let me deliver the evaluation.

---

## Evaluation

### Question 1: Should autoreply use `inject_message()` instead of building its own events?

**Recommendation: No. Keep them separate.**

The two paths serve different purposes and the apparent duplication is only 3 lines (building a `MessageEvent` and calling `adapter.handle_message`). Unifying them would require one of:

- Adding a `prefix` parameter to `inject_message()` -- introducing coupling so that `inject_message` needs to know about autoreply semantics.
- Having `inject_message()` accept a pre-built `MessageEvent` -- at which point it's not doing anything useful, just forwarding a call.

The current code is explicit: `_process_autoreply` builds an event with `autoreply-` prefix and calls `handle_message` with default `interrupt=True`. `inject_message` builds an event with `control-` prefix and passes `interrupt` through. Each is ~5 lines of straightforward code. Merging them saves no meaningful lines and adds a parameter that callers need to understand.

The "both call `adapter.handle_message` independently" pattern is actually the clean version -- `handle_message` is the entry point, and both callers construct their own `MessageEvent` to express their different intents. This is composition, not duplication.

### Question 2: Should autoreply detection use something other than message_id prefix?

**Recommendation: Yes, but minimally. Add an `is_synthetic` or `origin` field to `MessageEvent`.**

The current string prefix detection at line 1474 of `run.py` is fragile:
```python
is_autoreply = (event.message_id or "").startswith("autoreply-")
```

But the fix should be proportional. The simplest improvement: add a single optional field to `MessageEvent`:

```python
# In MessageEvent dataclass (base.py line 272)
synthetic_origin: Optional[str] = None  # "autoreply", "control", None for real messages
```

Then detection becomes `event.synthetic_origin == "autoreply"` -- explicit, type-safe, doesn't overload message_id semantics.

This also fixes the CLI inconsistency where the CLI uses `[autoreply]` text prefix (line 4906, 4909-4910) instead of message_id prefix. Both platforms would check the same field.

**However**, note the CLI doesn't use `MessageEvent` at all -- it's a synchronous loop with `_pending_input.put()`. So the CLI would still use its own detection mechanism. The `synthetic_origin` field only helps the gateway path. Given this, the ROI is marginal. The string prefix works and is only checked in one place. I'd defer this unless you're already touching `MessageEvent` for another reason.

### Question 3: What improvements are most valuable?

Ranked by value-to-effort ratio:

#### (A) Fix: autoreply should not interrupt the current run -- HIGH VALUE, EASY

This is a real bug. Currently `_process_autoreply` calls:
```python
await adapter.handle_message(synthetic_event)  # interrupt defaults to True!
```

At line 2131 of `run.py`. Looking at `handle_message` in `base.py` (line 648-673), when `interrupt=True` and there's already an active session, it sets the interrupt event, causing the currently running agent to abort. But `_process_autoreply` fires as a background task immediately after the agent completes (line 1617-1619), so there's a race: if a new `_process_message_background` has just started for a pending real message, the autoreply would interrupt it.

More importantly, conceptually autoreply messages should queue, not interrupt. They're synthetic follow-ups that should wait their turn.

**Fix**: One-line change at line 2131:
```python
await adapter.handle_message(synthetic_event, interrupt=False)
```

#### (B) Fix: silent autoreply LLM failure leaves loop stuck -- MEDIUM VALUE, EASY

At line 2132-2133 of `run.py`:
```python
except Exception as e:
    logger.error("[AutoReply] Failed: %s", e)
```

If the auxiliary LLM call fails, the error is logged but no feedback reaches the user, and no next autoreply is scheduled. The loop silently dies. The user sees the agent just stop responding with no indication why.

**Fix**: Send a visible error to the user and optionally retry or disable:
```python
except Exception as e:
    logger.error("[AutoReply] Failed: %s", e)
    try:
        adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
        if adapter:
            metadata = {"thread_id": source.thread_id} if source.thread_id else None
            await adapter.send(
                chat_id=source.chat_id,
                content=f"_[Auto-reply error: {e}. Loop paused. Use `/autoreply` to check status.]_",
                metadata=metadata,
            )
    except Exception:
        pass
```

#### (C) Improvement: CLI/gateway autoreply detection consistency -- LOW VALUE, MEDIUM EFFORT

The CLI uses `[autoreply]` text prefix (pollutes message text, requires stripping at line 4909-4910). The gateway uses `message_id` prefix. Both work but are different mechanisms for the same concept. Unifying would require either giving the CLI a `MessageEvent`-like abstraction (over-engineering) or just accepting the divergence. **Accept the divergence** -- the CLI is synchronous with a queue, the gateway is async with events. Different shapes demand different detection.

#### (D) Safety valve: max total autoreplies -- LOW VALUE, LOW EFFORT

A global or per-session circuit breaker for runaway autoreply loops. Currently `max_turns` handles this per-config, but a `--forever` flag or bugs could cause infinite loops. A hard cap (e.g., 100 total messages per session without real user input) would be a safety net.

Worth adding only if you've seen or expect runaway loops in practice.

### Summary recommendation

**Do (A) and (B). Skip the rest.**

- (A) is a one-line bug fix that prevents a real race condition.
- (B) is a small addition that prevents silent failures, which are the hardest bugs to diagnose in production.
- (1) Unifying inject paths: not worth it, current design is fine.
- (2) MessageEvent field: defer, string prefix is checked in exactly one place.
- (C) CLI consistency: accept the divergence.
- (D) Safety valve: add later if needed.

This follows the codebase's refactoring direction: fix real bugs, keep things simple, don't add abstraction unless it solves a concrete problem.

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/base.py` - Lines 648-673: `handle_message` interrupt/queue logic that (A) relies on
